# Plan: Phase 9, dashboard completeness and flow builder

**Source:** `docs/PLAN.md` §2 (Inquiries and Platform rows), §3 (the flow, `@openverify/js`, hardening: CSP and the iframe allowlist), §6 Phase 9; ADR 0002 (tiers and honesty), ADR 0003 (web routing), ADR 0004 (Kotlin Multiplatform), and the Phase 7 and Phase 8 plans, which Phase 9 builds on. Two research notes were taken on 2026-10-29 for this plan and are committed with it under `docs/research/`:
- `phase9-behaviour.md`: the public model of inquiry templates and the flow editor (screens, components, branching, action and connector steps, modules, fields, prefill, redirects, preview and simulate), theme sets, locales and translations, analytics, and the dashboard settings (allowed domains, keys, webhooks, team, roles, audit, MFA, SSO), with the gap tables F1–F28, T1–T15, A1–A13 and S1–S25, the items earlier plans carried to Phase 9 (K1–K18), and 13 owner questions;
- `phase9-engineering.md`: the flow builder on the Phase 8 editor, dynamic friction and strict evaluators, the credential-free preview, themes and the contrast gate, translations and right-to-left layout, analytics rollups measured on the pinned Postgres image, small-count suppression, the WCAG 2.2 AA audit and its tools, the allowed-domains header, key and webhook rotation, the console's script CSP, and 14 owner questions.

The sibling notes for Phase 10 (`phase10-android-engineering.md`, `phase10-android-behaviour.md`) and Phase 11 (`phase11-hardening.md`, `phase11-connect.md`) were read for what they need from Phase 9 (C175). Each Phase 9 note ends with a fact-check: the behaviour note 24 facts confirmed and 1 corrected (the version theme has 88 properties, not "about 100"); the engineering note 32 confirmed and 2 corrected (SVG shown as an image is restricted only in some browsers; the 48-hour inquiry recompute took 2.7 s, not 1.7 s), plus an addendum that three adoptions, not one, entered the lockfile 3–4 days old (decision 62). This plan uses the corrected figures (decision 66). Downloads made during research stay out of git.

**Complexity:** Very large. Four Workflows of at most ten agents each:
- **9A, the flow (ten agents):** contracts and plumbing for flow-spec v2, template versions, the flow API and theme sets; then, in parallel, the pure flow-spec logic (v2 schemas' rules, migration, graph checks, the client view and the v1 projection) and the built-in translations; then the server flow engine, the flow actions with their decision records and provisional signals, the template API, the web renderer, and the flow server with embedding and preview, each after its named Stage 2 dependency; then integration.
- **9B, the platform (ten agents):** contracts and plumbing for analytics, exports, saved views, access, keys and webhooks and the carried API items; then the pure analytics logic; then the theme API with the flow's theme application, analytics, exports, access, keys and webhooks, and the case and report completions; then integration.
- **9C, the console (eight agents):** the flow builder on the shared editor, the template forms, the preview and simulate, themes and copy, analytics and exports, settings with the console CSP, and the case and report completions; then integration.
- **9D, audit, review and fixes (nine agents):** the WCAG 2.2 AA audit; a review of the whole phase in four lenses; a verifier; three path-owned fixers.

**Why this split.** The flow is the centre of Phase 9 and everything else reads it: analytics counts the steps the server records, the console edits and previews the format, the audit walks every step. 9A therefore lands the format, the server engine and the renderer first, freezing their contracts in its own Stage 1a, as Phase 8's 8A did for the engine. The platform work of 9B (themes, analytics, exports, roles, keys, webhooks) is API work that needs 9A's step records but not its console; the theme builder moved there from 9A in review (F04), on plumbing 9A freezes, so 9A's slot could go to the flow actions. The console of 9C needs every API document of 9A and 9B. The audit and the review of 9D need everything built. Each Workflow is pushed once, after a full local validation (Rules).

**Stage 1b carries plumbing only**, as in Phases 6–8: migrations, repository and route stubs with frozen schemas, masking lists, shared hooks, queues and job stubs, configuration. Every algorithm stays with its owner behind a declared interface. Stage 2 starts as soon as the Stage 1a commits it needs land, beside Stage 1b, and each Stage 3 agent waits for its family's Stage 1b checkpoint and its named Stage 2 dependency (Phase 8 F07). Each checkpoint is committed with every suite green, so a pause resumes from the last one.

**Phase 7 and Phase 8 dependency.** Phase 9 starts only once `docs/PROGRESS.md` records Phase 8 done; until then the Phase 7 and Phase 8 plans are the interfaces it relies on, and each is named where it is used. In short:
- **Phase 8, the editor and the dialect:** the `@xyflow/react` editor with its outline view, undo, badges and problem mapping (C141), which the flow builder joins as a second document type (C155); the JSONLogic dialect with its typing, limits and `on-null` rules (C114) and the typed variable scope (C115), which the server evaluates flow routes with (C150); `z.toJSONSchema()` scope publishing for the picker (C115.3);
- **Phase 8, runs, decisions and cases:** the run service, run outputs and `workflow-run.completed`/`.errored` (C119.10, C120), which embedded workflow steps start and wait on (C152); the decision record, its basis and contest route (C128), which adverse flow paths use (C150.7); the test runs (C120.7), which the workflow canvas overlay draws (C156.6); case templates, versions, the case page and `user_notifications` (C130, C134.4, C142), which the layout editor, checklists and alerts extend (C169, C172); the export machinery of C138, which generic exports mirror (C166); the vision rendition endpoint (C135.3), which theme logos go through (C160.6);
- **Phase 7:** signals with their tiers, nullable values and `ON_NULL_DEFAULTS` (C90), which flow branches read (C150.2, C150.3); list screenings (C99), read where Phase 7 screens before completion; the parsed device class (C91); the formula-safe CSV writer (C107, `packages/checks/src/csv/`), which exports and translations use; the flow's `preview.headers` (C107.9), which the flow server replaces in the app profile (C157); `organization-display-name` (C107.7), shown when a logo is decorative (C160.6); `settings:privacy` (C90.8).

Each Stage 1a re-verifies every fact under "Starting point" and every Phase 7 and Phase 8 interface it uses against the code it finds, and records drift as a "Workflow 9A (or 9B, 9C) Stage 1a amendment".

**Review of this plan.** Three adversarial reviews (privacy, security and law; honesty and product coverage; feasibility and fit with the code) read the first draft on 2026-10-29 and raised 96 findings (4 CRITICAL, 20 HIGH, 50 MEDIUM, 22 LOW). `docs/plans/phase-9-review-resolution.md` disposes of each, the "Review" subsection of the decisions records the counts and the main changes, and decisions 67–73 are new owner items from the round. A verification round checked the resolutions, as for Phases 6–8: 87 resolved, 9 partly resolved and 17 new defects (V01–V17), each applied to this plan and recorded in the resolution's "Verification round" section.

## Summary

OpenVerify's inquiry templates are versioned and published through the API today, but the flow inside them is a list of fixed screens edited as JSON, its conditions are evaluated by the clients over `{ inquiry: { status } }` only, and the server requires every verification step of the flow whatever the path. Nothing themes the flow, it speaks English and Spanish, nothing frames it from another site, the console has no analytics, no exports and no script CSP, and the WCAG 2.2 AA audit has never been run. Phase 9 adds them, each at its tier (ADR 0002, extended to flows by ADR 0008):
- **R** (real logic): the flow-spec v2 format and its migration from v1; the server flow engine that decides every next step; template fields, copy and translations; theme sets with a WCAG contrast gate; five built-in locales with right-to-left Arabic; analytics rollups with small-count suppression; exports; environment-scoped roles; key, webhook, team and MFA settings; the console's nonce CSP; the flow server's per-request `frame-ancestors`; the embed client.
- **H** (heuristic): no new heuristic. A flow branch may read provisional snapshots of Phase 7's H-tier signals (C150.9) and heuristic checks; such a branch inherits their error rate, is shown `beta` in the editor, needs `heuristic:` whichever way it routes, and a path that fails a person on one also needs an acknowledgement and, in production, a declared basis (C150.7).
- **A** (an adapter with a deterministic sandbox provider): none new. Address autocomplete and machine translation are deferred (C177), because no permissive provider exists and a sandbox-only adapter would add nothing in production.

What lands:
- **The flow** (C147–C152): flow-spec v2, a graph of steps and port-labelled edges like Phase 8's definitions, with `screen` steps holding components (C148), `branch` steps with ordered routes and a catch-all, closed-list `action` steps (run a workflow and wait, copy extractions, update account fields, tag, flag for review), connectors to any screen, and an `exhausted` port on verification steps for fallbacks; typed template fields with immutable keys and the public 1,000 cap (C149); a typed flow scope over the inquiry, earlier verifications, the account, the device class, Phase 7 signals and lists, and embedded workflow outputs, evaluated **on the server** with the Phase 8 dialect, where an unknown path is a publish error and a nullable fact needs an explicit `on-null` (C150); the server flow engine that records each step, decides the next one after every submission, requires only the verifications on the path taken, and serves clients the step to render, with a v1 projection that keeps today's Android SDK working (C151).
- **Templates** (C153–C154): structured forms for every template member generated from the zod contracts (verification, report, signal and list configs; settings with the public expiry windows); publish notes and descriptions; drafts that save with problems; `If-Match` with 412; validation, diff of any two versions, restore, copy, "used by", promote between environments, and a read-only deployment history with the public `deployment.completed` event.
- **The builder, preview and embedding** (C155–C159): the flow as a second document type in the Phase 8 editor, outline first; a credential-free preview fed by `postMessage` into a flow document that makes no network call; simulation of verification and check outcomes on the server; the Phase 8 canvas overlay of test runs; a small flow server that sends `frame-ancestors` per request (hosted `'none'`, preview the console origin, embed the environment's allowed origins narrowed per template, fail closed); redirects carrying only `inquiry-id`, plus `status` when the version opts in (C158.5); a minimal `@openverify/js` embed client with origin-checked messages.
- **Themes and languages** (C160–C162; the theme builder in 9B): environment-scoped `theme-sets` (`theset_`) with light and dark hex palettes, a closed token set, uploaded raster logos and operator fonts, the contrast gate in both variants, and live, audited edits; `en`, `es`, `fr`, `de` and `ar` with `dir`, bidi isolation, mirrored directional icons, Arabic-Indic digit normalisation, plural completeness and pseudo-locale tests; operator copy as plain text on declared keys, the public translations sub-resource with CSV import and export, and every built-in translation marked machine-drafted until a fluent person reviews it.
- **Analytics** (C163–C166): the server's step records; daily rollups in each environment's reporting time zone; the overview, the per-screen funnel by version, verification pass rates by capture method, check failure distribution with tiers, case and signal analytics; suppression of counts from 1 to 10 with complementary suppression; a read-only aggregates API; saved views; asynchronous CSV exports with per-type permissions; CSV bulk report creation.
- **Settings** (C167–C170): roles granted per environment with a role editor that never offers what the editor cannot grant; invitations resent and users reactivated; `require-mfa` on for new organisations; console MFA reset, "my sessions" and MFA enrolment revoking other sessions; the audit log explorer; key rotation in one call, key age and attribute blocklists; webhook names, attempt headers, failure alerts, delivery search and notification preferences; the console's enforced nonce CSP.
- **Accessibility** (C171): the WCAG 2.2 AA audit on the WCAG-EM method, automated with `@axe-core/playwright` under rule 4's tooling exception and our own tests for the criteria axe cannot check, with a dated accessibility statement that claims only what the evidence shows.
- **Carried items** (C172–C173): the case layout editor, checklists, the status-switcher rule, preset comments and action placements; the reports and verification console completions; browser print views; the trust-store flags.

ADR 0008, "Flows: server-decided branching, embedding and preview origins", extends ADR 0002 to flows:
- the server decides every step a person sees and every verification they must pass; a client renders the step it is given and never chooses a path, except through the facts it reports itself or the person enters, which are marked by source (C150.10);
- a condition over a missing or unknown fact never takes a branch silently: an unknown path is refused at publish, and a nullable fact is routed by an `on-null` the author chose;
- a flow never executes operator code or reaches the network; code and HTTPS calls stay in Phase 8 workflows behind their sandbox and guard;
- who may frame which document is decided by response headers per request, failing closed, and every cross-origin message names its exact origin;
- a heuristic read by a branch keeps its tier and stage, and a path that fails a person on anything but a verification's own outcome (its status and attempts) is acknowledged and, in production, carries a basis and a contest route.

## Starting point (interim, from the research notes; re-taken at the Phase 9 base)

The research notes and this plan read the code at `91c83e02`–`6c299151` (Phase 6 being closed, Phases 7 and 8 not built); each "What exists today" table in the notes carries its sources. The full inventory at the Phase 9 base (Phase 8 done) is `contracts-9a`'s first deliverable, written to `docs/research/phase9-inventory.md`, and every claim below is re-checked there. In short:

| Area | Exists (at the research commits) | Does not exist |
|---|---|---|
| Flow definition | `@openverify/flow-spec` (`packages/flow-spec/src/flow.ts`): `flowSchema` with `metadata.specVersion: z.literal(1)`, 15 step types in a discriminated union, `transitions {from, to, condition?}` with an opaque JSONLogic condition; steps run in list order and the first transition whose condition holds overrides the next; `superRefine` checks ids and terminals; `safeParseFlow` reports dotted paths (`transitions.2.to`); every visible string is an i18n key; JSON Schema generated from zod | No screen or component model (the `form` step has 6 field types and no screen in either client); no layout, transition ids, copy, theme or locale list; no migration; one version only |
| Where conditions run | On the clients: `apps/flow/src/flow/flow-position.ts` and `json-logic.ts` (web, `readVar` returns its `null` fallback for a missing path, `json-logic.ts:44-45`), `android/.../flow/FlowNavigator.kt` and `JsonLogic.kt`, over `flowConditionContext` = `{ inquiry: { status } }` (`packages/contracts/src/flow-api.ts:110-117`); an unknown operator throws, a missing path does not | No server evaluation: `apps/api/src/services/flow.ts` creates a verification for any step type the flow has, in any order (`createFlowVerification`, lines 284–366), and `requiredVerifications` requires every verification step of the flow (`apps/api/src/jobs/inquiry-outcome.ts:61`) |
| Templates and versions | C11: `itmpl` (`active`, `archived`) and `itmplv` (`draft`, `published`, `superseded`), one published, inquiries pin their version; members `flow`, `verification-config` (a complete check map per API type, options `allowed-countries`, `allowed-id-classes`, `allowed-chains`, `accept-review-without-provider`), `settings` (`inquiry-expiration-seconds`, `session-expiration-seconds`, `one-time-link-expiration-seconds`, `retention-days`), `report-config` (Phase 6); `PATCH` drafts only and last-write-wins, `POST …/publish` with `inquiry-templates:publish`; publish runs `checkFlowMatchesConfig` (one step per verification type), `checkFlowPublishable`, `templatePublishProblems` and the async publish hooks in `services/publish/`, each refusal a 422 with JSON Pointers; `SUPPORTED_FLOW_STEP_TYPES` excludes `form` | No `If-Match`, ETag or 412 anywhere in `apps/api/src`; no publish note, description, last updater, typed fields, copy, theme, locales, diff, validate, restore, copy, promote or deployment history; no template events |
| Flow API | C3 `GET /flow/session` answers `{ inquiry {id, status}, flow, verifications, consent, environment {kind}, face-template-retention-days }`; consent records `text-keys` and `locale`; one-time links redeemed with a generic 401; credentials only in the URL fragment (`packages/contracts/src/flow-url.ts`) | No position, step, theme, copy, locales or embed members; no client capability header; no screen submission; no redirect |
| Web flow | Vite 8, React 19.3, React Router declarative mode, one hosted page (ADR 0003); i18next 26.5.2 and react-i18next 17.1.0 with bundled `en` and `es` typed catalogs (`apps/flow/src/i18n/languages.ts:1`); `?lang=` then `navigator.languages`; `lang` and `dir` synchronised; logical spacing utilities; a build-time CSP `<meta>` (`src/security/security-meta.ts:19-33`) whose comment says `frame-ancestors`, Permissions-Policy and Referrer-Policy need a serving layer; compose serves the build with `vite preview` (`infra/docker-compose.yml:438-453`), which Phase 7 C107.9 gives fixed `preview.headers` with `frame-ancestors 'none'` | No theming, no right-to-left locale, no embedded or inline mode, no `postMessage`, no server that computes headers per request |
| Android SDK | KMP, decodes with `ignoreUnknownKeys = true` (`model/OpenVerifyJson.kt:11`), `specVersion: Int` (`model/Flow.kt:36`), unknown steps as `UnsupportedStep` (`Flow.kt:449`); `FlowNavigator.next` evaluates transitions itself; strings in `composeResources` (`values`, `values-es`); every `contracts/fixtures/{api,flows}` file must be decoded by the SDK or listed as server-only (`ContractFixturesTest.kt:87-100`); unpublished (0.1.0) | No v2 step model, theme or copy decoding, `fr`, `de`, `ar` or RTL review (Phase 10) |
| Console | Next.js 16.4 App Router as a backend for frontend; server components and actions; `proxy.ts` optimistic session check; headers `frame-ancestors 'none'`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, `nosniff`, a denying Permissions-Policy, and **no script CSP** (`apps/dashboard/next.config.ts:3-14`, "left for a later phase"); English only; `DataTable`, `Dialog`, `JsonEditor` (JSON Pointer errors), `FormField`, `OneTimeSecretDialog`; templates edited as JSON (`app/templates/[id]/versions/[versionId]`); report templates edited as JSON (Phase 6 C86); Phase 7 adds Cytoscape with a table view, Phase 8 the `@xyflow/react` editor (C141) | No flow builder, theme or translation editor, analytics, saved views, exports, notification preferences or role editor beyond the permission list |
| Design tokens | `packages/ui/src/styles.css`: semantic tokens as `oklch()` in Tailwind 4 `@theme static` (`primary`, `on-primary`, `danger`, `surface`, `canvas`, `ink`, `ink-muted`, `line`, `control`, `focus`), contrast noted in a comment; `color-scheme: light`; the system font stack | No dark mode; no computed contrast check |
| Permissions and roles | 28 API scopes and 8 operator permissions (`packages/core/src/permissions.ts`); built-in `admin`, `developer`, `reviewer`, `viewer`; custom roles; a user's permissions are the union of its roles, organisation-wide (`user_roles` keyed `(user_id, role_id)`, `packages/db/src/tables/users.ts:168`); unknown values grant nothing; `missingPermissions` stops escalation; Phase 7 adds `settings:privacy` and `graph:read`, Phase 8 the workflow and case scopes | No environment-scoped grant; no theme, analytics, export or deployment scope |
| Team and MFA | Invitations (`ovi_`, 7 days, single use, `packages/contracts/src/tokens.ts:36`), deactivation; TOTP, WebAuthn and 10 recovery codes; organisation `require-mfa` defaults to `false` (`dashboard-api.ts:191`, `tenancy.ts:30`); `user:reset-mfa` CLI with a system audit entry; console sessions of 12 hours; the audit log stores the operator's IP encrypted (`audit_logs.ip`, `tables/audit-logs.ts:40`) and filters by action, actor, resource type and id | No resend or reactivation, no console MFA reset, no "my sessions", no date or IP filters, no SSO |
| API keys | `ov_sbx_`/`ov_prod_` plus 43 base64url characters, shown once, `token-prefix` afterwards; scopes, `allowed-ips` (≤ 50), `api-version`, `expires-at`, `last-used-at`, `pii-masked`; `clone` and `expire` (≤ 30 days) (`packages/contracts/src/api-keys.ts`) | No one-call rotation, age warning or attribute blocklist |
| Webhooks | `enabled`/`disabled`/`archived`; `enabled-events`, `filters` (templates, account types), `api-version`, `custom-headers`, `attribute-blocklist`; 8 attempts at +0, 1 min, 5 min, 30 min, 2 h, 6 h, 12 h, 24 h with ±10% jitter; `rotate-secret` with 0–604,800 s of overlap (default 86,400) and one `v1` per live secret; simulate and replay; a delivery list (`packages/contracts/src/webhooks.ts:41-49`) | No `name` or `description`, attempt headers, failure alerts or delivery search |
| Analytics data | `inquiries` timestamps (`created_at`, `started_at`, `completed_at`, `failed_at`, `expired_at`, `decisioned_at`, `redacted_at`) with environment indexes; `verification_checks` one row per check with `status`, `requirement`, `reasons[]`, `stage`; Phase 7's CSV writer | No step record (the server never sees which screen a user is on), no rollup, no chart library |
| Settings and configuration | C16 environment settings in `environments.settings` jsonb with retention windows and the OTP budget, served by `GET`/`PATCH /dashboard/settings`; `OPENVERIFY_FLOW_PUBLIC_URL`, `OPENVERIFY_FLOW_CORS_ORIGINS` (install-wide), `OPENVERIFY_DASHBOARD_PUBLIC_URL` (`apps/api/src/config.ts:376-377`, `:871`) | No allowed embed origins, reporting time zone, suppression threshold or default theme |
| Packages and records | No `packages/sdk-js`, `packages/sdk-react` or `apps/demo-web`, though PLAN §3 lists them; ADRs 0001–0005 (Phase 7 writes 0006, Phase 8 0007); migrations up to `0021_watchlist_check_backoff.sql` at the research commit; i18next 26.5.2 and react-i18next 17.1.0 were added in `86fa58f5` and eslint 10.13.0 in `28fce4eb` on 2026-10-20, 3–4 days after their releases; `pnpm-workspace.yaml` sets no `minimumReleaseAge` | No Phase 9 prefix, event, scope, table or setting |

Records Phase 9 corrects are listed in the behaviour note's "Corrections" and handled in C176. Items earlier plans carried to Phase 9 are K1–K18 of the behaviour note, plus two this plan's own search found (`docs/plans/phase-6.plan.md:1825-1826`, `docs/PROGRESS.md:73`): **K19**, the console's "Other lists" e2e check on a synthetic OFAC Consolidated entry, and **K20**, the reports index and history cells that read "Complete" without Phase 6 decision 6's sentence. The engineering note adds four console items it found carried: the axe-core check open since Workflow 5A, the 2.2.1 capture-time warning open since Phase 4, the flow's response headers open since Phase 1, and the editor's `ariaLabelConfig` strings (Phase 8 C141.3). Each is placed in C172–C177 (decision 61).

## Patterns to mirror

| Category | Source | Pattern |
|---|---|---|
| Versioned format with migrations | `packages/flow-spec/src/flow.ts`, Phase 8 C113.7 (`readDefinition`) | Strict zod objects; stored rows never rewritten; pure upgrade functions with golden fixtures per version; canonical hash without `layout` |
| Graph definitions and their checks | Phase 8 C113 (`packages/workflow-engine/src/graph/`) | Nodes with stable ids, edges with ports, a pure graph check after the schema, problems as `{pointer, code, detail}` with RFC 6901 pointers, warnings that never block |
| Conditions and typed scopes | Phase 8 C114, C115 (`packages/workflow-engine/src/expressions/`, `variables/`) | The JSONLogic dialect with budgets; every `var` typed against the scope at publish; nullable paths need `on-null`; `z.toJSONSchema()` of the scope feeds the picker |
| Resource contracts | `packages/contracts/src/inquiry-templates.ts`, `reports.ts`, `json-api.ts`, `list-filters.ts`, `contracts/fixtures/api/` | Zod attributes with `.meta({ pii: true })` and `PII_<RESOURCE>_ATTRIBUTES`; builders that validate output; golden fixtures; `pii-collisions.test.ts`; the Android fixture list |
| Versioned templates | `packages/db/src/tables/inquiry-templates.ts`, `services/inquiry-templates.ts`, `services/publish/` | Draft, published, superseded; one published; publish under the template lock with async publish hooks; each inquiry pins its version |
| Flow API | `apps/api/src/services/flow.ts`, `routes/flow/`, `auth/flow-session.ts` | Always within the session's inquiry; the inquiry row lock for every change; events staged in the same transaction; no fraud detail in responses |
| Promote and environments | Phase 8 C112.2 | Copy a published version as a draft into another environment of the organisation; re-resolve references by name or key; user only; never replace a draft |
| Exports | Phase 8 C138, Phase 7 CSV writer (`packages/checks/src/csv/`) | Asynchronous job, sealed file for 24 hours, creator-only, permissions re-checked on download, audit of the filter's shape only, formula neutralising |
| Console | `apps/dashboard/src/lib/api/page.tsx`, `components/data-table.tsx`, `json-editor.tsx`, Phase 8 C141 (`components/workflows/`), Phase 7 explorer | `renderPage`, server actions validated by contract schemas, masked values, an outline or table view beside every canvas or chart, client-only rendering of libraries that set inline styles |
| Flow and i18n | `apps/flow/src/i18n/` (`create-i18n.ts`, `languages.ts`, `locales/`), `steps/*/locales/`, `translate-key.ts` | Typed catalogs per locale and per step folder; `translateKey` returns `null` for an unknown key; `fillPlaceholders`; React text only |
| Security headers | `apps/dashboard/next.config.ts`, `apps/dashboard/server.ts`, `apps/flow/src/security/security-meta.ts` | One builder per policy with a unit test; a custom Node server for what the framework cannot do; infra tests on the running container |
| Settings | `packages/contracts/src/dashboard-api.ts`, `routes/dashboard/settings.ts`, Phase 7 C107.7, Phase 8 C143.7 | Kebab-case keys in `environments.settings`; defaults served when absent; every change audit-logged; sensitive keys under `settings:privacy` |
| Masking, redaction, retention | `apps/api/src/auth/pii.ts`, `services/api-logs.ts`, `packages/db/src/sealed.ts`, `repositories/redaction.ts`, the C16 pass | By attribute name at every exit with a test each; row-bound sealing; redaction and rewrap coverage lists |
| Tests | Vitest colocated; Testcontainers; Playwright (`apps/flow/e2e` mocked, `e2e/` full stack) | RED commit first; ≥ 80% per package; `docs/tdd/phase9-<agent>.tdd.md` |

## Locked contracts

C1–C146 stay locked except where a section below amends them. Stated amendments of earlier contracts:
- C3 (the flow API): `GET /flow/session` gains `position`, `step`, `values`, `theme`, `copy`, `locales`, `embed`, `completion` and `spec-version`, and `flow` becomes the v1 projection under `flowProjectionSchema` (C151.5, C151.6); the `OpenVerify-Flow-Spec` header (C151.7); screen submission and theme assets (C148.5, C160.6); verification creation, consent and uploads only for the current step (C151.4); the upload's `capture-method` field (C153.2); the consent body's digests, copy source and catalog version (C162.9); the selfie-capture extension route (C171.4);
- C5: `inquiry.completed`'s `meta.flow-outcome` (C151.13);
- C7, C30.5, C50.5 (the inquiry outcome): the required verifications are those on the path taken, with the fallback and review rules of C150.6 and C151.3;
- C10 (inquiries): `previous-step-name`, `next-step-name`, `flow-outcome`, `theme-set-id`, `redirect-uri`, the public expiry `meta` keys and sealed initial fields (C149.4, C151.8, C151.10, C151.13, C158.4, C160.7);
- C11 (template versions): flow-spec v2, `field-schemas`, `copy`, `enabled-locales`, `default-locale`, `theme-set-id`, `embed-origins`, `redirect-uri`, `publish-note`, `description`, `last-updater`, `validation`, ETag and `If-Match`, drafts saved with problems, new settings, and the routes of C154.4;
- C12: the rate-limit policies of C174.4;
- C14 (webhooks): `name`, `description`, attempt headers, failure alerts, delivery search (C165.4, C169.5, C169.6); strict filters for the new subjects (C154.8);
- C15 (roles): the new scopes and environment-scoped assignments (C167); C16: the settings of C174.6;
- C53.7 and C15: new PII members on screen submissions, bulk report files and simulation presets, joined to the API-log sets (C174.5);
- C54.1: the repeatable jobs and queues of C174.3;
- the inquiry-comparison checks (`id_inquiry_comparison`, `database_inquiry_comparison`): they compare with a field's initial value when one exists (C149.4);
- Phase 7 C107.9: the compose `app` profile serves the flow with the flow server (C157.3); Phase 7 C90.1 and C90.5: the registry's `flow-provisional` flag and the provisional snapshots a branch reads, the inquiry's own `signals` still computed at completion (C150.9);
- Phase 8 C112.4 and C120.1: a ninth trigger type `trigger-flow` and the run creator `inquiry-flow` (C152.2); C128.1, C128.3 and C128.7: decision records, bases and contest routes for adverse flow paths (C150.7); C130 and C140.1: case template version members and case-action placements, with `visible-when` enforced in `run-action` (C172); C134.4: the notification kind `webhook-failure` and a polymorphic notification subject (C169.7); C114 and C115: a typing and evaluation entry point over a caller-supplied scope (C150.12); C141: the editor becomes shared (C155.1).

### C147. Flow-spec v2: format, versions and migration

1. **Versions** (ours; the public product does not publish its serialisation, behaviour note 1.11). `@openverify/flow-spec` exports `FLOW_SPEC_VERSIONS = [1, 2]`, one strict schema per version (`flowV1Schema`, which is today's `flowSchema` unchanged, and `flowV2Schema`), `parseAnyFlow(input)` dispatching on `metadata.specVersion`, and `migrateFlow(input): FlowV2`, which applies pure upgrade functions in order. Stored template versions are **never rewritten**: the API migrates on read, every new draft is v2, and a v1 draft opened by the builder is migrated and saved as v2 with a notice. `schema:export` writes `schema/flow.v1.schema.json` and `schema/flow.v2.schema.json`, so third-party tools can validate old documents.
2. **Shape** (decision 4; strict zod objects; member names stay camelCase, as in v1, so every v1 step schema is reused unchanged; the example is abridged, without the branch's `route:minor` and `else` edges):

   ```json
   {
     "metadata": { "specVersion": 2, "id": "kyc-standard", "version": 3, "name": "KYC standard" },
     "entry": "consent",
     "steps": [
       { "id": "consent", "type": "consent", "textKeys": ["consent.biometric.body"], "required": true },
       { "id": "id_card", "type": "government_id", "sides": "both", "maxAttempts": 3, "captureMethods": ["camera"] },
       { "id": "age_route", "type": "branch", "routes": [ { "key": "minor", "label": "Under 18", "condition": { "<": [ { "var": "verifications.id_card.age-years" }, 18 ] }, "onNull": "else" } ] },
       { "id": "done", "type": "completion" }
     ],
     "edges": [
       { "id": "e1", "from": "consent", "port": "next", "to": "id_card" },
       { "id": "e2", "from": "id_card", "port": "next", "to": "age_route" }
     ],
     "layout": { "consent": { "x": 0, "y": 0 } }
   }
   ```

   Step ids keep v1's pattern (`^[a-z][a-z0-9_-]{0,63}$`), are generated by the builder, stay stable across versions (they key the diff and the step record) and read as names in scope paths (`verifications.id_card.status`). Edge ids use the same pattern and are unique. Steps run only along edges: list order means nothing in v2 (it is the outline's display order).
3. **Step types:** the fourteen supported v1 types (`consent`, `government_id`, `selfie`, `document`, `phone_otp`, `email_otp`, `database`, `aamva`, `nfc`, `crypto_wallet`, `database_business`, `business_website`, `completion`, `failure`) with their v1 members, plus additive optional members (`captureMethodsByDevice`, C153.2; `onFallback`, C150.6; `prefillFrom`, C148.7; `decisionBasis`, `contestRoute`, `biometricBasis` on `failure`, C150.7); Phase 7's `device_handoff`; and three new types, `screen` (C148), `branch` (C150.1) and `action` (C152). `form` is not a v2 type: migration turns it into a `screen` (10).
4. **Ports:** `next` (every non-terminal step except `branch`); `exhausted` (verification steps, optional; C150.6); `button:<key>` (a `screen`'s extra submit buttons, C148.1); `route:<key>` and `else` (`branch`); `error` (`action`, required, C152.1). Each output port takes exactly one edge; any edge may target any step (the public connector step is simply an edge, behaviour note 1.3), backwards included, within rule 6; `completion` and `failure` have no ports.
5. **Caps** (ours, provisional; decision 23; behaviour note F28; the public plan caps of 3 routes and 20 steps are not ours): 200 steps, 400 edges, 20 routes per branch, 40 components per screen and nesting depth 3 (C148.6), 1,000 template fields (C149.2), a step's serialised config ≤ 16 KiB, a whole flow ≤ 512 KiB, names ≤ 200 characters.
6. **Graph rules** (decisions 7 and 8; pure, `packages/flow-spec/src/v2/graph.ts`, run after the schema on save, validate, publish and promote; problems `{pointer, code, detail, severity}` with RFC 6901 pointers):
   - `entry` exists; every edge endpoint exists; every port is valid for its step's type and wired where required (`next` on screens, consent, handoff and verification steps; `else` on branches; `error` on actions);
   - every step is reachable from `entry`, and every step reaches a `completion` or `failure` step;
   - a cycle is allowed only when every step on it is a `screen` or a `branch` (ours: "go back and correct the form"); a cycle through a verification, consent, handoff or action step is refused (`cycle_not_allowed`), because a verification is entered once per inquiry (C151.2's visit caps bound the rest);
   - at most one step per verification type, as C11 has it (ours; several steps of one type on exclusive branches are deferred, C177);
   - the existing order rules hold on **every path**: `nfc` after a `selfie`, and after a `government_id` when the flow has one; an `aamva` step with `source: government_id` after a `government_id` (each "after" meaning dominated on every path from `entry`);
   - **new publish rule** (reviews P27, F23): a consent step dominates every step that uploads data; every `screen` with a form component bound to a `pii: true` field counts as such a step, and for those screens a dominating screen that shows a `legal: notice` text (C162.2) satisfies the rule in place of consent. Today this is a run-time precondition only (409 "No consent recorded", `services/flow-preconditions.ts`); as a publish rule it refuses a migrated v1 draft that puts a verification before consent, which already fails at run time today, and the migration note says so; a golden invalid flow per case;
   - warnings, never blocking: a verification step whose required checks are all `beta`; a path from `entry` to a `completion` step through no verification step (in production it also needs C151.13's `unverified-path:` acknowledgement; review H09); two steps collecting the same value, whether through a field binding or as a verification input (`phone_otp`, `email_otp`, `database`, `aamva` with `source: form`) without `prefillFrom` (3.3.7; review H22); a session or inquiry window below 20 hours (2.2.1, C171.4).
7. **Hash:** `flow-sha256` is the SHA-256 of the RFC 8785 canonical JSON of everything except `layout` (Phase 8 C113.7).
8. **Pointers everywhere:** `safeParseFlow` for both versions returns `issues[] {pointer, path, message}`, where `pointer` is RFC 6901 (`/edges/2/to`) and the dotted `path` stays one more phase for existing callers; every API problem and the console use `pointer`.
9. **Never code:** a flow document is data walked by the server engine; no member reaches `eval`, `Function`, a template compiler or a module loader (Phase 8 C113.8).
10. **v1 to v2** (lossless, golden-tested on every v1 fixture in the repository, `contracts/fixtures/flows/` and the flow-spec test fixtures): each v1 step keeps its id; for each non-terminal step S, the conditional transitions listed **before** S's first unconditional transition become a `branch` step `<S>-routes` (a numeric suffix when taken; an id that would pass 64 characters is shortened with a hash suffix) whose routes are those transitions in order, with `{"var": "inquiry.status"}` kept as the scope path `inquiry.status` and `onNull: "else"` (the path is never null), and whose `else` is that first unconditional transition or, failing one, the next step in list order; transitions after the first unconditional one are dropped, since v1 never reaches them, and each drop is a migration note (review F07: v1 takes the first transition whose condition is absent or true); a step with only an unconditional transition or none gets a `next` edge to its target or the next step, and a last step that is not terminal gets a `next` edge to a synthetic `completion` step (no `messageKey`), as v1 ends there with completion; **conditions keep v1's semantics**: a `var` path outside v1's context (`inquiry.status`) becomes the literal `null`, v1's `readVar` fallback, so a migrated flow never meets `scope_path_missing` (C150.4), and `==` or `!=` whose operands are not both strings or `null` (where Phase 8's strict alias could differ from v1's loose comparison) is a migration note covered by the equivalence test (C151.3); a v1 `form` step becomes a `screen` with the matching form components (`text` → `text_input`, `date`, `email`, `phone`, `select`, `country` → `country_select`) and a submit button; a v1 transition cycle is kept and reported by rule 6 only when a draft made from it is published (published v1 versions are never re-checked; at run time C151.2's caps apply).
11. **No down-conversion:** no v2 document is turned back into a v1 flow; clients that only know v1 get the projection of C151.6.
12. **Honesty:** the format is ours and documented in `docs/flows/format.md`; modules are editor macros (C155.4), not a runtime type; parallel steps are not built (behaviour note F7, C177).

### C148. Screens and components

1. **The `screen` step** (behaviour note F6, F12): `{ id, type: "screen", name?, components: Component[] }`, discriminated by `kind`. A screen has a `next` port for its primary submit button and a `button:<key>` port for each further submit button, so two buttons can lead to two places (the public button transitions, behaviour note 1.4).
2. **The component catalogue v1** (public kinds, our names and members; decision 21):
   - **content:** `text` (`variant` `title`, `body` or `caption`; `copyKey`), `image` (a theme asset `assetId`, `altKey`, `decorative`), `spacer` (`size` `s`, `m`, `l`), `container` (`direction` `vertical` or `horizontal`; children), `footer` (children; the fixed help slot of C162.3 is rendered after it), `stepper` (operator labels `labelKeys[]` and a `current` index: a static indicator, ours, since a branching flow has no fixed step count);
   - **form:** `text_input`, `text_area`, `number`, `date`, `email`, `phone` (allowed countries), `select`, `radio_group`, `checkbox`, `checkbox_group`, `multi_select`, `country_select` (allowed countries), `url`, `masked_text` (shown masked once entered), `address` (street lines, city, subdivision, postal code, country; no autocomplete, C177);
   - **buttons:** `button` with `action` `submit` (with `port`, default `next`), `cancel` (asks for confirmation, then cancels the session; the inquiry stays resumable) or `link` (an allowlisted `https:` URL from the version's link fields, C162.3, opened with `rel="noopener noreferrer"`). The public Complete button is a `submit` whose edge leads to a `completion` step, and the public Action button a `submit` whose edge leads to an `action` step (documented).
3. **Field binding:** each form component binds `field`, a template field key (C149); the publish check refuses a type mismatch (`text_input`, `text_area`, `masked_text` → `string` or `email-address`; `number` → `integer` or `number`; `date` → `date`; `checkbox` → `boolean`; `select`, `radio_group` → `choices`; `multi_select`, `checkbox_group` → `multi-choices`; `country_select`, `phone`, `url`, `email` → `string`; `address` → its declared field set). Validation members: `required`, `minLength`, `maxLength`, `min`, `max` (numbers, dates), `allowedCountries`, `options[] {key, value, labelKey}` (≤ 1,000; `key` generated, C162.2), and `pattern`, an ECMAScript `u` regex of at most 500 characters from a restricted grammar (no backreferences, no lookaround, no nested quantifiers), checked at publish (`pattern_refused`). The grammar (verification round V10): literal code points and escaped metacharacters; `.`; bracket classes with ranges and negation over code points; `\d` and `\w` (ASCII, as in `u` mode) and `\s` (ECMAScript's fixed whitespace list); `^` and `$`; alternation; capturing and `(?:…)` groups; `*`, `+`, `?` and `{n}`, `{n,}`, `{n,m}` with `m` ≤ 100; the `u` flag only. No `\p{…}` property escapes and no `i`, `m`, `s` or `y` flag, since both need Unicode property or case-folding data the plan does not source; a pattern using them is refused with its reason. The grammar alone does not stop backtracking (`^(a|a)*$` is exponential with no nesting, and adjacent overlapping quantifiers are polynomial; review P03), so a pattern never reaches `RegExp`: it is compiled to our own linear-time matcher (a Pike VM over the restricted grammar, `packages/flow-spec/src/v2/pattern/`, no new dependency), used by the server and by both clients' inline feedback (the web flow's from Phase 9, Android's Kotlin port against the same vectors in Phase 10, C175.1), so they always agree; golden vectors compare it with `RegExp` on non-pathological inputs, and a test feeds the known pathological patterns 10,000-character inputs within a time budget. Inputs stay capped at 10,000 characters (Phase 11's G-REGEX re-checks it).
4. **Visibility and enablement** (behaviour note F13): `visibleWhen` and `enabledWhen` on a component, written in the strict subset (`var` over `screen.<field>` of the **same screen** only, `===`, `!==`, `==` as an alias of `===`, `!`, `!!`, `and`, `or`, `in`), evaluated by the client on load and on every change and **re-evaluated by the server** over the submitted values: a hidden component's value is discarded and its `required` ignored. A rule naming another screen's field or any server fact is refused at publish (`visibility_scope_invalid`), so no signal, verification or account fact ever reaches the client for presentation. Nothing that decides an outcome depends on visibility.
5. **Submission:** `POST /api/v1/flow/screens/:step-id/submit` (flow API, session token) with `{ values: { <field>: value }, button: <key> }`: only for the current step (`flow_step_not_current`, 409, C151.4); the server validates types, visible required fields, lengths, ranges, patterns, options and countries (Arabic-Indic digits normalised first, C161.4), writes `fields` (sealed, merged; a write-once field that holds a value answers 422 `field_write_once`), records the step's exit through the button's port, advances (C151.2) and answers the session response. A resubmission of a step already exited answers the current session with 200. `values` joins the API-log masked members.
6. **Limits:** 40 components per screen, container nesting ≤ 3, a value ≤ 10,000 characters, 1,000 options.
7. **Built in, not configured** (WCAG 2.2): every form component has a visible label from its copy key; errors are linked by `aria-describedby` and summarised at the top on submit; `autocomplete` tokens by type (`email`, `tel`, `bday`, `street-address`, `postal-code`, `country`); targets ≥ 24 CSS px; focus moves to the screen's heading on arrival; a field that already holds a value (prefilled, entered earlier, or copied from an extraction) is shown filled and editable unless write-once (3.3.7). Verification steps that collect a value (`phone_otp`, `email_otp`, `database`, `aamva` with `source: form`) gain an optional `prefillFrom: <field key>` (typed at publish), and show that field's value filled and editable, so a number entered on a screen is not asked again by the phone step (review H22).
8. **Not built** (C177): File upload, Home, List Container, the Device Handoff Button (Phase 7's `device_handoff` step covers the handoff), ID type select (the `government_id` step chooses the type), country multi select, international database inputs, the vendor-bound bank component, dynamic images from a field.

### C149. Template fields and prefill

1. **`field-schemas`** on the template version (decision 16; the public name; ours on the version, so it is pinned with the flow): `[{ key, type, label, pii, maxLength?, default?, writeOnce?, stripHtml?, choices?[] {value, label} }]`. Types (the public subset): `string`, `integer`, `number`, `boolean`, `date`, `datetime`, `email-address`, `choices`, `multi-choices`, `array` (of strings). Not built: `file`, `hash`, `json`, `platform-file`, `relation-schema` (C177).
2. **Keys and changes** (public): keys match the existing `INQUIRY_FIELD_NAME_PATTERN`; a key published once keeps its type in every later version (`field_type_changed`); a later version may add fields and may leave one out (it stays readable on inquiries that pinned an older version); at most 1,000 fields (public), with the inquiry's own `MAX_INQUIRY_FIELDS` raised from today's 100 to match (a C10 amendment), and a cap on the serialised size of `fields` of 256 KiB (provisional, with a test; 422 `fields_too_large`, V17) on every write path, since 1,000 values of up to 10,000 characters would make a 10 MB sealed blob decrypted and re-encrypted on every submission (review F25).
3. **Validation:** when the pinned version declares `field-schemas`, every write of `fields` (API create and `PATCH`, screen submissions, `copy_extractions`) is validated by them and an unknown key answers 422 `field_unknown` with its pointer; a version without `field-schemas` keeps today's untyped record.
4. **Prefill through the API only** (decision 14; behaviour note F16; ours: the public product also accepts a `fields` URL parameter and an embed option): the `fields` of `POST /inquiries` are prefill; no URL parameter or embed option sets a field, so personal data never lands in a URL, a history or a referrer. The values set at creation are also kept in `inquiries.initial_fields` (sealed, row-bound, never changed). `id_inquiry_comparison` and `database_inquiry_comparison` compare with a field's initial value when one exists, else its current value, and record `comparison-source: initial | entered` in their evidence, so a person cannot edit an expected name to match the document they hold (an R-tier change with tests).
5. **Write-once** (public write policy): once a value is set, neither the flow nor the API changes it (`field_write_once`); `stripHtml` removes tags on write; `maxLength` is enforced on every write. The public redaction policy ("never redact" for non-PII fields) is not built: redaction empties every field, whatever its `pii` mark (ours, fail closed; review H25).
6. **References:** `fieldReferences(version)` lists every component, condition, copy placeholder and action that reads or writes each field; the builder shows it (public).
7. **Masking:** every `fields` value stays masked without `pii:view`, whatever its `pii` mark (fail closed, as today); `pii: false` only lets a string field be compared with a literal in a condition (Phase 8 C114.5) and is stated as such in the docs.

### C150. The flow scope, conditions and dynamic friction

1. **The `branch` step** (behaviour note F6, F8; decisions 2 and 5): `{ id, type: "branch", name?, routes: [{ key, label, condition, onNull }], waitFor?: { facts: [paths], maxSeconds: 1–30 } }`. Routes are tried in order and the first whose condition holds is taken; the `else` port is the catch-all (public: an unconditioned route last). Conditions are Phase 8's dialect (C114: the operator allowlist, the limits, `==` as an alias of `===`), evaluated by `@openverify/workflow-engine`'s evaluator **on the server only** (ADR 0008); the flow-spec package receives the evaluator as a parameter, so neither client bundle carries it.
2. **The scope** (typed with Phase 8's C115.3 DSL; its `z.toJSONSchema()` feeds the condition builder):
   - `inquiry`: `status`, `fields.<key>` (typed by C149, PII as marked), `tags`, `created-at`, `started-at`;
   - `verifications.<step id>`: `status`, `attempts`, `attempts-remaining`, `checks.<check name>.status` (`passed`, `failed`, `not_applicable`, nullable: a check that is `pending`, or `not_applicable` with a reason in the registry's new `CHECK_ERROR_REASONS` (`execution_error`, `input_missing`, `provider_not_configured`, every `*_unavailable`), reads `null`, so an outage needs the route's `onNull` and never reads as "not failed"; review H03; a golden `nextPosition` test, `flow-spec-logic`'s, and an engine test, `api-flow-engine`'s, show that an advisory check errored with each reason class never takes a route conditioned on `{"!==": [<check status>, "failed"]}` or its `!` form, verification round), and the extracted values each type exposes, listed with their type, PII mark and tier in `FLOW_SCOPE_EXTRACTIONS` (for example `government_id`: `country-code`, `id-class`, `birthdate`, `age-years`, `expiration-date`; `document`: `document-type`);
   - `account`: `account-type-id`, `status`, `tags`;
   - `device`: `kind` (`desktop-web`, `mobile-web`, `native`; ours, a pure mapping with golden vectors from Phase 7 C91's parsed `device-type` and `mobile-sdk-name`: `mobile-sdk-name` set → `native`; `smartphone` or `tablet` → `mobile-web`; `desktop` → `desktop-web`; `tv` or `null` → `null`. It is never Phase 7's `device-class`, which is a pseudonymous HMAC; reviews H27, F15) and `platform` (`web`, `android`), both nullable;
   - `signals.<name>`: Phase 7's values (C90) with their tier and stage, nullable (K3), **only** for signals the registry marks `flow-provisional` and only through the provisional snapshot of 9, because Phase 7 computes the inquiry's own signals on its first move into a terminal state (C90.5), after the person's part; any other signal is refused at publish (`scope_path_unavailable_in_flow`; review H02);
   - `lists.<list id>.matched`: `true`, `false` or `null`, only for the list screenings Phase 7 completes before the inquiry does, which `contracts-9a` names from Phase 7 as built; any other list is refused at publish (`scope_path_unavailable_in_flow`). Phase 7 C99.4 screens at the first move out of `pending`, so as planned no list qualifies and `lists.*` is refused in flows until a screening runs earlier (stated in `docs/flows/`; review H02);
   - `workflows.<step id>`: `status` and `outputs.<name>`, typed from the workflow version's output mappings (C152.2), nullable (K2);
   - `session`: `locale`, `resumed`, and Phase 7's `handoff.state`, nullable.
3. **Typing at publish** (Phase 8 C114.5, C114.6, `definition_invalid` problems): an unknown path is `scope_path_unknown`; a path of a step that does not dominate the branch is typed nullable; a PII string compared with a literal is `pii_literal_in_condition`; a nullable path as a direct boolean operand is `nullable_in_boolean_position`; a route that reads a nullable path without `onNull` is `on_null_missing`, with **no default** (the builder suggests Phase 7's `ON_NULL_DEFAULTS` for signals). `onNull` takes `route:<key>`, `skip-route` (try the next route), `else`, or `review` (take `else` and send the inquiry to `needs_review` at completion, recorded on the step record; review H08); there is no `error` value, because a person must never reach a dead end. The engine receives each null's reason class from Phase 7's `ON_NULL_DEFAULTS`: a `needs_review`-class null (a data or collection gap such as `provider_not_configured`) always flags review, whatever `onNull` says, and the builder maps `ON_NULL_DEFAULTS` onto `review` or `else`.
4. **Strict evaluation everywhere** (decision 3):
   - **server:** a typed nullable value is `null` and follows the route's `onNull`, never `false`; a path that a stored version names but the scope no longer has (a removed signal) never follows `else`: it records `scope_path_missing` on the step record, ends the person's part on the completion screen, and sends the inquiry to `needs_review` (C151.3), so it never chooses a route, `else` included (review H08);
   - **web:** the web flow no longer evaluates transitions at all (C151), and visibility rules run only through `packages/flow-spec`'s strict `visibility` evaluator (C148.4), the one the server uses; `json-logic.ts` gains no strict mode (review F26);
   - **Android:** Phase 10 (C175.1); until then the SDK only follows the unconditional projection (C151.6).
5. **Waiting for facts:** `waitFor` names nullable facts the branch may wait for (signals still computing, a list screening running), up to `maxSeconds`; the session shows a waiting screen (`position.state: waiting`, polled every 2 s); after the deadline, values still `null` follow `onNull`; the step record notes the wait and which facts were null.
6. **Retries and fallbacks** (behaviour note F11; decision 6): `maxAttempts` (1–10) stays the hard bound; `attempts` and `attempts-remaining` are in scope; the optional `exhausted` port is taken when a verification step ends without passing (its attempts used up, or a failure that cannot be retried); without it, today's rule holds and the inquiry fails (C50.5). On a path that took an `exhausted` port, the inquiry goes to `needs_review` at completion (`onFallback: "review"`, the default); `onFallback: "continue"` needs the publish acknowledgement `fallback:<step id>`. The failed verification stays `failed` on every exit and is never counted as passed. This is the fallback route Phase 10's NFC step needs (behaviour note M22 of the Android note).
7. **Adverse paths** (behaviour note F10; Phase 8 C128, not legal advice; decision 9): a **verification outcome** is a verification's own decided `status`, `attempts` and `attempts-remaining`, and nothing else; every `checks.*` status and every extracted value (`country-code`, `birthdate`, `age-years`, `id-class`, `document-type`) is a non-outcome fact (reviews H07, P25). A `failure` step reachable through a route that reads anything other than this inquiry's verification outcomes (checks, extractions, signals, lists, account, device, workflow outputs, fields), or through the `else` edge or an `onNull` resolution of a branch whose routes read such facts (review H08), needs the acknowledgement `adverse-path:<failure step id>`; in production it also needs `decisionBasis` (`contract`, `law`, `explicit-consent`) and `contestRoute` on the failure step (C128.3's forms). Whether such a path is biometric follows C128.4's three cases (a biometric result read, an inquiry status or a check status of a template with any biometric check or face list), decided by Phase 8's own function, not a copy, and a biometric path needs `biometricBasis` (review H19). With `explicit-consent` as either basis, publish requires that a consent step dominating the failure lists `consent.automated-decision` (and `consent.biometric` on a biometric path), and at run time the engine checks the recorded consent as C128.4 does: a missing key sends the inquiry to `needs_review` instead of failing it, recorded on the decision record (reviews P25, H19). **Heuristics, whichever way they route** (review H07): every route that reads a non-required check, an H-tier check or extraction, or an H-tier signal needs `heuristic:<step id>`, whether it adds friction, lowers it or fails the person, as Phase 8 C128.3 asks of adverse steps. When an inquiry fails on such a path, a decision record (`inquiry_decisions`, kind `flow-failure`, ours) records the routes taken, the facts as codes with their tiers, `solely-automated: true`, the basis and the contest route, and the failed screen shows the contest route (C128.7's `decision-contest-route`, extended to failed inquiries). The builder's templates offer `flag_for_review` (C152.6) first, as the public guidance on signals advises review over decline.
8. **Honesty:** a branch on a `beta` heuristic inherits that heuristic's error rate, and the builder shows the tier, stage and source (10) of every fact a path reads; an unknown is never false; the server is the only evaluator, so a modified client cannot choose a path except through the facts it reports itself or the person types, which are marked (reviews P06, H20).
9. **Provisional signals in the flow** (review H02; decision 68; a Phase 7 C90 amendment, labelled ours): the signal registry (C90.1) gains `flow-provisional: boolean`, true for signals whose inputs exist before completion (network, device and session facts; `contracts-9a` names them from Phase 7 as built), false for signals that read completion-time facts (`inquiry/list_match_count` and any signal fed by a verification or a screening). A branch that reads `signals.<name>` must name those signals in its `waitFor` (publish error `signal_wait_missing`). When the branch is entered, the engine enqueues a `flow-signal-snapshot` job that computes the named signals from the session facts stored so far through Phase 7's own computation function (the `inquiry-signals` job's compute step, named in the inventory), and writes `inquiry_flow_signal_snapshots (environment_id, inquiry_id, step_record_seq, name, value, tier, stage, null_reason, computed_at)` (PII-free: Phase 7's signal values are counts, levels, a country code and distances; deleted with the step records). The branch waits for it within `maxSeconds`; values still missing follow `onNull`. **Advancing** (verification round V09): once every named signal is written, the snapshot job calls `advanceFlowInTx` under the inquiry row lock, so the branch moves as soon as its facts exist; a wait whose deadline passes is ended by the session poll or by `flow-action-sweep`, whichever comes first (both exits of C151.2). **Before the first collector payload** (verification round V06, plausible: Phase 7's collector starts after consent and posts per-screen aggregates, so a branch right after consent can be snapshotted before anything is stored): a client-collected signal whose session has sent no payload yet is recorded with the null reason `not_yet_collected`, a flow-only reason in the `ignore` class of `ON_NULL_DEFAULTS` (a C90 amendment, labelled ours), so the route's own `onNull` decides instead of Phase 7's `collector_missing`, whose `needs_review` class would flag every early inquiry; the builder warns when a branch on a client-collected signal is not preceded by a screen after consent, and a test puts such a branch right after consent. The inquiry's own `signals` attribute is unchanged: it is still computed once at completion (C90.5) and may differ from a snapshot taken earlier, which the docs and the builder say. Simulate and the 9A e2e show a non-null provisional signal choosing a route (C156.4, C178.4).
10. **Where each fact comes from** (reviews P06, H20): every scope path carries `source` in `FLOW_SCOPE_EXTRACTIONS` and in the scope's JSON Schema: `verification` (decided by a verification: statuses, checks, extractions), `server` (observed or computed by the server: the account, lists, workflow outputs, server-side signals, `session.resumed`), `person` (typed or chosen by the person: fields entered on screens, screen buttons), `client` (reported by the client software: `device.*`, `session.locale`, signals from the client collector, which Phase 7 marks) and `operator` (set by the customer's server through the API: prefilled fields, tags). The builder labels `person` facts "self-asserted" and `client` facts "client-reported". Publish needs `asserted-fact:<branch id>` in production (sandbox warns) when a route on a `person` or `client` fact leads to a `completion` through fewer verification types than the branch's `else` path, because a person or a modified client can choose that fact.
11. **Routes that can tip off** (review P37; not legal advice): the builder warns when a route on `lists.*` or on a screening-kind signal leads to a person-visible difference (an extra screen, a waiting screen, a failure), and recommends `flag_for_review` (C152.6) with an unchanged path; `docs/flows/` says why (tipping-off rules in anti-money-laundering law).
12. **Typing and evaluation over the flow scope** (review F05): Phase 8's typing (C114.5, C114.6, C115.3) resolves paths against its fixed roots, and its `on-null` values are `error`, `route:<key>` and `skip-route`. The flow needs one more entry point in `packages/workflow-engine`: type a condition against a caller-supplied scope description, and evaluate it reporting which operand met a `null`, so `nextPosition` can apply the flow's own `onNull` (including `else` and `review`) itself. `contracts-9a` names Phase 8's entry points in the inventory; if they are not parameterised by a scope, `flow-spec-logic` adds the parameterised export as a borrowed edit (a new function only, Phase 8's suites green).

### C151. The server flow engine and the session API

1. **Model** (ADR 0008): each inquiry has a position on `inquiries` (`flow_step_id`, `flow_state` `active`, `waiting`, `halted` or `ended`, `flow_previous_step_id`, `flow_spec_version`), the internal `first_device_kind` and `first_platform` (those of the session that served the first step, set by the engine when it records that step; reviews F14, H05), and an append-only step record `inquiry_flow_steps (environment_id, inquiry_id, seq, step_id, step_type, entered_at, exited_at, exit_port, outcome, waited_ms, sensitive, notes[])`, written in the transaction that moves the position, under the inquiry row lock. It holds ids, step ids and codes, no value, but it is **pseudonymous, not PII-free**: a route on a field, an extraction or a screen button the person chose can reveal what they entered (review P15). `sensitive` is set at write time when the exit is a screen button other than `next` or a route that reads a PII-marked path; no Phase 9 route serves the step record, and any later one serves sensitive rows' ports only with PII visibility.
2. **Advancing:** `advanceFlowInTx(tx, inquiry, exit)` assembles the scope (C150.2) and calls the pure `nextPosition(flow, scope, from, port, evaluate)` of `packages/flow-spec`, which walks from the exited port through `branch` and `action` steps until it reaches a step a person sees, a wait, or a terminal step. Each exit advances in the transaction that records it (the verification decision, the consent, the submission), so no reader ever sees a finished step as current. Exits come from: consent recorded; a screen submitted; a verification decided (`passed`, `needs_review` or `skipped` → `next`; ended without passing → `exhausted`, or the inquiry fails when it has no such edge); an action finished or errored; a branch's signal snapshot written (C150.9); a branch wait elapsed, found by the session poll or by `flow-action-sweep` (verification round V09); Phase 7's handoff resolved. Caps: 20 entries of one step and 200 moves per inquiry; past either the flow is `halted` with `flow_loop_limit`, the session shows that it cannot continue, and the console shows the inquiry for an operator to expire or resume (ours). The pure outcome rules of 3 live in `flowOutcome(stepRecord, verificationOutcomes, flags)` in `packages/flow-spec`, used by the engine and by simulate alike (review H16).
3. **Required verifications** (C7, C30.5, C50.5 amended): the outcome considers only the verification types whose steps were entered on the path (from the step record) and the current step. The inquiry completes when the position reaches a `completion` step with every entered verification decided; fails when it reaches a `failure` step or a verification step ends without passing and has no `exhausted` edge; and goes to `needs_review` at completion when any entered verification needs review, an `exhausted` edge was taken with `onFallback: "review"`, a `flag_for_review` action ran, a branch took `onNull: "review"` or met a `needs_review`-class null, or a branch recorded `scope_path_missing` (which ends the person's part at once, C150.4). A golden equivalence test runs every stored v1 fixture, branching ones included and one whose condition reads a path outside v1's context, through both the v1 rules and the migrated v2 engine and requires the same path and outcome (review F07).
4. **Only the current step:** the flow API records consent, creates or reuses a verification, accepts an upload or submission, a screen submission and the handoff routes only for the step the position names; anything else answers 409 `flow_step_not_current` (no detail of another path). This closes today's gap in which `createFlowVerification` accepts any verification type the flow has, in any order.
5. **Session response** (C3, additive; every member optional in the schema so older documents still validate):
   - `position {step-id, state, previous-step-id, retry-after-ms?}`, where `previous-step-id` names only a person-visible step (never a branch or an action; review P14);
   - `step`: the current step's **client view**, sent only to a client that declared spec 2 (7): an allowlist of the members the renderer needs (for a screen, its components with their copy keys, validation and visibility rules), never the step's `name` or any member outside the allowlist; branch and action internals are never sent to any client, so no route condition, threshold or signal name reaches an end user (C3's rule that the flow API shows no fraud detail; review P14);
   - `values` (review P16): for a screen, the current values of the fields bound **on that screen** only, so a prefilled or earlier value is shown filled (C148.7); a `masked_text` value is never sent back (`{set: true}`; re-entered to change), nor a write-once value (`{set: true}`, shown as set); a verification step with `prefillFrom` (C148.7) gets that one field's value; no other field leaves the server (a contract fixture and a test);
   - `theme` (C160.7), `copy` (C162.6), `locales {enabled[], default}`, `embed {allowed-origins[]}` (C159.4), `completion {redirect-url}` (C158.5), `spec-version` (the version served);
   - `flow`: the v1 projection (6), for every client.
6. **The v1 projection** (decisions 2 and 73; rewritten after reviews H01, F01: today's SDK does not refetch the session after consent, walks the steps it holds, and falls back to list order, so a projection of `[current, completion, failure]` would show "Completed" right after consent): `{ metadata {specVersion: 1, id, version, name}, steps: [...], transitions: [] }`, where `steps` lists, in order, the person-visible v1 steps the path has already exited **that today's SDK passes as done** (a recorded consent; a verification step whose verification passed, or whose decided status the SDK's `verificationState` accepts as done, which the inventory names from `FlowCoordinator.kt`), the current step, and then the following run of steps the path reaches **unconditionally** that have a v1 form. The run ends at a terminal step, which is included, or, where the path next meets a `branch`, an `action`, a wait, a `screen` or any other v2-only step, at a **placeholder** `{ id: "continue-<n>", type: "server-continuation" }` that no v1 client knows, so today's SDK decodes it as `UnsupportedStep` (`Flow.kt:444-457`) and stops on its unsupported screen there, never on "Completed". A terminal step is never listed after a placeholder. A current step with no v1 form is itself sent as such a placeholder, and so is a consent step that uses custom consent copy (C162.9: today's SDK cannot show the operator's words, so it must not record consent to its own; since consent dominates every upload, C147.6, such a version stops today's SDK at its first step; verification round V11). A verification step exited through `exhausted`, or exited in any other way without passing, is **left out** of the exited steps (verification round V02): today's SDK treats a step whose attempts are used up as `Resolution.Finalize` (`FlowCoordinator.kt:598-600`, "the server … fails the inquiry in a moment") and polls until it sees the failure, which a v2 fallback never produces, so listing it would leave the person on `STILL_PROCESSING` instead of the fallback step. No condition, step name or v2 internal is ever sent. The member's schema is `flowProjectionSchema` (v1 metadata; `steps` of v1 steps or placeholders; `transitions` an empty array), not today's strict v1 `flowSchema`, which cannot carry a placeholder (review F06; a golden fixture per v2-only step type). **The proof is a test of the SDK's navigation, not of decoding:** a `FlowCoordinator` host test against a fake `FlowApi` serves the successive projections of a consent → government ID → selfie → completion flow, of a flow with a branch, and of a consent → government ID exhausted → document → completion flow, and asserts that no `Completed` state appears before the server's position is a completion step and that no `Finalizing` loop appears in the exhausted case (the one test-only Android edit besides the fixture list; Rules). **The proof chain** (verification round V03): `contracts-9a` commits the host test **green** in Stage 1a over hand-written golden fixtures of the successive projections (the SDK is unchanged, so a red result means the projection rules are wrong and stops the Workflow); `flow-spec-logic` adds a golden test that `projection(flow, position)` produces each of those fixtures from its flow and position; `api-flow-engine` adds a session-builder test that serves them byte for byte. The fixtures then change only through `integration-9a`, so the Android test and the server's output cannot drift apart. Linear flows therefore keep working on today's SDK, and v2-only steps stop safely until Phase 10.
7. **Negotiation** (ours): the request header `OpenVerify-Flow-Spec` lists the versions a client renders (`1, 2`); absent means `1`; unknown values are ignored; the response header names the version served. Negotiation changes only what is sent, never the path, a wait or the required verifications, so declaring an older version cannot route a person around friction. The web flow sends `1, 2`; publishing a version that uses screens, actions, waits or custom consent copy warns that clients declaring only spec 1 show their unsupported screen there (for custom consent copy, at the first step; verification round V11). The SDK's `User-Agent` (`OpenVerify-Android/<version>`) fills `device.platform`.
8. **Inquiry attributes** (public names): `previous-step-name` and `next-step-name` (the current step's id; `null` once ended). Without PII visibility each is `null` when the step was reached through a `sensitive` exit (1), because the step it names can reveal a masked value (review P15); `filter[next-step-name]` (C165.3) needs PII visibility. Step ids are visible to the person (5) and to operators, so the builder generates opaque ids (`s_` and 8 random base32 characters, names kept separately), and publish warns when a person-visible step id or a component id contains a word from a short list (`pep`, `sanction`, `fraud`, `risk`, `watchlist`, `flag`); the docs say ids are visible (review P14).
9. **No event per step:** analytics reads the step record; `inquiry.started` and the existing events are unchanged.
10. **Expiry windows** (behaviour note F20; public `meta` names): template settings `inquiry-expiration-after-start-seconds` (absent: no change at start) and `inquiry-expiration-after-resume-seconds` (default 86,400); on `created → pending` the expiry becomes `started-at + value`, and on a resume `max(expires-at, now + value)` (an absent after-start value changing nothing at start is ours; the public default is the creation window; review H25); `POST /inquiries` accepts `meta.expiration-after-create-interval-seconds`, `meta.expiration-after-start-interval-seconds`, `meta.expiration-after-resume-interval-seconds` and `meta.one-time-link-expiration-seconds` (public), within C11's bounds; semantics the public sources leave open are ours and documented.
11. **Races** (Testcontainers): two tabs submitting one screen; a verification decision racing a screen submission; a resume during a branch wait; an action's run completing as its timeout sweep runs.
12. **Honesty:** the server's position is the only truth; nothing a client sends moves it except the current step's own submission; a step that cannot be rendered is shown as unsupported, never skipped; the step record is the engine's evidence of the path taken: it decides which verifications are required (3), and nothing else (review H30).
13. **Completed without evidence** (review H09): a production version needs the acknowledgement `unverified-path:<completion step id>` (sandbox warns) for every completion reachable through no verification step, or through a path that skips a verification type another path of the flow requires. Every inquiry carries a PII-free `flow-outcome { verification-types-on-path[], fallbacks-taken[], flags[] }` (C10 amended), served on the inquiry and in `inquiry.completed`'s `meta` (a C5 payload amendment with a golden sample), so a consumer can tell a fully verified completion from one through a fallback or with no verification; analytics splits them (C164.1).
14. **Where the position starts** (review F08): the position is set to `entry`, under the row lock, on the `created → pending` move (`startInquiry`, the first `GET /flow/session`). An inquiry with a NULL position pinned to a v1 version (every inquiry at the migration, and the existing test factories) is positioned once, on its next flow request, by replaying the migrated flow from `entry` over its stored facts (consent, verification statuses), recorded as one synthetic step-record entry with the note `position_backfilled`, so an in-flight inquiry keeps the verifications it has done; a test for each factory path in `packages/db/src/testing.ts` and the API harness.

### C152. Action steps and embedded workflows (K1, K2)

1. **The `action` step** (decision 10): `{ id, type: "action", name?, action: { kind, …config } }`, ports `next` and `error`; publish refuses an action without an `error` edge (`action_error_edge_missing`), so a failure always has a path the author chose. The closed list follows; custom JavaScript and HTTPS requests stay in Phase 8 workflows behind their sandbox and guard (behaviour note F14; reach them through `run_workflow`).
2. **`run_workflow`** (K1): `{ workflowId, version: "latest-published" | wfv_…, inputs: { <name>: scope path or literal }, timeoutSeconds: 1–300 (default 30) }`. The target's trigger type is `trigger-flow` (ours; a ninth Phase 8 trigger type, C112.4 amended, startable only by a flow action, with the inquiry as subject and declared inputs). The run is created through Phase 8's run service in the transaction that enters the step, with creator `inquiry-flow` and the inquiry id (C120.1 amended), unique per `(inquiry, step, entry)`; the session waits (`position.state: waiting`) and the engine advances on a poll or on the `flow-action-sweep` (15 s; it also ends expired branch waits, C150.9): `completed` puts the outputs in scope (`workflows.<step id>.outputs`) and takes `next`; `errored`, `canceled` or the timeout take `error`, with `workflows.<step id>.status` and `.error-type` in scope and the outputs `null`. At the timeout the engine **cancels** the run through Phase 8's run cancellation (C120), recorded on the action row, so a run can no longer decline the inquiry or change its account after the person has been shown where the flow went; a step already executing when the cancel lands finishes, and the docs say so (review H21; a Testcontainers test). **Authority** (Phase 8 C121): the template publisher must hold `workflows:trigger`, every scope of the target version's grant and `pii:view` when the grant has `pii`, checked when the template is published; a flow-started run has no causing principal (the end user's flow, C121.3) and acts on its frozen grant; a target with egress marks needs `egress:<step id>` re-acknowledged on the template; only workflows of the same environment; `latest-published` resolves on entry and is recorded. **Checked again on entry** (review P07, as Phase 8 C121.3 checks a child run): the target version's grant (scopes and `pii`) and egress marks are stored on the template version with the publish acknowledgement, and on entry the resolved version's grant and egress must be subsets of them; otherwise no run starts, the step takes `error` with `workflows.<step id>.error-type: workflow_grant_widened`, and the template shows a banner naming the workflow until it is republished (a Testcontainers test publishes a wider workflow version after the template).
3. **`copy_extractions`:** `{ fromStep, mapping: { <field key>: <extraction attribute> } }`: copies a decided verification's extracted values into fields (types checked at publish; write-once respected).
4. **`update_account_fields`:** `{ mapping: { <account field key>: scope path or literal } }` on the inquiry's account through the accounts service (the account type validates), exactly once per entry, firing `account.updated-fields`. Each copied key is recorded on the action's own row (C152.8: `copied_keys`, `target_account_id`), because Phase 8's copy record is a member of a workflow run's effect row (`phase-8.plan.md:267`, `:418`) and a flow action has no run (verification round V08); redacting the inquiry lists those targets in its result and audit entry, as Phase 8 does for a workflow's account copies, and the docs say what redaction does to such copies (review P13).
5. **`tag_inquiry`:** `{ add[], remove[] }` with literal tag names only (Phase 8 C115.6's rule: no tag from a PII value).
6. **`flag_for_review`:** `{ reasonCode }` (snake_case, ≤ 64): the inquiry goes to `needs_review` at completion with the code recorded and shown in the console, never to the person.
7. **Honesty:** a timed-out or errored workflow is never read as an output; its outputs are `null`, and the `error` edge is the only way on.
