8. **Action rows** (review P13): `inquiry_flow_actions (environment_id, inquiry_id, step_id, entry, workflow_run_id, status, error_type, target_account_id, copied_keys text[], entered_at, finished_at)` is PII-free (keys and ids, never a value); `target_account_id` and `copied_keys` are set once per entry by `update_account_fields` (V08); outputs are read from Phase 8's sealed run outputs and never copied into it, and late outputs of a canceled run stay only there. Inquiry redaction first reads the copy targets through `flowActionCopyTargets(inquiryId)` (stub in `flow-actions/copies.ts`, `api-flow-actions`, called from `services/redaction.ts` by Stage 1b (S)) into its result and audit entry, then deletes the rows; they follow the inquiry's retention (C174.5), with a redaction-coverage entry.

### C153. Check configuration, settings and schema-driven forms (F19, F20, F23, K14)

1. **One form engine** (console, `components/schema-form/`): forms generated from `z.toJSONSchema()` of the contracts for every template-version member (`verification-config` per type with each check's registry `configSchema`, `settings`, `report-config`, Phase 7's `signal-config` and `list-config`) and for report template versions; the raw `JsonEditor` stays one click away; no field list is written by hand. Each check shows its tier, its type (`user_behavior` or `fraud`), its stage with the `beta` badge and its default requirement from the registry, and the form says that an enabled check that is not required runs but does not decide (ADR 0002 §3c), unless a flow branch reads it; each check panel lists the branches that read the check (review H07).
2. **Capture methods by device class** (behaviour note F19; decision 22): steps with `captureMethods` gain `captureMethodsByDevice { desktop-web?, mobile-web?, native? }`, each a non-empty subset of `CAPTURE_METHODS`; the client applies its class's entry; uploads gain a client-reported `capture-method` multipart field for analytics (C3 amended), stored in a new `files.capture_method` column (review F14). Labelled guidance: the server cannot prove how a photo was taken, so the existing production rules (Android photos from the live camera) stay where they are.
3. **Settings forms:** every C11 setting, C151.10's windows, Phase 7's `max-sessions` and `expire-inquiry-on-session-limit`, with the 2.2.1 warning below 20 hours (C171.4).
4. **Report templates** (K14): structured forms over report template versions and a create-template form (`POST /report-templates` exists since Phase 6), with the JSON editor kept as the fallback.
5. **Honesty:** a form never offers a value the contract refuses, and wherever a check can be enabled its tier is shown.

### C154. Template versions, publishing, promote and deployments

1. **Version attributes** (C11 amended): `publish-note` (≤ 255, locked at publish; public), `description` (≤ 2,000, editable on every version; public), `last-updater {type, id}` (an OpenVerify id; the public attribute is an email address, which the API never serves, as Phase 8 decision 64 has it), `field-schemas` (C149), `copy` (C162), `enabled-locales` (public) and `default-locale`, `theme-set-id` (C160.7), `embed-origins` and `redirect-uri` with `redirect-uri-schemes` (C158), `validation {status: valid | invalid, problems[] {pointer, code, detail, severity}}` (ours), `flow-sha256`, and `published-at` (public; served from the existing `published_at` column; review H23).
2. **Drafts save with problems** (public, behaviour note 1.10; decision 11): `PATCH` stores a draft whose members pass their shape schemas but fail coherence, graph, field or publish rules when the request carries `meta.allow-invalid: true` (the console always does), with `validation.status: invalid`; without it, today's 422 stays, so existing API clients see no change. Publishing an invalid draft answers 422 with every problem.
3. **Concurrency:** every version answers a strong `ETag` (the SHA-256 of its stored content); `PATCH` and `publish` honour `If-Match` and answer 412 `precondition_failed` with the current ETag on a mismatch (RFC 9110); without `If-Match` the API stays last-write-wins for existing clients; the console always sends it.
4. **Routes** (ours unless marked public):

   | Route | Scope | Notes |
   |---|---|---|
   | `POST /inquiry-template-versions/:id/validate` | `inquiry-templates:read` | a dry run of every rule including the async publish hooks, on the stored draft or an unsaved body |
   | `GET /inquiry-template-versions/:id/diff?against=<itmplv_>` | `inquiry-templates:read` | 6 |
   | `POST /inquiry-template-versions/:id/preview`, `…/simulate` | `inquiry-templates:read` | C156 |
   | `POST /inquiry-templates/:id/restore` | `inquiry-templates:write` | `meta.version-id`; creates a draft from that version (`draft_exists` when one exists), as Phase 8 decision 19 does (decision 12) |
   | `POST /inquiry-templates/:id/copy` | `inquiry-templates:write` | `meta.name`, `meta.version-id?`; a new template whose draft copies the version (public copy) |
   | `GET /inquiry-templates/:id/used-by` | `inquiry-templates:read` | the Phase 8 workflows whose triggers or steps name it, report and case templates that reference it, and its embed origin narrowing (public "connections"); each referencing object is named only when the caller can read it (`workflows:read`, a case template's `visible-to-roles`), and otherwise counted per type ("2 workflows you cannot see"; review P34) |
   | `POST /inquiry-templates/:id/promote` | `inquiry-templates:write` in both environments | 5 |
   | `GET`, `POST /inquiry-templates/:id/translations` | read; write | public (C162.7) |
   | `GET /deployments`, `GET /deployments/:id` | `deployments:read` | public paths, 7 |

   Every `POST` honours C12's `Idempotency-Key`. Each route is frozen in its implementer's route file.
5. **Promote** (behaviour note F3; Phase 8 C112.2's rules; decision 1): copies a published version into another environment of the organisation as a draft of the target template, which is linked by `promoted-from-template-id` and created with the same name on the first promote; references are re-resolved by key or name (report templates, case templates, workflows of `run_workflow` steps, theme sets by name (C160.8), Phase 7 lists and signal sets); none or several candidates refuse with `promote_unresolved` naming each reference by JSON Pointer, and only to a caller who can read the target environment; users only (API keys get 403); `environments:production` when either side is production; never replaces a draft (`draft_exists`); audited in both environments.
6. **Diff** (server-computed between any two versions of one template, keyed by step and edge ids; ours: the public product compares older pairs only after a restore): steps added, removed and changed (each a list of JSON Pointers with before and after: definitions hold configuration, never subject data); edges added, removed and re-targeted; route order; `verification-config` per check; settings; `report-config`; field schemas; copy per locale and key; theme set; locales; layout-only changes collapsed. Consequences flagged in words: a required check disabled or made optional; a `ga` check replaced by a `beta` one; a verification step removed or now skippable on some path; a consent text changed (new sessions need fresh consent); a locale added that is not reviewed.
7. **Deployments** (public resource, read-only history; behaviour note F3; decision 13): each publish, including the publish of a promoted or restored draft, writes one `deployment` (`dply_`): `resource-type: inquiry-template`, `created-at`, the actor, and one route `{template-id, environment-id, rules[] {strategy: "constant", version-id}, source-deployment-id}`; `GET /deployments` filters by `filter[inquiry-template-id]` and `filter[environment-id]` (public; always the caller's environment, so another id answers an empty list; review H23) and accepts the public `filter[changes-only]` (every record writes one route, so it changes nothing); `deployment.completed` fires once per publish. In OpenVerify publishing is deploying to the template's own environment; weighted splits, request criteria, undeploy and "publish without deploy" are not built (C177).
8. **Events** (public names from the events page, which lists no webhook files for them; golden payloads registered here): `inquiry-template-version.created` (a draft), `inquiry-template-version.published`, `deployment.completed`; subjects `inquiry-template-version` and `deployment`, both resolved through their template for strict webhook filters (`inquiry-template-ids`).
9. **Publish rules added:** C147.6 (with the new consent rule); C148.3 and C148.4; C149.2; C150.3; the acknowledgements, bases and consent checks of C150.6 and C150.7 (`heuristic:` on every route that reads a heuristic); `signal_wait_missing` (C150.9); `asserted-fact:` (C150.10); the grants and egress of C152.2, stored for the check on entry; `unverified-path:` (C151.13); C158.2; C160.7 (an active set of this environment, through `themeSetPublishProblems`); C161.5; C162.2 (`legal-copy:` and the `{days}` placeholder) and C162.4. Acknowledgements are stored on the version with the publisher's id and time and written to the publish's audit entry, in Phase 8 C116.4's form.
10. **Honesty:** publishing never changes an inquiry already created (each pins its version); the diff never collapses a change with consequences into "layout only"; a deployment record says what was published in its own environment and never implies a version is live elsewhere.

### C155. The flow builder (console, Workflow 9C)

1. **One editor, two document types** (engineering note 3.2): Phase 8's editor (outline, canvas, history, badges, errors panel, the `JsonEditor` fallback, C141) moves into `apps/dashboard/src/components/graph-editor/` behind a `GraphAdapter` (node kinds and ports, `isValidConnection`, the condition scope's JSON Schema, the problem mapper, the palette, the diff renderer); `components/workflows/` keeps the workflow adapter with its existing tests green; `components/flow-builder/` adds the flow adapter.
2. **The outline first** (WCAG 2.1.1, 2.5.7, 2.5.8): an APG tree from `entry` along `next`, with each port's target as a child group, branch routes in order, and connectors shown as "go to <step>"; every canvas operation is reachable from it (add after, add route, move a route up or down, connect to a valid target, delete with confirmation, open settings); built and tested keyboard-only before the canvas.
3. **Palette:** v2's supported step types (no `form`, with the reason); each step's form generated from its zod schema (C153.1); the check panels beside each verification step.
4. **Modules** (public names, editor macros, not a runtime type; behaviour note F6): government ID, selfie, document, database, phone, email (each inserts its step, an optional explanation screen and an `exhausted` fallback branch), success and failed screens, and country select with branching (a screen with a `country_select` and a branch on it); a module the graph rules would refuse where the cursor is is not offered, with the reason (public).
5. **Screen editor:** a component library and a screen canvas with drag and drop and, for every drag, keyboard and single-pointer alternatives (move up, move down, into or out of a container); the component's Design (style overrides within the token types, with live contrast, C160.4), Data (field binding and validation) and Logic (visibility rules, written with the same condition builder over the screen's own fields) tabs (public tab names).
6. **Validation in place:** the shape, coherence, graph and field rules run in the browser on every edit from `@openverify/flow-spec` and `@openverify/contracts` (the code the API runs); server-only rules through `validate` on demand and before publish; problems by JSON Pointer to node badges (icon and word) and the errors panel, announced politely; drafts autosave with problems (C154.2) and `If-Match`; a 412 opens "reload the latest draft" with the server diff of what changed; publish lists every flagged change of the diff and every acknowledgement with its reason.
7. **Versions:** history; the diff of any two versions as a list (the accessible form) and as canvas badges; restore, copy, promote, "used by", the deployment history.
8. **Panels:** fields with their references (C149.6); theme picker (C160); locales and copy (C162, owner `dashboard-themes-copy`).
9. **Strings:** the editor's own strings and `@xyflow/react`'s `ariaLabelConfig` come from a typed English catalog (`apps/dashboard/src/lib/copy/`), so translating the console later is a data change (decision 33; the Phase 8 C141.3 carry).
10. **Tests:** Playwright keyboard-only authoring through the outline (a screen with two fields and a visibility rule; a branch on a signal with `onNull`; a verification step with an `exhausted` fallback; publish with acknowledgements); outline and canvas in sync; undo and redo; the 412 dialog; the diff list; axe on each builder state; the real-header CSP test (C170.3).
11. **Honesty:** the canvas never lets an author draw a graph the format cannot express (every connection runs the shared port rules); every fact a branch reads shows its tier, stage and source, every enabled locale its review status, and every module what it inserts before it is inserted.

### C156. Preview and simulate (F22, K4)

1. **Resolve:** `POST /inquiry-template-versions/:id/preview` with an optional unsaved body (`flow`, `copy`, `field-schemas`, `theme-set-id`, and unsaved theme `styles`, validated by C160.3's schema and the contrast gate, so the theme editor previews a change on real screens before it goes live; review H15) and `meta.locale` (an enabled locale or the pseudo-locales `en-XA`, `ar-XB`) answers a preview document: every renderable step's client view in C151.5's `step` form, the resolved theme, the copy for the locale and synthetic guidance values; never inquiry data; another environment's version is 404.
2. **The frame:** `<iframe src="{flow origin}/preview" title="Flow preview" sandbox="allow-scripts allow-same-origin" referrerpolicy="no-referrer">`. `allow-same-origin` only keeps the flow its own origin, which must differ from the console's; the console refuses to render the frame when the two origins are equal (a configuration error shown in the page). **Protocol** (versioned, ours): the console posts `{ source: "openverify-console", version: 1, type: "preview-document" | "select-step" | "set-outcome" | "set-viewport" | "set-scheme", … }` with `targetOrigin` set to the flow origin; the preview posts `{ source: "openverify-preview", version: 1, type: "ready" | "error" | "navigated", … }` to the exact console origin; each side checks `event.origin` and `event.source` (`window.parent`, the iframe's `contentWindow`), parses with zod, caps a message at 1 MiB and ignores anything else. **Assets** (reviews P17, H15, F09): the preview has no session and `connect-src 'none'`, so the console fetches the theme's logo and font through `GET /theme-sets/assets/:id/content` (C160.6) and posts them in a separate `preview-assets` message as transferred `ArrayBuffer`s (at most 1 MiB in all), which the preview sniffs again (PNG, JPEG or WebP; `wOF2`) before any `blob:` or `FontFace` use; the preview's policy is never relaxed for them.
3. **The preview entry** (`apps/flow/src/preview/`): renders with a fake client that makes no network call; capture steps show a still placeholder instead of the camera; viewport presets at 320, 375 and 768 CSS px; light, dark and automatic through the iframe's `color-scheme`; served with C157.1's preview headers (`connect-src 'none'`, `frame-ancestors` the console origin), so a preview cannot leak what it was given.
4. **Simulate** (public; behaviour note 1.10): `POST /inquiry-template-versions/:id/simulate` with a simulated scope (per verification `passed`, `failed` or `needs_review`; per check overrides; extracted values; fields; signals; list outcomes; device class; workflow outputs; `null` for any nullable fact) answers the path the server engine would take (`steps[]`, each route taken with its condition's result and any `onNull` used, waits, and the outcome C151.3 would reach), computed by the same `nextPosition` and the same `flowOutcome` (C151.2), with each simulated verification's status derived from the per-check overrides through the existing core decision function (required checks, ADR 0002 §3b), so simulate and the live flow cannot disagree on an outcome (review H16); `integration-9a` replays the 9A e2e inquiries through simulate and requires the same path and outcome. Simulated signals are the provisional snapshot values of C150.9, and only `flow-provisional` signals can be set (review H02). Presets: a passing and a failing preset per verification type from the synthetic fixtures; custom presets saved per user (`ssp_`, values sealed, labelled "synthetic values only").
5. **Both environments** (ours; the public preview is sandbox only): the preview creates no inquiry, calls no provider and holds synthetic values only, so it is safe in production, where promoted drafts live. A "test in sandbox" mode that runs a real sandbox inquiry against a draft is deferred (C177; decision 17).
6. **The workflow canvas overlay** (K4; Phase 8 decision 23): the workflow editor draws a sandbox test run's path (C120.7) over the read-only canvas and the outline, each step's state as an icon and a word (succeeded, failed, skipped, waiting, overridden), from the run's steps route.
7. **Honesty:** the preview says what differs from a live flow (no camera, no checks, simulated outcomes, and the server-side validations it does not run: patterns, field writes, consent digests; review H15); a simulated path is labelled as such and never stored as a run or a step record.

### C157. The flow server

1. **`apps/flow/server.ts`** (decision 18; ours; Node's `http` with erasable TypeScript, as `apps/dashboard/server.ts`; no new dependency): serves `dist/` (path traversal refused, known extensions only, `nosniff`, immutable caching for hashed assets, `no-store` for documents) and three document entries:
   - **hosted** (`/` and its routes): `Content-Security-Policy` = the flow policy of `security-meta.ts` as a header plus `frame-ancestors 'none'`; `Permissions-Policy: camera=(self), microphone=(), geolocation=()`; `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`; `X-Frame-Options: DENY` for browsers that ignore CSP (hosted only; review P31);
   - **preview** (`/preview`): `default-src 'none'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self'; img-src 'self' blob: data:; font-src 'self'; connect-src 'none'; frame-ancestors <console origin>; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'` and `Permissions-Policy: camera=(), microphone=(), geolocation=()`; the console origin comes from `OPENVERIFY_DASHBOARD_PUBLIC_URL`;
   - **embed** (`/embed/{inquiry id}`; the id is public and not a secret, and the session credentials stay in the fragment): the hosted policy with `frame-ancestors` set to the inquiry's embed policy (C158), or `'none'` when the list is empty, the inquiry is unknown or its template inactive (one answer for both, review F03), or the lookup fails (fail closed). The path id is checked with `isId('inq', …)` before anything else; a malformed id answers 404 with `'none'` and makes no lookup (reviews P05, P10). The id travels in the path only because the server must compute the header before any script runs; it is an identifier, not a credential, and the flow server's access log records the entry kind and the status, never the path.
2. **The lookup:** `GET /api/v1/internal/embed-policies/:inquiry-id` on the API (ours; outside the public OpenAPI document), authenticated by `OPENVERIFY_FLOW_SERVER_TOKEN` compared in constant time (with `OPENVERIFY_FLOW_SERVER_TOKEN_PREVIOUS` also accepted during a rotation, and a startup check of at least 32 random bytes; review P31), answering `{ inquiry-id, template-id, environment-id, frame-ancestors[] }`. The flow server builds the URL with `encodeURIComponent`, checks the answer with `parseEmbedPolicyAnswer`, a hand-written guard in the dependency-free `origin-pattern` module of C158.1 that a `packages/contracts` test holds to the frozen zod schema over the same golden vectors (the flow server imports no zod and no package index; verification round V04), and re-checks each origin against C158.1's grammar, dropping the whole answer to `'none'` on any failure, so a stored value can never inject a directive or a header (review P10). It caches each answer by **inquiry id** for at most 60 s (holding the template and environment for invalidation; at most 10,000 entries, least recently used first), and caches negative answers for 60 s, so an allowlist change reaches new loads within a minute. **Abuse** (reviews P05, F03): before any lookup the flow server applies an in-memory token bucket per client address on `/embed/*` (60 a minute, provisional; the address taken through `OPENVERIFY_FLOW_SERVER_TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS`, never a raw `X-Forwarded-For`) and answers 429 over it, so no client can drain the shared lookup budget; the API's `internal-embed-policy` policy becomes a capacity ceiling (C174.4). Tests: a burst of unknown ids from one client does not change another client's header; a malicious id and a malicious answer both end in `'none'`.
3. **Deployment:** the compose `app` profile's `flow` service runs `node --conditions=@openverify/source apps/flow/server.ts` (C158.1) instead of `vite preview`; development keeps `vite` and `vite preview` with Phase 7's fixed headers; an infra test asserts every entry's headers on the running container; the docs give the headers for operators who serve the build themselves and say that a static host cannot embed (it cannot compute the header). The `<meta>` CSP stays as defence in depth.
4. **Limits** (ADR 0008, docs): the allowlist does not stop a compromised allowed site, a top-level copy of the hosted page on a phishing domain, or a browser that ignores CSP; `frame-ancestors` is ignored in `<meta>` and overrides `X-Frame-Options`, so the header is the only control.

### C158. Allowed origins, embedding rules and redirects (S2, S3, F17)

1. **`allowed-embed-origins`** (decision 19; environment setting, `settings:write`, audited, ≤ 50): each entry `https://host[:port]` or `https://*.host[:port]` (CSP host-source); no path, query, fragment, userinfo, IP literal, bare `*` or `*` port; IDNs stored as `xn--`, lower case; production refuses `http:` and `localhost`; sandbox also accepts `http://localhost[:port]` and `http://127.0.0.1[:port]` (public: `localhost` in sandbox only). **Wildcards** (review P08): a wildcard needs at least two labels after `*.` and is refused for any suffix on our own short list of shared-hosting suffixes (`vercel.app`, `github.io`, `netlify.app`, `pages.dev`, `herokuapp.com`, `web.app`, `azurewebsites.net`, `cloudfront.net` and the public second-level country suffixes such as `co.uk`; documented as incomplete, since the Public Suffix List is MPL-2.0 and cannot run in the API under rule 4); a production wildcard needs `meta.acknowledge-wildcards: true` on the settings change, audited (otherwise 422 `embed_wildcard_unacknowledged`, V17). Invalid entries answer 422 `embed_origin_invalid` naming each. **One matcher:** `matchesOriginPattern(origin, entry)` in `packages/contracts` (a pure function with golden vectors, frozen in Stage 1a because three owners use it) implements CSP host-source matching (scheme match, a dot boundary at any depth, the scheme's default port), so `https://evilhost.com` never matches `https://*.host.com`; the settings validator, the flow server and the embed bridge all use it. It lives with the origin grammar and `parseEmbedPolicyAnswer` (C157.2) in `packages/contracts/src/origin-pattern.ts`, a module with **no import at all**, exported at the subpath `@openverify/contracts/origin-pattern` (`@openverify/source` → the `.ts` file, default → `dist`); `apps/flow/server.ts` imports only that subpath and is started with `node --conditions=@openverify/source` in development, tests and compose, so Node's type stripping runs it from source on a clean checkout, while the API and the bridge import it as usual (verification round V04: the package index uses `.js` specifiers and zod, which type stripping cannot load). The `--help` smoke test of Stage 1b (F) imports it; if Node refuses the workspace link, the fallback is that `server.ts` runs against built `dist` with `dependsOn: ["^build"]` on the flow's test task, a `contractDeviation` that `integration-9a` (which owns `turbo.json`) applies and records as a 9A amendment.
2. **Per template:** the version's `embed-origins`, a subset of the environment's list checked at publish (empty or absent: the environment's list). The public `embedded-flow-domain-allowlist` holds bare domains; ours holds origins, so scheme and port are explicit (documented mapping).
3. **Rules:** an inquiry embeds only while its template is `active` (else the flow shows the public `inactive_template` error); drafts never embed (inquiries pin published versions; the preview is the only draft surface); the embed bridge checks that the path's inquiry id equals the session's `inquiry.id` (the server's truth, not the fragment's, which the URL author also chose; review P10) and otherwise shows its link error.
4. **Redirects** (behaviour note F17): `redirect-uri` on `POST /inquiries` (public) and on the template version, checked against the environment's `allowed-redirect-origins` (default: the embed origins), which take C158.1's grammar and production rules (review P09): entries without a wildcard match by parsed scheme, host and port exactly (OWASP), wildcard entries through `matchesOriginPattern`; plus the version's `redirect-uri-schemes` for custom schemes (public `hosted-flow-redirect-uri-schemes`; empty means `https` only, and `http` for sandbox localhost), whose entries must match `^[a-z][a-z0-9+.-]*$` and are refused for `javascript`, `data`, `vbscript`, `file`, `blob`, `about`, `filesystem`, `intent`, `http` and `https`; userinfo refused; 422 `redirect_uri_not_allowed`. The redirect is checked again against the current lists whenever the session answers, so removing an origin also stops redirects for inquiries already created: on a mismatch the flow shows its final screen and does not navigate.
5. **On completion or failure** (decision 15) the session's `completion.redirect-url` is the URI with only `inquiry-id` appended, plus `status` when the version sets `redirect-include-status: true` (default `false`, because "this inquiry failed" in a landing page's query string reaches its logs, analytics scripts and later referrers; review P32) (ours: the public product also appends `reference-id` and `fields`, which would put personal data in URLs, server logs and referrers). The flow announces the outcome in a live region and shows a "Continue" button that performs the redirect; it never navigates on a timer, because an automatic redirect is a time limit the person cannot control (WCAG 2.2.1, failure F41; review H13), and a C171.4 test asserts it. The docs say `status` is not authoritative (read the inquiry or the webhook).
6. **CORS is unchanged:** the embedded flow is served from the flow origin and calls the API as the hosted flow does.
7. **Honesty:** the console says what an allowlist does and does not stop (C157.4); a redirect's `status` is labelled a hint wherever the docs show it.

### C159. The embed client `@openverify/js` (F26)

1. **Package:** `packages/sdk-js` (`@openverify/js`), no runtime dependency, ESM and IIFE builds through Vite's library mode, at most 6 KB gzipped (a size test), for the flow's supported browsers.
2. **API:** `OpenVerify.embed({ container, flowUrl, inquiryId, sessionToken | oneTimeLink, language?, styleVariant?: "light" | "dark", iframeTitle = "Identity verification", frameHeight?, frameWidth? (≤ 768 px, public), onLoad, onReady, onComplete({inquiryId, status}), onCancel({inquiryId}), onError({code, message}), onEvent({name, metadata}), eventsAllowlist? }) → { destroy() }`. It builds `<iframe src="{flowUrl}/embed/{inquiryId}#…" allow="camera" title=… referrerpolicy="no-referrer" sandbox="allow-scripts allow-same-origin allow-forms allow-popups">` (public: the first three sandbox tokens cannot be removed; `allow-forms` is a default) and puts the credentials only in the fragment (`language` and `styleVariant`, which are not secrets, go in the query as the hosted flow's `language` and `style-variant`, C160.9).
3. **Not offered** (ours, each with its reason in the docs): starting from a template id, `fields`, `referenceId`, `accountId` and `environmentId` (the customer's server creates the inquiry, C149.4; the same rule the Android note keeps, M6, and Phase 11's Connect relies on); `frameAncestors` and `messageTargetOrigin` for nested frames (later).
4. **Protocol** (versioned, ours): on load the client posts `{ source: "openverify-parent", version: 1, type: "hello" }` to the iframe with the flow origin as target; the flow accepts it only from `window.parent` and only when `event.origin` matches an entry of `session.embed.allowed-origins` through `matchesOriginPattern` (C158.1; review P08), cross-checked with `location.ancestorOrigins` where present and never gated on it (a value of `"null"`, which `referrerpolicy="no-referrer"` produces, counts as absent; review P31), and from then on posts only to that exact origin: `ready`, `event` (`start`, `step-changed` with the step **type** only, `complete`, `cancel`, `error`), `resize {height}`. No message carries PII, a token, a step id or a field value; the client accepts a message only when `event.origin` is the flow origin and `event.source` its iframe.
5. **Errors** (public codes): `application_error`, `camera_error`, `invalid_config`, `unauthenticated`, `inactive_template`, `unknown`.
6. **Not authoritative** (public guidance, our words): `onComplete` may never fire, and its status is a hint; business logic belongs on webhooks or the API.
7. **Release:** built and tested in Phase 9; published to npm with the release (Phase 11); `sdk-react` and `apps/demo-web` are deferred (C177, decision 20); the e2e embeds from a fixture page the e2e stack serves.

### C160. Theme sets (T1–T8, T15)

1. **Resource** (public; decision 29 for the scopes): `theme-set`, id `theset`, attributes `name` (1–100), `status` (`active`, `archived`), `styles`, `created-at`, `updated-at`, `archived-at`; ours `revision` and `is-default`. Routes (public paths): `GET /theme-sets` (newest first, `filter[status]`), `POST /theme-sets`, `GET /theme-sets/:id`, `PATCH /theme-sets/:id` (an omitted attribute is unchanged; `styles`, when sent, replaces the whole tree, as public), `DELETE /theme-sets/:id` (archives, as public); ours `GET /theme-sets/:id/revisions` and `POST /theme-sets/:id/restore` (`meta.revision`). Scopes `theme-sets:read` and `theme-sets:write` (ours, for the public `theme_set.read` and `theme_set.write`).
2. **Environment-scoped** (decision 24; ours: the public sets are organisation-level): a theme changes how production flows look, so a sandbox key or an operator who holds only sandbox roles must never change it; promote copies by name (8).
3. **Styles** (ours; the public cell list is not documented, behaviour note 2.1):

   ```json
   {
     "settings": { "use-dark-theme": true, "preferred-theme-variant": "light" },
     "light": { "palette": { "primary": "#1f4fd1", "primary-hover": "#1a43b3", "on-primary": "#ffffff", "surface": "#ffffff",
                             "canvas": "#f6f7f9", "ink": "#14171f", "ink-muted": "#4b5262", "line": "#d5d9e0",
                             "control": "#6b7280", "focus": "#1f4fd1", "danger": "#b42318", "danger-hover": "#9a1e14" } },
     "dark": { "palette": { "…": "the same twelve tokens" } },
     "shape": { "radius": 8 },
     "typography": { "font": { "kind": "system" } },
     "branding": { "logo": { "asset-id": "tsa_…", "alt-key": "brand.logo.alt", "decorative": false }, "logo-placement": "header" }
   }
   ```

   The palette's closed token set is exactly the built-in stylesheet's twelve colour tokens (`packages/ui/src/styles.css`: `primary`, `primary-hover`, `on-primary`, `danger`, `danger-hover`, `surface`, `canvas`, `ink`, `ink-muted`, `line`, `control`, `focus`), so a themed flow never keeps a built-in hover or separator colour against an operator's palette; the first draft's `on-danger` is dropped, because the danger button renders `on-primary` on `danger` (`packages/ui/src/button.tsx:18`; review F13). `settings` keeps the public member names. Colours are `^#[0-9a-f]{6}$` only (no names, alpha, `url()` or `var()`), so a value copied into a custom property can never make a request or inject a rule; `radius` is 0–24 CSS px; `font` is `{kind: "system"}` or `{kind: "uploaded", "asset-id"}`. The tree is sparse: an absent cell takes the documented default (the built-in tokens). An unknown or mistyped cell answers 400 with JSON Pointers (public); a contradiction answers 422 (`dark` absent while `use-dark-theme` is true, a contrast failure, an archived or foreign asset; public 422 behaviour).
4. **The contrast gate** (behaviour note T3; decision 26; `packages/core/src/contrast/`, pure; the console runs it live, the API on every save): WCAG 2.2's relative luminance (8-bit sRGB, the 0.04045 threshold, 0.2126, 0.7152 and 0.0722) and `(L1 + 0.05) / (L2 + 0.05)`, never rounded, so 4.499 fails 4.5. Pairs, in each palette: `ink`, `ink-muted` and `danger` on `surface` and on `canvas` ≥ 4.5; `on-primary` on `primary`, `primary-hover`, `danger` and `danger-hover` ≥ 4.5 (review F13); `line` is decorative only (separators; form controls are outlined with `control`) and has no pair, which the editor says; `primary` on `surface` ≥ 4.5 (links are underlined, so 1.4.1 never rests on colour); `control` on `surface` and `canvas` ≥ 3; `focus` on `surface`, `canvas` and `primary` ≥ 3; the 7:1 pairs warn only. Failures answer 422 `theme_contrast_insufficient` with `pairs[] {pointer, foreground, background, ratio, required}`. Vectors: `#000000` on `#ffffff` 21.000, `#767676` 4.542, `#949494` 3.033 (engineering note [B2]). The built-in tokens are **stored as hex** in `packages/ui/src/styles.css` (converted once from today's `oklch()` values and recorded; review H29: two OKLab conversions disagree near a threshold, so a test on our own conversion would prove the conversion, not what browsers render), the form operators use, and their pairs are asserted by the same function, replacing the stylesheet's comment. A **built-in dark palette** is added beside the light one and passes the same gate, so dark mode is defined with no theme set (review F13); both palettes' pairs are tested. What it does not cover, said in the editor and the docs: text over images, the logo's own contrast, thin fonts and anti-aliasing, forced colours, the embedding page around the frame, and operator copy.
5. **Live and audited** (decision 25; public: set edits apply live): a saved change reaches every flow that resolves to the set at its next session load, after the gate; each save is an immutable revision kept for audit and restore; every change writes an audit entry naming the revision; the session response names the revision it rendered.
6. **Assets** (`theme_assets`, `tsa_`, environment-scoped): `POST /theme-sets/assets` (multipart, `theme-sets:write`): a `logo` is PNG, JPEG or WebP by its bytes, ≤ 256 KiB and ≤ 2,048 × 2,048 px, re-encoded through the vision rendition endpoint (Phase 8 C135.3) **synchronously**, so the upload answers only once the rendition is stored and no asset is ever pending (review F27; no queue), and no metadata reaches a browser, and stored through `FileStorage` like every file (envelope-encrypted, rewrap-listed); a `font` is WOFF2 (`wOF2`), ≤ 512 KiB, uploaded with `meta.licence-attested: true`, recorded with the user and the time (the operator confirms a licence that allows web embedding; OpenVerify ships none, decision 27); SVG answers 415 `asset_type_refused` (decision 28). Operators read an asset's rendition with `GET /theme-sets/assets/:id/content` (`theme-sets:read`, same environment, `nosniff`, `Content-Disposition: attachment`, `private, no-store`; review P17), which the console's theme editor and the preview use. Flows fetch assets with `GET /api/v1/flow/theme-assets/:id` (session token, same environment, `private, max-age=3600`), show a logo from a `blob:` URL (the flow's `img-src` already allows `blob:`) and load a font with `FontFace` from bytes, the system stack as fallback and the font's `unicode-range` when given, so Arabic never renders in a Latin-only face. When a logo is decorative, the organisation's name appears as text (Phase 7's `organization-display-name`).
7. **Resolution** (public): an inquiry's `theme-set-id` (`POST /inquiries`, public), else its version's `theme-set-id` (an active set of the environment, checked at publish), else the environment's `default-theme-set-id`, else the built-in tokens. Archiving detaches (public): flows resolving to an archived set fall back to the default, and published versions are not rewritten; archiving the default answers 409 `theme_set_default` (public). Per-component overrides (C148, behaviour note T8) stay within the token types and pass the gate against their screen's surface. The session response carries `theme {theme-set-id, revision, mode, light, dark, radius, font {kind, asset-id?, platforms}, logo {asset-id, alt-key, decorative}, placement}` (T15), the contract Phase 10 renders (C175.1). `font.platforms` is `["web"]` for an uploaded font: browsers pass font bytes through the OpenType Sanitizer before parsing, Android's `Typeface` does not and does not load WOFF2, so native clients use the system font unless Phase 10 adds a validated font path (review P18; C175.1, C177).
8. **Promote:** a promoted version's `theme-set-id` is re-resolved by name in the target environment; with no match, the promote copies the set and its assets there, only when the caller holds `theme-sets:write` in the target environment (otherwise `promote_unresolved` names the theme reference; review P19, with a refusal test), and says so in its answer and audit entry.
9. **In the flow** (`apps/flow/src/theme/`): tokens applied with `document.documentElement.style.setProperty("--color-…", value)`, never `cssText`; `color-scheme` set from the mode; `prefers-color-scheme` followed with the preferred variant (public); `style-variant` (`light`, `dark`; public) from the hosted URL or the embed option, which `@openverify/js` passes as the same `style-variant` query parameter, so `flow-renderer` parses one parameter in `src/session/` in 9A and passes it to `applySessionTheme(session, styleVariant)`, and `api-themes` applies it in 9B (verification round V15); a Playwright spec (`theme-csp.spec.ts`) in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit fails on any `securitypolicyviolation`, and if one engine refuses `setProperty`, the fallback is a same-origin stylesheet the flow server builds, never an `unsafe-*` source. Where it runs (review F19): CI's mocked flow suite runs inside the `e2e` job with Chromium only, so Firefox and WebKit projects are added for this one spec, in the `e2e` job with the browser install cached; if they cost more than 3 minutes, a `schedule:` trigger in `ci.yml` runs them instead of every push. This machine has Chromium only, and WebKit's system dependencies need sudo, so local validation runs the spec in Chromium and leaves the other two engines to CI unless the owner installs them (an owner step), as instrumented Android tests already are. Built in 9B by `api-themes` (Workflow 9B).
10. **Honesty:** the gate proves the palette's pairs, not a screen; the editor says so, and the audit checks screens (C171).

### C161. Locales, right-to-left and the built-in translations (T9, T10, T13)

1. **Built-in locales** (decision 30): `en` (source), `es`, `fr`, `de`, `ar` in `apps/flow/src/i18n/locales/` and every `steps/*/locales/` folder, typed against `Messages`, so a missing key fails the type check; `SUPPORTED_LANGUAGES` gains `fr`, `de` and `ar`.
2. **Choice** (public mechanism): the `language` parameter (public; `lang` kept as an alias; in the fragment or the query), the embed option, the person's selector (offered only among the version's `enabled-locales`, on every screen's footer; ours, where the public selector is on the first screen, so a person can change language at any step; review H25), then `navigator.languages`; matched by RFC 4647 lookup on the base language; fallback to the version's `default-locale`, then `en`. `enabled-locales` and `default-locale` hold base language tags only (ours; the public values are region tags such as `ar-EG`): a region tag answers 422 `locale_unsupported` naming it (review H25). Catalogs are chosen by base tag and `Intl` formats with the full detected tag, so `ar-EG` shows Arabic-Indic digits and plain `ar` Latin ones, as CLDR has it.
3. **Right-to-left** (ours; behaviour note T13, not documented publicly): `<html lang dir>` from the locale; logical properties only, enforced by a lint rule in `apps/flow` and `packages/ui` that refuses `ml-`, `mr-`, `pl-`, `pr-`, `left-`, `right-`, `text-left` and `text-right`; every interpolated value inside `<bdi>`; directional icons (back, next, chevrons) mirrored with `rtl:-scale-x-100`, and never the selfie pose prompts ("turn left" means the person's own left in every language), the camera, check marks, document outlines or logos, each with a test.
4. **Digits:** Arabic-Indic (U+0660–U+0669) and Extended Arabic-Indic (U+06F0–U+06F9) digits typed into code, phone, date, number and ID fields are normalised to ASCII before validation and submission, in the client and again on the server (`normalizeDigits`, `packages/flow-spec`), so a person on an Arabic keyboard can enter their code.
5. **Review status** (decisions 31 and 67; review H14): `LOCALE_REVIEW_STATUS` in `@openverify/flow-spec` records each built-in locale as `source` (`en`), `machine-drafted` (written by the build agents, which is machine translation, with no review), `partially-reviewed` (listing the keys not yet reviewed), or `reviewed {by, date, catalog-sha256}`. A test computes each catalog's per-key digests and downgrades a `reviewed` locale to `partially-reviewed`, listing the keys, whenever a key was added or changed after the review, so a later key is never shown as reviewed. `es`, `fr`, `de` and `ar` ship `machine-drafted` unless Stage 1a finds a recorded human review. Publishing a version whose `enabled-locales` include a locale that is not `reviewed` and whose built-in `legal` texts (C162.2: consent, notice, decision and contest, retention) are shown needs the acknowledgement `locale-unreviewed:<locale>` (production; sandbox warns); the console and `docs/i18n/README.md` show the status; no text calls a translation reviewed without a record.
6. **Plurals:** a test asserts that every plural message defines each category `new Intl.PluralRules(locale).resolvedOptions().pluralCategories` names (Arabic six; French and Spanish `many` unless the message's range cannot reach a million, noted beside the key); no gendered messages (the flow knows nothing reliable about a person's gender).
7. **Pseudo-locales** (decision 35): `en-XA` (accented, lengthened, bracketed) and `ar-XB` (reversed, right-to-left) generated from `en` at test time and in the preview by our own transform, a pure function in `packages/flow-spec/src/pseudo-locale.ts` (owned by `flow-i18n`) that `api-templates` calls in the preview's resolve (C156.1), so the copy arrives already transformed; a bundle test asserts that it is absent from the hosted flow's bundle (review H26); a Playwright pass renders every step in both at 320 CSS px and fails on horizontal scrolling, clipped text and unbracketed visible text.
8. **For Phase 10:** `apps/flow/scripts/export-flow-locales.ts` (under the flow app, which has `tsx` and the catalogs' import style; review F23) writes one JSON per locale from the TypeScript catalogs to `contracts/fixtures/i18n/<locale>.json`, the source of Phase 10's `values-fr`, `values-de` and `values-ar` (C175.1), and appends the released consent texts, unfilled, to `consent-texts.json` (C162.9; verification round V01); a test asserts that the committed JSON matches the catalogs.
9. **The console stays English** (decision 33).
10. **Honesty:** what the flow shows in a locale is either reviewed or marked with its status in the console and docs; the built-in non-English catalogs are machine-drafted and say so; no machine translation runs at run time or on operator copy (C177; review H14).

### C162. Operator copy and the translations API (T11)

1. **`copy`** on the template version: `{ messages: { "<locale>": { "<key>": "<text>" } } }`, with `enabled-locales` and `default-locale` (C154.1); pinned and diffed with the version, immutable once published.
2. **Customisable keys** (`COPY_KEYS` in `@openverify/flow-spec`): every built-in key an operator may override, each with `maxLength`, its allowed placeholders (a closed list per key, such as `{organization}`, `{days}`, `{attempts}`), `consent`, `multiline` and `legal` (`consent`, `notice`, `decision`, `retention` or `null`: the texts with legal weight, such as the consent texts, Phase 7's `notice.*` keys, the contest-route and decision text of C150.7, and the face-template retention text; reviews P26, H14); and component text attributes `<step id>.<component id>.<attribute>` (`label`, `hint`, `title`, `body`, `button`, `alt`, `option.<option key>`), where an option is keyed by its generated `key` (`^[a-z0-9_]{1,40}$`; v1 options migrate to `o1`, `o2`, …), never by its value, and component copy keys have their own schema of at most 255 characters with a test at the maximum lengths (review F28). Unknown keys, unknown placeholders, empty strings and over-long strings answer 422 with JSON Pointers. **Legal keys** (P26): an override of a biometric consent text must contain `{days}`, which the server fills from the configured face-template retention, so the text cannot state another period (`consent_copy_missing_placeholder`); overriding any `legal` key in production needs the acknowledgement `legal-copy:<key>` (sandbox warns).
3. **Plain text only** (ours; decision 32): no HTML, Markdown, links or ICU `plural` and `select`; one placeholder form, `{name}`, which both clients fill in a few lines; NFC-normalised on save; control characters other than line feed refused; the bidi embedding and override controls U+202A–U+202E refused (text that reads differently from how it is stored), the isolates U+2066–U+2069 allowed only when each initiator is closed in the same text; format characters (category Cf: U+200B–U+200F, U+2060, U+FEFF, the tag block U+E0000–U+E007F) refused except LRM and RLM (U+200E, U+200F), and refused entirely in `legal` keys (review P30, the Trojan Source class); rendered as React text with `white-space: pre-line` and, in Phase 10, as Compose `Text`. Links are typed `https:` URL fields on the version (`help-url`, `privacy-url`) with their own copy keys, rendered in one fixed slot after every step's content (3.2.6).
4. **Consent copy** (ours): a custom consent text must be given in every enabled locale or the version is not published (`consent_copy_incomplete`), since a fallback to built-in text in another locale would record consent to words the operator did not write; the diff flags any consent text change. The consent record names the version, the `text-keys` and the `locale`; with the digests of 9 it identifies the exact words shown without storing them again (reviews P04, H10: without them it does not, because built-in texts change between releases and differ between clients).
5. **Fallbacks for other copy:** the person's locale's custom text, else its built-in text, else the default locale's custom text, else its built-in text; never a raw key (`translateKey` returns `null` today).
6. **Delivery:** the session response carries `copy` for the version's enabled locales (a few kilobytes), so changing language needs no request. It carries only the built-in-key overrides and the component texts of the **current** step, never another step's component keys or texts, so the copy of a screen on another path (and the step ids in its keys) never reaches the person (review P14).
7. **Translations** (public, behaviour note 2.4): `GET /inquiry-templates/:id/translations` answers `inquiry-template-translations` with `translations[] {step, step-display-name, component, attribute-name, locale-values[] {locale, value}}` from the latest draft, else the latest published version; `POST` imports rows partially into the draft (creating one from the latest published version when none exists), never publishes, and a blank cell never deletes (public); `inquiry-templates:read` and `:write`. Ours: `GET` and `POST …/translations.csv` in the same shape (`step,component,attribute,<locale>…`), written by Phase 7's formula-neutralising writer and read by Phase 8's `readCsv` (≤ 2 MiB, ≤ 20,000 rows); import problems name the row and column and never echo a value.
8. **Not built:** machine translation (behaviour note T12; decision 34; C177).
9. **Consent evidence** (reviews P04, H10; a C3 amendment): the consent body gains `text-sha256[]`, the SHA-256 of each NFC-normalised text exactly as rendered (placeholders filled), `copy-source` (`custom` or `built-in`) and the client's `catalog-version`. The server computes the expected digest itself (verification round V01: a rendered text with a placeholder, such as today's `consent.biometric-matching` with `{{days}}`, `apps/flow/src/i18n/locales/en.ts:23-25`, can never be in a table of rendered digests): for each recorded key it takes the stored **template text**, which is the version's custom copy for that key and locale or, for a built-in text, the entry for that key and locale of each released catalog in `contracts/fixtures/i18n/consent-texts.json` (an append-only table of the unfilled consent texts per catalog version, client, key and locale, exported with the locale JSON, C161.8; a test refuses removing an entry), fills its placeholders in their own syntax (`{{days}}` in the built-in catalogs, `{days}` in operator copy) with the session's values (`days` from `face-template-retention-days`, `organization` from `organization-display-name`), NFC-normalises, hashes and compares; a mismatch with every candidate answers 422 `consent_text_mismatch`. Tests: `consent.biometric-matching` accepted at two retention values on the web flow, and a custom biometric override with `{days}`. The record stores the digests, the copy source and the client (`platform`, SDK or flow version). Clients that send no digests (today's Android SDK) are recorded with `text-sha256: null` and their client, never as matched. A version whose consent step uses custom consent copy serves that step as unsupported to clients declaring only spec 1 (C151.6, C151.7), so today's SDK never records consent to text it did not show. Phase 10 sends the digests (C175.1).

### C163. Step records and rollups (A2, A13)

1. **Step records:** C151.1's `inquiry_flow_steps`, pseudonymous (C151.1), deleted with the inquiry's redaction and by its retention (decision 38). Step-derived metrics start at the environment's first step record: earlier buckets show "no step data", never 0 (review H17).
2. **Reporting time zone** (decision 36; engineering note 6.3): the environment setting `analytics-time-zone` (an IANA name the runtime knows; default `UTC`); daily buckets are computed in it with `date_trunc('day', ts, zone)`, so a 23- or 25-hour day is right; every chart and answer names it; changing it queues `analytics-rebuild` over the **open** days only (4), because the base facts of closed days have been partly redacted; every bucket carries the `time-zone` it was computed in, and the console marks a range that mixes zones (review H04).
3. **Tables** (counts only, environment-scoped; Stage 1a fixes the exact columns from these; no id of an end user and no value, with one exception: `analytics_case_assignee_daily` is keyed by an operator's `user_id`, so it is **personal data about employees**, deleted or re-keyed to "former user" when the user is deleted, kept for `analytics-assignee-retention-months` (default 13), and covered by an employee-notice paragraph in `docs/analytics/README.md`; review P20):
   - `analytics_inquiry_daily (environment_id, day, inquiry_template_id, inquiry_template_version_id, platform, device_kind, created, started, interacted, finished, completed, completed_verified, completed_with_fallback, completed_unverified, failed, expired_before_start, expired_after_start, approved, declined, needs_review, marked_for_review, decided_automatic, decided_manual, decided_not_declared, finish_seconds_histogram int[])` and `analytics_inquiry_tag_daily (…, tag, created, finished, completed, failed)`: `platform` and `device_kind` are the inquiry's `first_platform` and `first_device_kind` (C151.1; reviews H05, F14); the tag dimension is keyed by the tag's name, since inquiry tags are a `text[]` of names with no id (review F14; tags are operator labels, already served unmasked on inquiries);
   - `analytics_step_daily (…, inquiry_template_version_id, step_id, entered, exited, revisits)`, `analytics_transition_daily (…, from_step_id, to_step_id, count)` and `analytics_path_daily (…, inquiry_template_version_id, path_sha256, step_ids[], count)` for the per-path view (review H17);
   - `analytics_verification_daily (…, verification_type, capture_method, country, created, submitted, passed, failed, requires_retry, skipped, needs_review, canceled)` and `analytics_verification_step_daily (…, step_id, entered, passed, failed_after_attempts, needs_review, skipped)` for the per-person rate (review H06); `capture_method` comes from a new `files.capture_method` column (C153.2; review F14);
   - `analytics_check_daily (…, verification_type, check_name, requirement, status, count)`, where `status` is `passed`, `failed`, `not_applicable`, `errored` (a `not_applicable` result with a reason in `CHECK_ERROR_REASONS`) or `not_configured` (`provider_not_configured`), so an outage never counts as "not applicable" (review H03), and `analytics_check_reason_daily (…, check_name, reason, count)`;
   - `analytics_case_daily (…, case_template_id, created, resolved, sla_met, sla_missed, resolution_seconds_histogram int[])` and `analytics_case_assignee_daily (…, user_id, assigned, resolved)`;
   - `analytics_signal_daily (…, signal_name, bin, count)`.

   An inquiry counts on its creation day in the reporting zone (a cohort), and its later events update that day while it is open (4); its verifications and checks count on the same cohort day, cases on the case's own creation day (review H05).
4. **Refresh** (decisions 36 and 72; engineering note 6.2: rollups read in about 0.1 s where checks on the fly take 2–4 s at 12 million rows): the repeatable `analytics-rollup` job every 5 minutes recomputes whole **dirty days**, the local days of inquiries, verifications, checks and cases updated since the watermark (through `updated_at` indexes, added where missing), each day deleted and inserted again in one transaction, so a rerun is harmless; the watermark trails `now()` by 2 minutes, since timestamps are not commit-ordered. **Open and closed days** (review H04): a day is recomputed only while it is **open**, for `analytics-settle-days` after it (default 30, 1–90); after that it is frozen. **Redaction while a day is open** (verification round, H04: `POST /inquiries/:id/redact` redacts on demand in any status under `inquiries:redact`, and the retention pass applies a template version's own `settings.retention-days` before the environment's, `services/retention.ts:3-6`, so no setting can keep redaction away from open days): every rollup table gains `source` (`facts` or `redacted`); the redaction transaction, before it clears anything, computes the inquiry's contribution with the same rollup SQL restricted to that inquiry and adds it to its cohort day's `source = redacted` rows (counts only, no inquiry id), through `recordRedactedContributionInTx` (stub in `services/analytics/redaction.ts`, `api-analytics`, called from `services/redaction.ts` by `foundation-9b` (A)); a recompute deletes and inserts only `source = facts` rows, from unredacted inquiries, and readers add both, so redaction may move `updated_at` and mark a day dirty and still changes no count; a redacted inquiry's contribution is frozen at redaction. A status change after a day has closed (a review decided weeks later) is not reflected in that day, and the console and docs state the settle window. Testcontainers tests: redacting an inquiry of an open day through the route, and through a template version's own retention, leaves every count unchanged after the next recompute; redacting one of a closed day leaves every count unchanged; a zone change never relabels a bucket it did not rebuild (redacted contributions keep the zone they were computed in, which the mixed-zone mark of 2 shows). **Open past days** (verification round, P21): their outcome cells change while outcomes are decided, so two polls minutes apart could show one person's outcome; the 5-minute runs therefore refresh only `created` and `started` of open days, and the outcome columns of open days are recomputed once a local day, in the first run after the reporting zone's midnight (served with `freshness.outcomes-as-of`), so a cell's outcome change is visible at most once a day and across the day's whole population; the day-to-day difference that remains is a stated limit (C164.7). **The current day** (reviews H28, P21) is computed by running the same rollup SQL for that one day without storing it, marked `live: true`, and shows only `created` and `started` until it closes: no outcome breakdown (passed, failed, declined, approved, needs review, check failures) is served live, so two snapshots of one cell cannot be differenced into one person's outcome; a test compares the live and the rolled-up result for a closed day. Every answer carries `freshness.rolled-up-through`.
5. **Durations:** 40 log-spaced buckets from 1 s to 30 days; medians and percentiles are reported as their bucket's bounds ("between 4 and 6 minutes") and labelled approximate.
6. **Retention** (decision 38): `analytics-retention-months` (default 13, 13–60), enforced by the C16 pass; rollups are deleted with their environment, and the rollups of inquiries survive inquiry redaction because they hold no end user's data (C163.4's redacted contributions keep redaction from shrinking them, on open and closed days alike; review H04, verification round); the assignee rollup follows 3's own rule (review P20).
7. **Benchmark:** a non-gated `apps/api/scripts/analytics-benchmark.ts` on the pinned image re-measures build, refresh and read times at 1 M inquiries and 12 M checks; the result goes into `docs/PROGRESS.md`.

### C164. Analytics: metrics, suppression, the API and the console (A1–A4, A6, A7, A12, K7, K8)

1. **Definitions** (ours, written in `docs/analytics/README.md` and the console's help; the public product does not say how it measures, behaviour note 3.5): created; started (the server served the first step, `pending`); interacted (the first step was exited); finished (the flow reached `completed` or `failed`; an expired inquiry is not finished); success rate (finished inquiries now `completed` or `approved`, over finished), always shown split into completed with every verification on the path passed, completed through a fallback, and completed with no verification (C151.13; review H09); rejected rate (now `failed` or `declined`, over finished); needs-review rate; expiration rate (`expired_after_start` over started; inquiries that expired before starting are shown as their own count, so the rate can never pass 100%; review H05); time to finish (bucketed median); decided automatically, manually or not declared (from Phase 8's decision records, where an API decision's `solely-automated` defaults to "not declared"; else the deciding actor's type; review H05). A golden test per definition on the seeded population. Counts are of inquiries, never sessions: a resumed inquiry or a handoff counts once. The public statement that start equals finish plus expiry holds only once every started inquiry has settled, and the console says so.
2. **Funnel** (public, behaviour note 3.1; decision 39): per template version and step, inquiries that entered (first entry), exited and revisited; per transition, the count; overall (share of started) and per step modes, the per-step share being the inquiries that entered the step over the sum of the transitions into it, since a step in a graph can have several predecessors (review H17); version comparison; the most frequent step sequences (top 20) from `analytics_path_daily` as the per-path view; person-visible steps are the funnel's nodes, and branch and action steps are drawn as edges, not screens; computed from the server's step record, never from client-reported screen views, which could not be verified.
3. **Verifications** (A3): created, submitted, passed, failed, requires retry, skipped, needs review and canceled per type and template, grouped by capture method (client-reported, labelled, C153.2) and by the government ID's country. Two rates, each labelled with its unit and denominator (review H06): **per attempt**, `passed / (passed + failed + requires_retry)` (every attempt is its own verification row); **per person and step**, the outcome of the step's last attempt (passed, failed after all attempts, needs review, skipped) over the inquiries that entered the step; the counts of every other status always beside both.
4. **Checks** (A4, ours: no public check-failure view): per check, runs, passed, failed, not applicable, errored (by reason), not configured and pending, each its own column (review H03); its share of failed verifications; required versus advisory; the reasons distribution; the check's tier and stage with the `beta` badge; a check that never ran in the window shows "not run", never zero failures.
5. **Cases** (A6, K7; decision 41): created, resolved, resolution-time percentiles (bucketed), SLA met and missed, assignment distribution per reviewer (shown only to readers who also hold `users:write`, which the built-in viewer does not, never exported with names; reviewers are people too; review P20: `users:read` is in the viewer role); no availability metrics (decision 58).
6. **Signals** (A7, K8): per signal, the distribution of values in fixed bins from Phase 7's registry over a period, with tier and stage; `null` counted as "not evaluated".
7. **Suppression** (decision 37; engineering note 6.4): counts from 1 to the threshold minus one show as "fewer than <threshold>" (zero is shown; review H18); a rate is shown only when its denominator, its numerator and its complement each reach the threshold, and otherwise as a bound computed from the threshold and the denominator and rounded outward to a whole percent (with a numerator below threshold *t* and denominator *D*, "under ⌈100·(t−1)/D⌉%"), or not at all, with a property test that the true value lies inside every displayed bound (review H18); **complementary suppression**: when a total and all but one of its parts would be visible, the next smallest part is suppressed too, so no hidden cell follows by subtraction, and it is applied along the time axis too (days within a week, weeks and days within a month), identically whatever `bucket` is asked, so a week total and its visible days never reveal a hidden day (review P21; a property test across `day` and `week` answers); applied after every filter, identically in the console, the API, CSV downloads and the chart captions, which are built only from the suppressed answer (review H18); the threshold is `analytics-suppression-threshold` (floor 11, at most 100; never lowered below 11); windows are whole local days. What it does not cover, said in the console and docs: differencing two overlapping windows, or a filtered answer against an unfiltered one (C177); snapshot differencing, that is, an open day's outcome cells read on two consecutive days, whose difference is the outcomes decided in between (C163.4 limits it to once a day; verification round, P21); readers who can also list inquiries; and a total combined with outside knowledge.
8. **The API** (A12, ours: the public product has none): `GET /api/v1/analytics/{inquiries,funnel,verifications,checks,cases,signals}` with `filter[from]`, `filter[to]` (local dates, ≤ 400 days), `filter[inquiry-template-id]`, `filter[inquiry-template-version-id]`, `filter[platform]`, `filter[device-kind]` (C150.2; review H27), `filter[tag]` (inquiries; a tag name, review F14), `filter[case-template-id]` and `bucket` (`day`, `week`, `month`); the answer `{ data: { type: "analytics-<name>", id: <hash of the query>, attributes: { time-zone, from, to, buckets[], totals, suppressed-cells, threshold, freshness } } }`; scope `analytics:read`; rate limit `analytics`. Always one environment: sandbox and production never mix (A13).
9. **The console** (decision 42): an Analytics section (Overview, Funnel, Verifications, Checks, Cases, Signals) with `@visx` primitives rendered client-only; every chart a `figure` whose caption states the finding in words ("pass rate 91% over 90 days, down 3 points"), generated from the suppressed answer only (7), an SVG `role="img"` with a name, and an adjacent table that is the default for keyboard and screen-reader users; series told apart by direct labels or patterns as well as colour; bars and lines at 3:1 against their background; nothing shown on hover only; the suppression marks in both views.
10. **Not built** (decision 40): forwarding flow events to a third party (A5: the embed's `onEvent` lets a host do it, and the docs warn that event metadata can carry ids); the SQL explorer (A11: the docs describe a read-only replica role instead); the session map (A10; decision 60; C177).

### C165. Saved views and filters (A8, S7, K10)

1. **`saved-views`** (decision 44; `svw_`; dashboard API, users only): owner, environment, `list` (`inquiries`, `accounts`, `reports`, `cases`, `transactions`, `api-logs`, `webhook-deliveries`, `events`, `audit-logs`), `name`, `filters` (validated by that list route's query schema), `columns`, `sort`, `shared` (visible to every user of the environment who can read the list; ours: the public rule is not documented); `GET`, `POST /dashboard/saved-views`, `PATCH`, `DELETE /dashboard/saved-views/:id` (owner only; a shared view is copied to be changed).
2. **No personal data in a view:** a filter key that the list marks as PII (`reference-id`, names, emails, IP addresses, field values) is refused in a saved view (`saved_view_pii_filter`), so a stored view never holds a person's data.
3. **New filters:** `GET /inquiries` gains `filter[list-match]` (`matched`, `not-matched`, `unscreened`, over Phase 7's screenings) and `filter[list-id]` (K10), `filter[check]` with `filter[check-status]` (the public inquiry-list filter by a check's outcome) and `filter[next-step-name]` (with PII visibility only, because the step reached can reveal a masked value, C151.8; review P15); `GET /transactions` gains `filter[fields.<key>]` for fields a transaction type version marks `filterable` (K10): enum, boolean, number and date fields through a plaintext projection column, string fields through an HMAC equality index under the environment's blind-index subkey with a label per type and key (`identifierKey`), which needs PII visibility (the Phase 8 C137.2 rule: an equality query is otherwise a membership oracle).
4. **Delivery search** (S7): `POST /webhook-deliveries/search` (ours, with the public `webhook-events/search` grammar: `and`, `or`, `not` over `updated_at`, `status`, `webhook_id`, `event_name`; depth ≤ 2, ≤ 20 clauses), `webhooks:read`.
5. **Honesty:** a shared view shows each reader only what that reader's own permissions and masking allow; a view whose filter a list no longer accepts says so instead of silently widening.

### C166. Exports and bulk report creation (A9, K9, K13)

1. **`exports`** (decision 43; `exp_`): `POST /exports` with `object-type` (`inquiries`, `verifications` with `filter[type]`, `accounts`, `reports`, `lists` with `filter[list-id]`, `transactions`), `filter` (that list route's filters, or a saved view id), `format: csv` and `include-personal-data` (needs `pii:view`; masked columns otherwise): an asynchronous job of at most 50,000 rows (ours, provisional; `export_too_large`; lowered if the benchmark passes 2 minutes at the cap). One CSV per export: the public product bundles an inquiry file with one file per verification type, and here each is its own export of the same filter, so no archive library is needed (ours). The file is sealed in storage for 24 hours and then deleted; only the creating principal lists, reads and downloads it (others 404), and every download re-checks the permissions, as Phase 8 C138.1 does; one audit entry per export and per download, recording the filter's shape only. An export cut before a redaction keeps that person's data until it expires (at most 24 hours); the export page and the docs say so (review P36).
2. **Permissions** (public: an export permission per object type): `inquiries:export`, `verifications:export`, `accounts:export`, `reports:export`, `lists:export`, `transactions:export`; admin only by default (Phase 8 decision 68's rule); none is a `:read`, so viewers never hold them.
3. **Content:** documented columns per type through Phase 7's neutralising writer; never included (Phase 8 C138.4): secrets, tokens, storage keys, wrapped keys, face templates, embeddings, raw fingerprints, EXIF, other environments' data; redacted content exports as "redacted"; list item values are third-party personal data and need `pii:view` (K9).
4. **Routes:** `GET /exports` (the caller's own), `GET /exports/:id`, `GET /exports/:id/download` (`Content-Disposition: attachment`, `private, no-store`); rate limit `exports`; no event (case exports keep `case.exported`).
5. **Bulk report creation** (K13; Phase 6's G7): `POST /reports/bulk` (ours, multipart): `report-template-id` and a CSV whose header names the template's query sources (≤ 1,000 rows, ≤ 2 MiB, read by `readCsv`), `reports:write` plus each link's scope (Phase 6 decision 39); an asynchronous `report-bulk` job (`rbulk_`) creates each report through Phase 6's create service with `origin: bulk` (ours; a C69 amendment) and records per-row outcomes as codes (`GET /reports/bulk/:id`). The uploaded CSV holds third-party personal data (query sources), so it is stored through `FileStorage` (envelope-encrypted, row-bound, rewrap-listed), deleted when the job ends, and swept by `export-sweep` for jobs that ended or are older than 24 hours (a failed, retried or killed job included); it is in the redaction coverage as third-party PII, and the CSV body is masked in API logs (review P24). The resource is ours; the public `batch-jobs` resource is deferred (C177; review H24).
6. **Honesty:** an export over the cap is refused, never truncated; the export resource records its filter's shape, the time it was cut, its row count and whether personal data was included, shown beside the download (the CSV itself stays plain); a bulk row that failed is reported failed, never skipped.

### C167. Permissions, environment-scoped roles and the role editor (S5, S9, S10)

1. **New API scopes** (`resource:action`, kept against the public `resource.action`, S5): `theme-sets:read`, `theme-sets:write`, `analytics:read`, `deployments:read`, `inquiries:export`, `verifications:export`, `accounts:export`, `reports:export`, `lists:export`, `transactions:export`. Translations use `inquiry-templates:read` and `:write`; allowed origins, the reporting zone and suppression are settings under `settings:write`. Each permission gets a `PERMISSION_INFO` label and a refusal test. Built-in roles: `admin` all; `developer` the theme, analytics and deployment scopes; `reviewer` `analytics:read`; `viewer` every `:read` outside `NOT_FOR_VIEWERS` (theme sets, analytics and deployments included: counts and styles hold no personal data).
2. **Environment-scoped grants** (public, behaviour note 4.6; decisions 49 and 70): `user_roles` gains `environment_ids text[]` (`NULL` means every current and future environment, the public "all current and new environments"); a session's permissions are the union of the roles assigned for its environment; existing assignments migrate as `NULL`; the built-in `admin` role is assignable organisation-wide only (ours: no half-admin); `missingPermissions` checks the granting user's permissions in **each** environment named; entering an environment needs a role there, and production still needs `environments:production` held there; an API key holds at most what its minting user holds in the key's environment.
   **Organisation-level permissions** (review P01): `PERMISSION_LEVEL` in `packages/core/src/permissions.ts` marks `users:*` and `roles:*` as **organisation-level**; every other permission, `settings:write` and `environments:production` included, stays environment-level. An organisation-level permission is effective only through an assignment with `environment-ids: null`; the API refuses it in a narrower assignment (422 `role_scope_invalid`). Because `settings:write` covers both kinds of setting, a per-setting map `SETTING_LEVEL` (same file) marks the organisation settings (today `require-mfa`, and every later organisation setting) as organisation-level, and `PATCH /dashboard/settings` changes one of them only for a caller who holds `settings:write` through an organisation-wide assignment, while a sandbox-only `settings:write` still changes that environment's own settings (allowed origins, the analytics zone; verification round V14). Editing a role's permissions needs the editor to hold each added permission in **every** environment where the role is assigned. Organisation-level actions (users, roles, invitations, MFA resets, organisation settings) write their audit entry in every environment of the organisation, so `audit-logs:read` in any one environment shows them. Phase 8's `visible-to-roles` resolves against the session environment's assignments. Refusal tests: a sandbox-only `settings:write` cannot change `require-mfa`; a sandbox-only `roles:write` cannot add a permission to a role assigned in production; a sandbox-only `users:write` cannot act on a user whose roles reach production (C168.1, C168.3).
   **Sign-in and the environment switch** (review F29): with roles per environment, sign-in opens the first environment where the user holds a role (sandbox first), and answers a problem (`no_environment_role`, 403) when there is none; `PUT /dashboard/sessions/current/environment` refuses an environment without a role. The hook site is `operator-auth/session-context.ts` (the union `permissionsForRoles(roles)` today), and `api-access` owns `operator-auth/operator-auth.ts` and `routes/dashboard/environment.ts` for these changes; a test covers a user who holds roles in production only.
3. **Routes:** `PUT /dashboard/users/:id/roles` with `{ assignments: [{ role-id, environment-ids | null }] }` (amended); `GET /dashboard/roles/grantable` (the permissions and roles the caller may grant, per environment, each refused one with its reason).
4. **The role editor** (console): permissions grouped by area with a line each on what they allow; those the editing user does not hold disabled with the reason (the escalation rule); sensitive permissions marked (`pii:view`, `settings:privacy`, every `:redact` and `:export`, `environments:production`, Phase 8's `secrets:*`); a confirmation listing what a change removes and from how many users; every change audit-logged.
5. **Not built:** per-template constraints and "assigned to me" (S9; Phase 8's template visibility covers cases), and a finer split of `pii:view` (S10) (C177).
6. **Honesty:** the editor never offers a grant the API would refuse, and a user's effective permissions are shown per environment as the API computes them.

### C168. Team, MFA, sessions and the audit explorer (S8, S11–S16)

1. **Invitations:** `POST /dashboard/invitations/:id/resend` (a new token, the old one revoked, 7 days: ours, against the public 14, decision 47); `POST /dashboard/users/:id/reactivate` (a deactivated user gets a new invitation and keeps their history; public). A reactivated user gets **no roles back**: roles are granted again through `PUT /dashboard/users/:id/roles` with its checks (review P02).
   **Acting on another user** (review P02; decision 70; reactivation, deactivation and C168.3's reset): the actor must hold, at C167.2's levels, a superset of the target's effective permissions in every environment the target can enter (403 `user_more_privileged`). The reset and reactivation also need the actor's session to have verified a second factor within the last 10 minutes (403 `step_up_required`; the console asks for the factor again); deactivation needs it only when the actor has an enrolled factor, because deactivation only removes access and revokes sessions (`services/users.ts`), so it stays the emergency control in organisations without `require-mfa` and on `seed:dev`, whose operators have no factor (verification round V05). `user_sessions` gains `mfa_verified_at`, set on every factor check. An operator with no factor cannot reset another user's MFA or reactivate a user. A test per rule, including deactivation by an actor without a factor and the existing deactivation tests unchanged.
2. **MFA by default** (S13; decision 45): organisations created from Phase 9 on start with `require-mfa: true` (the column default and every organisation-creation path the inventory names); existing organisations keep their value; `seed:dev` sets `false` explicitly and says so in its output, and `seed:dev --mfa-admin` keeps exercising the enforced path; TOTP and WebAuthn only (no SMS or email factor).
3. **Reset MFA in the console** (S14; decision 46; ours, instead of the public never-expiring admin codes): `POST /dashboard/users/:id/reset-mfa` (`users:write`; never on oneself; audited) removes the user's factors and recovery codes and revokes every session of that user; with `require-mfa` the user enrols at the next sign-in; the superset and step-up rules of 1 apply; when the mailer is configured, the target is sent a notice with a link only (a reset, and likewise a reactivation); `user:reset-mfa` stays for self-hosters.
4. **Sessions** (S16): `GET /dashboard/sessions` (the caller's own: a device summary from the user agent, created, last seen, current marked), `POST /dashboard/sessions/:id/revoke`, `POST /dashboard/sessions/revoke-others`; enrolling a factor revokes the user's other sessions (public).
5. **The users list** (S12): MFA status (factor kinds and counts, never secrets) and last sign-in, shown only to holders of `users:write`, so a viewer cannot list the accounts without MFA (review P20).
