6. **The audit explorer** (S11, ours: the action-level log stays): `filter[action-prefix]`, `filter[from]`, `filter[to]` and `filter[ip]` beside the existing action, actor and resource filters; `filter[ip]` matches exactly through a separate table `audit_log_ip_index (audit_log_id, environment_id, ip_hmac)` (HMAC under the environment's blind-index subkey, label `openverify/audit-ip/v1`), which `blind-index:rebuild` backfills and rewrites, joined for the filter and deleted with its row by the retention pass, so `audit_logs` stays append-only (its trigger refuses every update but the KEK rewrap; a test shows it still does; review P23), and needs `pii:view`; the setting `audit-log-retention-days` (absent: kept as today; 30–3,650), enforced by the C16 pass.
7. **Not built:** SSO (OIDC, SAML, SCIM; Phase 11, decision 48), the unused-permission review (S12), the idle timeout (Phase 11's G-IDLE) (C177).
8. **Honesty:** the users list shows which factors a user has enrolled, never a judgement that an account is "secure"; an organisation without `require-mfa` says so on the settings page.

### C169. API keys, webhooks and notifications (S4, S6, S7, S18, S24)

1. **Rotation** (ours; the public product documents a manual procedure; decision 50): `POST /api-keys/:id/rotate` with `meta.previous-expires-in-seconds` (0–2,592,000) clones the key (scopes, IPs, version, blocklist) through `cloneApiKey`'s own checks (the caller must hold every scope it copies, and `pii-masked` is forced when the caller cannot see PII; a refusal test; review P33) and expires the old one in one transaction, answers the new token once, and writes one audit entry linking both. Keys gain the public `note` (≤ 255, optional; review H25).
2. **Age:** the key list shows age; keys older than `api-key-age-warning-days` (default 90) are marked.
3. **Attribute blocklist** (S4; the public `api-attributes-blocklist`, ours `attribute-blocklist` in C14's webhook grammar): attributes matching a key's blocklist are removed from every document served to that key (ids, types and relationships kept), by one hook applied recursively to embedded documents, at every exit (review P22): top-level `data`, `included[]`, the `payload` of `GET /events` and `GET /events/:id`, export columns of exports the key creates (C166), bulk-report results, the `meta` of asynchronous jobs, and problems that echo attributes; a test per exit and per resource family.
4. **A gitleaks rule** for `ov_(sbx|prod)_[A-Za-z0-9_-]{43}` and the other bearer tokens of the same format, `ov[suomi]_[A-Za-z0-9_-]{43}` (`packages/contracts/src/tokens.ts`; review P33), in a new `.gitleaks.toml` that extends the default rules (`[extend] useDefault = true`), which the pre-commit hook and the CI scan already pick up when present. The repository already tracks such tokens in tests and in `docs/tdd/`, and CI scans the whole history (review F02), so the rule carries an allowlist the fixtures satisfy: a `[[rules.allowlists]]` regex on a case-insensitive `synthetic` in the token, and an entropy floor (`entropy`, set from the measured fixtures; a real token is 256 random bits) that low-entropy fixtures such as `ovo_ooo…` fall under; a test requires every new test token to carry `synthetic`. Tests: `gitleaks git` over the current history stays clean with the new configuration; a random token generated at test time in a temporary repository (never committed) is caught; the default rules still run. The configuration lands in `contracts-9b`'s common commit, so the first 9B commit already scans with it.
5. **Webhooks** (decision 52): `name` (≤ 200) and `description` (≤ 1,000) (public; optional in the API for existing clients, required by the console for new webhooks); delivery headers `OpenVerify-Webhook-Attempts-Made`, `OpenVerify-Webhook-Attempts-Left`, `OpenVerify-Webhook-First-Attempted-At` (ours, mirroring the public ones); the retry schedule stays 8 attempts over 24 hours with jitter (ours, against the public 7 over about 51 hours; documented); the public `included-allowlist` and `relationship-allowlist` are kept as ours (payloads carry relationships as ids only and include no related objects), and `file-access-token-expires-in` on keys and webhooks is not built (files are served only through authenticated routes, with no file-access tokens; review H25); the webhook page shows when the previous secret stops signing and that receivers must accept either signature meanwhile.
6. **Failure alerts** (S6, public): per webhook `failure-alert {enabled, threshold ≥ 1, window-minutes 5–1,440}` over failed first attempts; the `webhook-failure-alerts` sweep (60 s) notifies, at most once per window, the users who opted in and hold `webhooks:read` in that environment: a console notification and, when the mailer is configured and the user chose it, an email with a link only.
7. **Notifications** (S18): Phase 8's `user_notifications` gains the kind `webhook-failure`, and, because its row names a case id (C134.4) and a webhook failure has no case, `subject_type` and `subject_id`, with `case_id` made nullable and kept for Phase 8's rows, a check constraint per kind and a migration test (review F18; `foundation-9b` (W)); `GET`, `PUT /dashboard/notification-preferences` hold per kind `{ console, email }` (defaults: console on, email off).
8. **Sender name** (S24; ours): `mail-sender-name` per environment (a display name only, ≤ 100; control characters, `<`, `>`, `@` and `"` refused, so `Bank Security <alerts@bank.example>` cannot be set; encoded with RFC 2047 when not ASCII and quoted under RFC 5322 on every send; a test with the spoofing examples; review P29); the From address stays install-wide (Phase 8 decision 37), and DNS verification stays the operator's.
9. **Not built:** OAuth client credentials on deliveries, key inflection, payload filters, template-restricted keys (C177).
10. **Honesty:** an alert counts failed first attempts in its window and says so; a rotated key's old token keeps working until the expiry the operator chose, and the console shows that time.

### C170. The console's script CSP (K16, S17)

1. **`proxy.ts`** (Next.js 16) makes a fresh 128-bit nonce per request and sends `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self'; script-src 'self' 'nonce-{n}' 'strict-dynamic'; style-src 'self' 'nonce-{n}'; img-src 'self' blob: data:; font-src 'self'; connect-src 'self'; frame-src {flow origin}; worker-src 'none'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'self'; form-action 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'; report-uri /csp-reports; report-to csp`, with a `Reporting-Endpoints: csp="/csp-reports"` header (reviews P11, F21) and the nonce in a request header for Next.js; development adds `'unsafe-eval'` only; the flow origin comes from `OPENVERIFY_FLOW_PUBLIC_URL`. **Every page renders dynamically** (review F20): Next.js puts the nonce into its scripts only when a page renders per request, and `'strict-dynamic'` makes browsers ignore `'self'`, so a prerendered page would not hydrate; the root layout reads request data (`await connection()`), a build-output test asserts that no route is prerendered as static, and the real-header test includes the 404 page, the error page and `/login`.
2. **Enforced** by the end of Phase 9 (decision 51), with `OPENVERIFY_DASHBOARD_CSP_MODE=enforce | report-only` (default `enforce`); violations go to `POST /csp-reports` on the console, which is public in `proxy.ts` (reports often arrive without the session cookie), caps the body at 16 KiB, accepts only `application/csp-report` and `application/reports+json`, limits each client address to 60 reports a minute, and logs at warn only the `effective-directive` and the blocked origin's scheme and host (never `document-uri`, `referrer` or a query; reviews P11, F21); a test per rule and one that a violation reaches the log line.
3. **Inline styles:** `@xyflow/react`, visx and Cytoscape render client-only; the injected-policy tests of Phases 7 and 8 become real-header tests on every page with zero `securitypolicyviolation` events.
4. The static headers of `next.config.ts` stay; `frame-ancestors 'none'` stays in both.
5. **Honesty:** in report-only mode the console shows a banner that the script policy is not enforced.

### C171. The WCAG 2.2 AA audit (T14)

1. **Scope** (WCAG-EM 2.0; WCAG 2.2 conformance requirement 5.2.3 makes the flow one complete process): every flow step type with its error, retry and final screens, in each shipped locale and in right-to-left, light and dark, hosted and embedded, at 320, 375 and 768 CSS px and at 200% zoom; every console page, the builder, the preview, the theme and translation editors, analytics and settings; a structured sample plus a random sample per WCAG-EM. The native Android SDK's screens are **not** in Phase 9's scope (Phase 10, the Android behaviour note's M35), and the statement says so (review H12).
2. **Automated** (decision 53; engineering note 7.6): `@axe-core/playwright` 4.14.0 and `axe-core` 4.14.0 as dev dependencies (MPL-2.0, rule 4's tooling exception, unmodified, never in a bundle; `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` §5); one helper `expectAccessible(page, { include?, exclude? })` in `apps/flow/e2e/support/` and `e2e/tests/support/` with the tags `wcag2a`, `wcag2aa`, `wcag21a`, `wcag21aa` and `wcag22aa`, failing on any violation and attaching "incomplete" results for the manual pass; exceptions only in `e2e/a11y-exceptions.json`, each with a reason and an expiry date; called at each state of each step in the mocked flow suite (no stack) and once per console page in the full-stack suite, in its own shard (closes the axe carry open since Workflow 5A). **The CI matrix** (review F16: the full cross product is about 84 configurations per state, 5,000–8,400 axe runs, 1.5–2.5 hours): every state in `en` at 320 px light; every state in `ar` at 320 px dark; both pseudo-locales at 320 px; one embedded run and one run at 768 px per step type. The full cross product runs once, in 9D, in shards that each fit a 10-minute call, with its timing recorded, and the statement's method names this matrix.
3. **Our own tests** for what axe cannot check (its only WCAG 2.2 A/AA rule is `target-size`): 2.4.11 (tab through each page and fail when `elementsFromPoint` at the focused element's centre and corners returns a covering element); 2.5.7 (every builder, layout-editor and chart operation without `mouse.down` and `mouse.move`); 3.2.6 (the help slot's DOM position on every step); 3.3.7 (a value entered once is shown later, including screen → `phone_otp` and `database` → `phone_otp` through `prefillFrom`; review H22); 3.3.8 (a full code pasted into one `autocomplete="one-time-code"` input; the console password field allows paste); 2.5.8 (24 px targets); 1.4.10 and 1.4.12 (the pseudo-locales at 320 px under the text-spacing override); 1.4.1 and 1.4.11 (charts and the canvas); `toMatchAriaSnapshot` on step screens and the main console pages.
4. **Timing** (2.2.1; carried since Phase 4; decision 57): 20 s before the selfie capture limit (180 s) the web flow announces it in a live region and offers an extension by one simple action, at least ten times (2.2.1's "Extend" option; review H11). The lifetime and the cap of 10 captures are server rules (`SELFIE_CAPTURE_TTL_SECONDS`, `SELFIE_CAPTURE_LIMIT`, `services/flow.ts`), so the extension is a server route, a C3 amendment: `POST /api/v1/flow/selfie-captures/:id/extend` moves the capture's `expires_at` by 180 s, is allowed at least 10 times per capture, does not count towards the cap, and is refused after submit (409 `selfie_capture_not_extendable`); the contract is `contracts-9a`'s (F), the route `api-flow-engine`'s, the call `flow-renderer`'s, and the test extends ten times and then uploads successfully. The completion redirect never navigates on a timer (C158.5; review H13), with a test. The builder warns on session or inquiry windows below 20 hours; Android follows in Phase 10.
5. **Manual** (decisions 55 and 56): keyboard only in Chrome and Firefox; NVDA with Firefox and Chrome on Windows; TalkBack with Chrome on Android (the hosted flow on a phone, the main real case); VoiceOver with Safari on iOS and macOS (an Apple device); zoom, spacing, orientation and reduced motion; timing. A person runs these (the owner or a contracted auditor), never an agent; the checklist and its results are in `docs/accessibility/audit-<date>.md`.
6. **The statement** (`docs/accessibility/statement.md`; decision 71): the standard (WCAG 2.2 AA), the scope (the Android SDK named as not covered), the date, the method, what was tested automatically and manually, the known gaps with their tracking items, and the assistive technologies used. Until a person has run the manual pass it says "not yet evaluated for conformance" and never uses the word "conforms"; afterwards it claims conformance only for what the manual pass covered, and names anything untested (VoiceOver without a device) as untested (review H12). Phase 9 is recorded as "WCAG 2.2 AA audit: automated part done, manual part not run, no conformance claimed", and the manual part is carried as an open item to the release gate (Phase 11's acceptance).
7. **Not adopted** (decision 54): pa11y and pa11y-ci (LGPL-3.0-only; rule 4 fails them even as tooling); `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y` (its peer range ends at ESLint 9 while the workspace runs 10.13; re-checked at the base); Lighthouse in CI (it runs axe again).

### C172. Case console completion (K5, K6; S20, S21)

1. **Layout** (Phase 8 decision 70, delivered here): case template versions gain `layout {columns: 1–3, modules[] {key, column, order, collapsed}}` over Phase 8's `layout-modules`; the layout editor uses the shared editor's outline pattern (drag and drop, plus move up, move down and move to column for keyboard and single-pointer use); an unknown module or one without a permission is refused at publish.
2. **Checklists:** `checklists[] {key, label, items[] {key, label, required-for-statuses[]}}` on the version; per case `checklist-state` (item, checked, by, at) through `POST /cases/:id/checklist` (`cases:write`); a transition into a status that an unchecked required item names is refused with 422 `case_checklist_incomplete`; history rows (PII-free); `case.updated` (C131.5).
3. **The status-switcher rule** (public name; behaviour not documented, ours): the switcher offers only the transitions the version allows (C131.1) and, per status, `requires-fields[]` that must hold a value before entering it (422 `case_fields_required`).
4. **Preset comments:** `preset-comments[] {key, label, text}` (plain text, ≤ 2,000, no placeholders) on the version, inserted into the comment box and editable before posting, never posted by themselves.
5. **Case-action placements and visibility** (the Phase 8 C140.1 deferral): `placement` (`header`, `module:<module key>`, `status-switcher`) and `visible-when {statuses[], role-ids[]}` on case actions; `GET /cases/:id/available-actions` filters by them, and `POST /cases/:id/run-action` (Phase 8 C140.1) applies the same `visible-when` through the same function, answering 403 `case_action_not_available`, so a role restriction shown in the console is enforced by the API (review P28; a test).
6. **Teams and availability:** the next release (decision 58); case analytics ships without availability metrics (C164.5).
7. **Honesty:** a checklist records who checked each item and when, and nothing checks an item on a person's behalf; a preset comment is the operator's text, posted only by a person.

### C173. Reports, verifications and other console completions (K12, K15, K17, K18, K19, K20)

1. **Reports** (K15): the report resource gains `latest-run-notes[]` (the latest run's note codes, Phase 6 C74, where runs and their notes are defined; review F23); the console's inquiry document includes the inquiry's reports within Phase 6 decision 25's bounds (25 reports, at most 10 detail reads, masked); the `FormDialogButton` options of Phase 6's proposals; Phase 6 decision 6's coverage sentence beside every "Complete" in the reports index and history (K20); the "Other lists" console e2e check on a synthetic OFAC Consolidated entry added to the sandbox list fixture (K19).
2. **Verifications** (K17): `GET /verifications/:id` gains the `files` relationship (`include=files`: file resources with metadata only, the existing file contract) and `redacted-at`.
3. **Print** (K12; decision 59): browser print views for the inquiry, report and verification pages, under Phase 8 C142.5's rules (environment, ids, the printing operator's id, the time, "contains personal data"); `POST /api/v1/dashboard/print-audits` (`{object-id}`) writes the audit entry before the browser's dialog opens. It records use of the print button, not every print (a browser's menu or shortcut writes nothing), and the print view and the docs say so (review P36). The public `print` routes (`inquiries/{id}/print`, `reports/{id}/print`, `verifications/{id}/print`) and a server-made PDF stay deferred (C177): a renderer without a font that covers every script would drop characters from people's names, and no such font passes rule 4 without the OFL-1.1 exception.
4. **The trust store** (K18): the trust-store page shows the install flags `OPENVERIFY_TRUST_ANCHOR_REFRESH` and `OPENVERIFY_NFC_ALLOW_ICAO_CRLS` read-only, served by the dashboard trust-anchors route as `meta.flags`; changing them stays an install setting.
5. **Honesty:** a print view marks redacted content "redacted" and masked values as masked, never as empty.

### C174. Platform amendments

1. **Identifiers:** public `theset` (theme set), `dply` (deployment); ours `tsa` (theme asset), `exp` (export), `svw` (saved view), `rbulk` (bulk report job), `ssp` (simulation preset). Step records, action waits, theme revisions and rollup rows have no public id. Each prefix joins both prefix tables with a test.
2. **Events** (C154.8; golden payloads, webhook samples, the dashboard groups "Inquiry templates" and "Deployments"): `inquiry-template-version.created`, `inquiry-template-version.published`, `deployment.completed`.
3. **Repeatable jobs and queues** (C54.1): `analytics-rollup` (5 min), `flow-action-sweep` (15 s), `webhook-failure-alerts` (60 s), `export-sweep` (60 s, expired export files and the uploaded CSVs of ended or day-old bulk report jobs deleted, C166.5); queues `analytics-rebuild`, `export`, `report-bulk`, `flow-signal-snapshot` (C150.9), each removing its jobs on completion and failure (theme-asset renditions are synchronous, C160.6, so there is no rendition queue); retention steps in the C16 pass for step records, action rows and signal snapshots (with their inquiries), rollups (the assignee rollup by its own setting, C163.3), exports and audit logs.
4. **Rate-limit policies** (C12): `analytics` 60 a minute per principal; `exports` 10 an hour per environment; `translations-import` 30 an hour per environment; `template-preview` (preview, simulate and `validate`, which runs every async publish hook; review P35) 120 a minute per principal; `theme-assets` 30 an hour per environment; `internal-embed-policy` 6,000 a minute per flow-server token, a capacity ceiling only, since the flow server limits each client first (C157.2; reviews P05, F03); `invitations-resend` 5 an hour per invitation and `report-bulk` 5 an hour per environment (review P35); screen submissions and selfie-capture extensions under the existing `flow-session` policy. A test per policy shows it applied.
5. **Masking, redaction, rewrap** (each Stage 1b declares its rows with a test per exit): screen submission `values`, `initial_fields`, bulk report CSV bodies, simulation preset values, every member of the simulate body's `scope` and the `default` values of the preview body's `field-schemas` (operators will paste a real person's values there, in production too; review P12) join the API-log masked members and the PII lists, with a per-route test; operator copy, theme styles and consent digests are not personal data and are not masked; inquiry redaction deletes the inquiry's step records, action rows and signal snapshots (C151.1, C152.8, C150.9), nulls `initial_fields`, and lists the account targets of `update_account_fields` from the action rows' copy columns before deleting them (C152.4, C152.8; review P13, verification round V08); `initial_fields`, theme assets, export files, bulk report CSVs (review P24) and presets join `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS`, `SEALED` and `REWRAP_TABLES` with a `kek:rewrap` test; `audit_log_ip_index` (C168.6) and the transaction filter HMACs join `blind-index:rebuild`; the assignee rollup is deleted or re-keyed with its user (C163.3); audit metadata of every new call site validates against a strict schema with no free-text members (Phase 8 C143.5).
6. **Settings** (C16 route, audit-logged; under `settings:write`): `allowed-embed-origins`, `allowed-redirect-origins`, `default-theme-set-id`, `analytics-time-zone`, `analytics-suppression-threshold`, `analytics-retention-months`, `api-key-age-warning-days`, `mail-sender-name`, `audit-log-retention-days`, `analytics-settle-days` (C163.4) and `analytics-assignee-retention-months` (C163.3); the organisation's `require-mfa` default (C168.2), an organisation member changed only with organisation-wide `settings:write` (C167.2).
7. **Configuration** (per-process validation, `.env.example`, compose): `OPENVERIFY_FLOW_SERVER_TOKEN` (API and flow server; at least 32 random bytes, checked at startup) and `OPENVERIFY_FLOW_SERVER_TOKEN_PREVIOUS` (accepted during a rotation; review P31), `OPENVERIFY_FLOW_SERVER_API_URL` and `OPENVERIFY_FLOW_SERVER_TRUSTED_PROXY_HOPS` (flow server; C157.2), `OPENVERIFY_DASHBOARD_PUBLIC_URL` read by the flow server, `OPENVERIFY_FLOW_PUBLIC_URL` read by the console, `OPENVERIFY_DASHBOARD_CSP_MODE`; the token joins the secret-scan and startup-error redaction lists.
8. **Problems** (RFC 9457, golden fixtures): `flow_step_not_current`, `field_unknown`, `field_write_once`, `field_type_changed`, `pattern_refused`, `visibility_scope_invalid`, `scope_path_unknown`, `scope_path_unavailable_in_flow`, `cycle_not_allowed`, `action_error_edge_missing`, `on_null_missing` (with Phase 8's other `definition_invalid` codes), `precondition_failed`, `draft_exists`, `promote_unresolved`, `consent_copy_incomplete`, `theme_contrast_insufficient`, `theme_set_default`, `asset_type_refused`, `embed_origin_invalid`, `redirect_uri_not_allowed`, `saved_view_pii_filter`, `export_too_large`, `case_checklist_incomplete`, `case_fields_required`; from the review: `signal_wait_missing` (a `definition_invalid` code, C150.9), `role_scope_invalid`, `no_environment_role` (C167.2), `user_more_privileged`, `step_up_required` (C168.1), `consent_text_mismatch`, `consent_copy_missing_placeholder` (C162.2, C162.9), `case_action_not_available` (C172.5), `selfie_capture_not_extendable` (C171.4), `locale_unsupported` (C161.2); from the verification round (V17): `fields_too_large` (422, with `size` and `limit`, C149.2) and `embed_wildcard_unacknowledged` (422, a production wildcard without `meta.acknowledge-wildcards`, C158.1); publish acknowledgements missing answer Phase 8's `publish_acknowledgement_missing` with the keys. The step error `workflow_grant_widened` (C152.2) is listed with Phase 8's step errors in `docs/workflows/errors.md`.
9. **OpenAPI and SDK:** every new route registered as a plugin with zod schemas (the internal embed-policy route kept out of the public document); `@openverify/node` groups `themeSets`, `deployments`, `inquiryTemplateTranslations`, `analytics`, `exports`.

### C175. What Phase 10 and Phase 11 take

1. **Phase 10 (Android, in `commonMain`, iOS-ready):** send `OpenVerify-Flow-Spec: 1, 2`; render the `position` step from `step` and stop evaluating transitions; the `screen` step with C148's catalogue in Compose; visibility rules with a strict evaluator (a path outside the screen throws); the pattern matcher of C148.3 as a Kotlin port in `commonMain`, passing `packages/flow-spec`'s golden vectors, so the server and both clients agree (verification round V10; until then the server's answer is the only pattern check on Android); the theme onto `MaterialTheme` (palette and radius; the system font, since an uploaded font is `platforms: ["web"]` until Phase 10 adds a validated font path or bundled app fonts, C160.7, C177) with an `appearance` option; operator copy with `{name}` placeholders; `fr`, `de` and `ar` from `contracts/fixtures/i18n/*.json`, with an RTL review of every screen; digit normalisation (MRZ and BAC keys stay ASCII); the waiting state of action steps and branch waits; `exhausted` fallback routes, which the NFC step needs (the Android behaviour note's M22); the 2.2.1 capture warning and the extension route (C171.4); `prefillFrom` values on the OTP and database steps (C148.7); the consent digests, copy source and catalog version on every consent (C162.9); `device.platform` from its `User-Agent` and `device.kind` through the same mapping (C150.2); `locale` on `Request` passed as `language`. Until then, today's SDK runs linear flows through the v1 projection and stops on its unsupported screen at the first step it cannot render (C151.6); the contract fixtures of C178.1 prove it decodes every new session member, and the `FlowCoordinator` host test of C151.6 proves it never reports `Completed` before the server's position is a completion step (reviews H01, F01).
2. **Phase 11:** SSO (OIDC first, then SAML and SCIM); the console idle timeout; production images (the flow server becomes its own image) and the Helm chart, which needs no static `flow.allowedFrameAncestors` because the flow server reads the allowlist per request; npm publication of `@openverify/js`, `sdk-react` and `apps/demo-web`; the threat model's embed boundary (B2) entries for the preview and the embed, and the ASVS rows for both CSPs (G-CSP verified, G-FRAME closed by C157); `/api/v1/internal/*` denied at the chart's ingress, or bound to an `OPENVERIFY_INTERNAL_LISTEN` address that the ingress does not serve, with a chart test (C177; verification round V13); Connect's `share-consent` step joins flow-spec v2's step union without a format change.

### C176. Records, ADR and docs

- **ADR 0008** "Flows: server-decided branching, embedding and preview origins" (the five rules of the Summary; who frames whom; the headers; the message protocols; what the allowlist does not cover), written by `contracts-9a` (decision 64).
- **PLAN** (corrected by `integration-9a`; decision 63): §2 Inquiries row (screens, components, branching, action and connector steps, modules as editor macros, decided on the server); §2 Platform row (theme sets per environment, translations inside template versions, allowed origins for embedding and redirects, a deployment history); §3 hardening (`frame-ancestors` sent per request by the flow server, never in `<meta>`); §3 technology table (visx charts, the flow server, axe in tests only); §6 Phase 9 (the four Workflows); `phase-2.plan.md:921`'s stale "Phase 9 (cases)"; §2 Platform row's "batch jobs/importers": the public `batch-jobs` resource deferred (C177; review H24); the "Next release" section gains teams, availability and availability-driven assignment (K6, S21) and the public `print` routes with a server-made PDF (K12, S22, pending a font exception), as owner items, so neither is lost after Phase 9 (review H30).
- **`docs/research/persona-surface.md`:** `inquiry-template-version.created` and `.published` (listed on the events page without webhook files), `theset` and `dply` prefixes, the `inquiry-template-translations` type, and the 88-property version theme.
- **`THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`:** `@visx/scale`, `@visx/shape`, `@visx/axis`, `@visx/group`, `@visx/text` 4.0.0 (MIT) and `@visx/vendor` (MIT and ISC; d3 modules), with every transitive package visx 4.0.0 resolves in the lockfile, each with its version, licence and release date, and visx's `react` peer range checked against React 19.3 (review F24; `contracts-9b`); `axe-core` and `@axe-core/playwright` 4.14.0 in §5 (MPL-2.0, dev only, unmodified, never shipped); Excluded: pa11y and pa11y-ci (LGPL-3.0-only), next-intl (not adopted: install scripts and unattributed new dependencies), DOMPurify on jsdom (not adopted: raster logos only), OFL-1.1 fonts (not shipped without an owner exception); the dated release-age record of decision 62.
- **Docs:** `docs/flows/` (format, versions and migration, steps and components, branching with the scope and `onNull`, actions, the server engine, preview and simulate, embedding and redirects, the flow server's headers, `@openverify/js`); `docs/themes/README.md`; `docs/i18n/README.md` (locales, review status, operator copy, the consent-copy rule, CSV); `docs/analytics/README.md` (definitions, suppression and its limits, retention); `docs/accessibility/` (statement, audit); `docs/settings/` (allowed origins, roles per environment, MFA, keys, webhooks); the dashboard README sections.

### C177. Deferred

- **Flow** (each with its reason): weighted-random splits and request criteria on template deployments, undeploy and "publish without deploy" (behaviour note F4, F3: our publish is the deployment of an environment-scoped template); parallel steps (F7: semantics not public); `initial-step-name` (F27: it lets a caller skip screens); several steps of one verification type on exclusive branches (C147.6); File upload, Home, List Container, Device Handoff Button, ID type select, country multi select, international database inputs, the bank component and dynamic images (F12, T5); address autocomplete (F18: no permissive maps provider; a sandbox-only adapter would add nothing in production); "test in sandbox" with draft-pinned inquiries (engineering note 3.4, decision 17); ICU `plural` and `select` in operator copy; client-reported screen-view analytics.
- **Themes and languages:** machine translation (T12: no permissive engine, and consent texts carry legal weight); SVG logos (decision 28); a bundled web font and the OFL-1.1 exception (decision 27); translating the console (decision 33); locales beyond the five (T9); operator fonts on native clients (review P18: Android parses font bytes without the browsers' sanitiser and does not load WOFF2; Phase 10 decides between a sanitising re-encode at upload, after a licence check of the tool, and bundled app fonts).
- **Analytics:** noise-based privacy (differential privacy) against window and cross-filter differencing (review P21: blocking a filtered answer subtracted from an unfiltered one would suppress totals for every filter combination; stated as a limit in C164.7); the SQL explorer (A11); the session map (A10, K11: still no permissive tiles without network calls); availability metrics (A6).
- **Settings:** SSO, SAML and SCIM (S15; Phase 11); per-template role constraints and "assigned to me" (S9); a finer split of `pii:view` (S10); the unused-permission review (S12); OAuth client credentials on webhook deliveries (S6); key inflection and payload filters (S25); template-restricted API keys (S4); the console idle timeout (Phase 11); a separate listener for the API's internal routes (review P31; verification round V13: a Kubernetes NetworkPolicy filters addresses and ports, not paths, so it cannot restrict one path on the listener the public ingress also serves; Phase 11 instead denies `/api/v1/internal/*` at the ingress or binds those routes to an `OPENVERIFY_INTERNAL_LISTEN` address, C175.2; until then the route needs the flow-server token of C157.2); the public `batch-jobs` resource and its `batch-job.*` events (review H24: Phase 9's one bulk job, `rbulk_`, is ours; the public resource spans object kinds the Phase 9 research did not take, and PLAN §2 records the deferral).
- **Carried:** teams, availability and availability-driven assignment (K6, S21; next release); the public `print` routes and a server-made PDF (K12, S22; until a font exception); `sdk-react` and `apps/demo-web` (F26; Phase 11); npm publication of `@openverify/js` (Phase 11).

### C178. Fixtures and end to end

1. **Fixtures** (synthetic only): golden v2 flows (valid, one invalid per graph, component, field and publish rule), every v1 fixture with its migrated v2 form, golden session responses for v1 and v2 clients (`contracts/fixtures/api/flow-session-v1-projection.json`, one projection fixture per v2-only step type and the successive projections of C151.6's host test, and `flow-session-v2.json`, decoded by the Android SDK's `ContractFixturesTest`, which proves today's SDK ignores the new members; reviews F06, H01), theme sets with passing and failing palettes and the contrast vectors, translation CSVs (with formula cells), the locale JSON exports, analytics rows from a seeded synthetic population, simulation presets per verification type; names, emails at `example.com`, documentation IP ranges and fictional numbers only.
2. **Testcontainers:** the flow engine's races (C151.11); `run_workflow` waits against a Phase 8 run completing, erroring and timing out (the timeout canceling the run, C152.2), and a wider workflow version published after the template (C152.2); a provisional signal snapshot arriving within and after a branch's wait (C150.9); an open and a closed analytics day unchanged by redaction (on-demand and by a template version's own retention) and a zone change rebuilding only open days (C163.4); the rollup refresh with dirty days under concurrent writes, a rebuild after a zone change, and the read plans; complementary suppression on real rollups; environment-scoped permission resolution; the exports job with masking and expiry.
3. **Mocked flow suite** (`apps/flow/e2e`, no stack): every step and component state with `expectAccessible` on C171.2's CI matrix; branches driven by mocked session answers; RTL and pseudo-locales at 320 px; the preview's message checks (wrong origin, wrong source, oversized, malformed); the preview with an uploaded logo and font posted as `preview-assets`, rendered with zero `securitypolicyviolation` events, and a buffer that is neither PNG, JPEG, WebP nor WOFF2 refused (C156.2; review F09, verification round); the embed bridge's handshake; the theme CSP spec in three engines.
4. **Full-stack specs** (the reusable stack with shards, under `flock /tmp/openverify-e2e.lock`; each spec ≤ 90 s with its budget in a header line):
   - **9A:** a branching flow (a minor routed to a fallback screen by `age-years`, a retry exhausted into a document step that goes to review, a non-null provisional Phase 7 signal choosing the `flag_for_review` route, and a second run where it is null and follows `onNull`; review H02), the inquiry's outcome, `flow-outcome` and step record checked, and the same inquiries replayed through simulate with the same path and outcome (C156.4); a `run_workflow` step whose workflow output picks the route; an embedded flow from the e2e fixture page that works on an allowed origin and is refused (`frame-ancestors`) on another; the preview frame with its headers; an Arabic flow in RTL with an Arabic-Indic code typed; a translation CSV round trip;
   - **9B:** a theme refused by the contrast gate and an accepted one applied, with an uploaded logo (moved from 9A with `api-themes`; review F04); an analytics overview, funnel and check view over a seeded population with suppressed cells; an export with masked PII downloaded by its creator and refused to another user; a bulk report CSV; a role granted only in sandbox that cannot act in production; key rotation; a webhook failure alert;
   - **9C:** the console specs of C155.10 and the settings, analytics, theme and case pages, axe on every page and the real-header CSP test.

## Workflow 9A (the flow: spec, engine, templates, renderer, embedding, theme plumbing)

**Families and checkpoints**, as in Phases 7 and 8: (S) the spec and the server engine; (T) templates, versions, copy and translations; (F) the flow client, its server, the preview and the embed; (H) theme plumbing (contracts, tables and stubs; the theme builder `api-themes` runs in 9B, review F04). Stage 1a and Stage 1b commit by family, each checkpoint with every suite green and recorded in its TDD file. Ten agents (decisions 65 and 69).

- **Stage 1a: `contracts-9a`.** It runs alone. It first takes the inventory at the Phase 9 base into `docs/research/phase9-inventory.md` (every claim with its `path:line`), re-verifies the Starting point and every Phase 7 and Phase 8 interface 9A uses, and records drift as "Workflow 9A Stage 1a amendments". It names in the inventory: the verification decision sites that must call `advanceFlowInTx`; the call sites of `requiredVerifications`; the inputs of the two inquiry-comparison checks; every organisation-creation path; the Phase 7 list screenings that finish before completion (C150.2) and the Phase 7 device-class source; Phase 8's run-creation service, its output typing, its trigger-type table, where it stores trigger types, run creators and decision kinds (a Postgres enum needs `ALTER TYPE … ADD VALUE`), its typing and evaluation entry points (C150.12) and its workflow-reference lookup for "used by" and promote (review F17); Phase 7's signal computation function and which signals are `flow-provisional` (C150.9); whether any built-in translation has a recorded human review (C161.5); `MAX_INQUIRY_FIELDS`; the exact files of the session builder, the flow routes, the publish hooks and the flow's mock API.

  It delivers, committed in this order:
  - **Common:** ids (C174.1, its 9A part), the scopes `theme-sets:*` and `deployments:read` with roles, `NOT_FOR_VIEWERS` and `PERMISSION_INFO` labels (C167.1), problem codes with golden fixtures (C174.8), the dev dependencies `axe-core` 4.14.0 and `@axe-core/playwright` 4.14.0 for `apps/flow` and `e2e` with their `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md` §5 rows, `minimumReleaseAge: 20160` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` with no exclusions and the dated record of decision 62, ADR 0008.
  - **(S):** the flow-spec v2 schemas (`packages/flow-spec/src/v2/schemas/`: every step, component, edge, branch and action schema; `FLOW_SPEC_VERSIONS`; `parseAnyFlow`; issues with pointers), the scope description types and `FLOW_SCOPE_EXTRACTIONS`, `COPY_KEYS`' shape, `LOCALE_REVIEW_STATUS`'s shape (in `packages/flow-spec/src/locale-review-status.ts`, which `flow-i18n` then owns; verification round V16), and the export lines and stubs of `graph`, `migrate`, `next-position`, `projection`, `client-view`, `visibility`, `references`, `digits`, `pattern` and `pseudo-locale` (V10, V16); and, on the Phase 8 side (review F05), the `trigger-flow` entry in the trigger table with its trigger view (the inquiry as subject and the declared inputs only), `triggers` on each action allowed in flows (none until listed), the run creator `inquiry-flow`, and the decision record kind `flow-failure`; the registry flag `flow-provisional` (C150.9) and `CHECK_ERROR_REASONS` (C150.2); the hand-written golden projection fixtures and the `FlowCoordinatorProjectionTest.kt` host test of C151.6 over them, committed **green** (the SDK is unchanged; a red result stops the Workflow, because it means the projection rules of C151.6 are wrong; verification round V03). `flow-spec-logic` starts here.
  - **(T):** the template and version contract amendments (C149, C154.1–C154.3), the route schemas of C154.4 and C156.1, C156.4, the translations shape (C162.7), the deployment resource, the events of C154.8 with golden payloads and webhook samples, `@openverify/node` groups `deployments` and `inquiryTemplateTranslations`.
  - **(F):** the flow API amendments (C151.5–C151.8, C148.5, C153.2, C158.4–C158.5, C160.6's flow route), the `OpenVerify-Flow-Spec` header, the internal embed-policy schema, the settings of C158.1 and C174.6's 9A part, the preview and embed message schemas (C156.2, C159.4) in `packages/contracts/src/flow-messages.ts`, `matchesOriginPattern`, the origin grammar and `parseEmbedPolicyAnswer` with their golden vectors in the import-free `packages/contracts/src/origin-pattern.ts` and its subpath export (C158.1, C157.2; verification round V04), the consent body's digests (C162.9) and the selfie-capture extension route (C171.4).
  - **(H):** the theme-set, styles and asset contracts (C160.1–C160.3, C160.6–C160.7, the operator asset route included), the contrast function's signature and stub in `packages/core/src/contrast/`, the group `themeSets`.
  - **With each family's fixtures:** the API fixtures in `contracts/fixtures/api/` (with `flow-session-v1-projection.json` and `flow-session-v2.json`), the Android fixture list in `ContractFixturesTest.kt` (each new file decoded by the SDK or listed server-only; with the host test above, the only Android edits of Phase 9), the dashboard typecheck minimum (labels, event words, `PERMISSION_INFO`) and the flow's mock API minimum. The API, flow, dashboard and Android suites stay green.

  It owns any path its deliverables need.

## Workflow 9B (the platform: themes, analytics, exports, access, keys and webhooks, carried items)

**Families and checkpoints:** (H) themes, on 9A's frozen theme plumbing; (A) analytics; (X) exports, saved views and filters; (U) access (roles, team, MFA, sessions, audit); (W) keys, webhooks and notifications; (C) the case and report completions. Ten agents (`api-themes` moved here from 9A; review F04). 9B starts after 9A is pushed.

- **Stage 1a: `contracts-9b`.** It runs alone. It first re-verifies 9A as built (the step record, the session members, the settings route, the publish acknowledgement form, the theme plumbing and its stubs), Phase 8's `user_notifications`, export machinery, case template versions and transition service, Phase 7's screenings, transaction type versions and `identifierKey`, Phase 6's report runs and create service, and records drift. Then it commits by family:
  - **Common:** ids (C174.1, its 9B part); the scopes `analytics:read` and the six `:export` scopes with roles and labels (C167.1); problems; the 9B settings (C174.6) with their defaults; the dashboard typecheck minimum; `@visx/scale`, `@visx/shape`, `@visx/axis`, `@visx/group`, `@visx/text` 4.0.0 added to `apps/dashboard` through the catalog with their licence rows and every transitive package from the lockfile (C176; review F24), so 9C adds no dependency; `.gitleaks.toml` with C169.4's rule, allowlist and tests, so the first 9B commit already scans with it (review F02); the `analytics:benchmark` script entry in `apps/api/package.json`, in the existing form (`tsx --conditions=@openverify/source --env-file-if-exists=../../.env`), with separate `--seed` and `--measure` phases that each fit one call (review F22).
  - **(A):** the analytics query and answer schemas (C164.8), the definitions as documented constants, the rollup row types.
  - **(X):** the export, saved-view and bulk-report contracts (C165, C166), the new list filters, the delivery search body; `@openverify/node` groups `analytics` and `exports`.
  - **(U):** `PERMISSION_LEVEL` and `SETTING_LEVEL` (C167.2; V14), the role-assignment body, `grantable`, the invitation, reactivation, MFA reset and session routes with the step-up and superset problems, the audit filters (C167.2–C167.3, C168).
  - **(W):** rotation, the key `note` and blocklist, webhook `name`, `description`, `failure-alert` and the attempt headers, the notification subject, notification preferences, `mail-sender-name` (C169).
  - **(C):** the case template version members, checklist state, the case-action placement and visibility members (C172), `latest-run-notes`, `origin: bulk`, the verification `files` relationship and `redacted-at`, the print-audit route, the trust-store `meta.flags` (C173).
  - **With each family's fixtures:** API fixtures, the Android list, the dashboard minimum.

  It owns any path its deliverables need.
- **Stage 1b: `foundation-9b`** (plumbing only; one migration extended per checkpoint; the PGlite measurement before and after), beside Stage 2 (9B's theme family needs no Stage 1b of its own: 9A froze its tables, routes and stubs):
  - **(A):** the rollup tables of C163.3 with their indexes, each with C163.4's `source` column, and the `updated_at` indexes they need; the hook `recordRedactedContributionInTx` (stub in `services/analytics/redaction.ts`, `api-analytics`) in `services/redaction.ts` before anything is cleared (C163.4; verification round, H04); the `analytics-rollup` registration and the `analytics-rebuild` queue with stubs; the routes frozen in `routes/server/analytics.ts`; the `analytics` policy;

- **Stage 2:** **`analytics-logic`** (after Stage 1a common and (A)): `packages/core/src/analytics/`: the metric definitions as pure functions over rollup rows, suppression with rate bounds and complementary suppression (C164.7), the duration buckets and their bounds (C163.5), the signal bins, with property tests (no visible cell can be derived from the visible others) and golden vectors. It owns that folder.
- **Stage 3** (each after its family's checkpoint and its named dependency):
  - **`api-themes`** (H; after Stage 1a common; moved from 9A, review F04): C160 on 9A's frozen plumbing: the resource and its routes, revisions and restore, the 400 and 422 rules, the contrast gate in `packages/core/src/contrast/` with its vectors, the built-in tokens as hex with a built-in dark palette in `packages/ui/src/styles.css` and their pairs (C160.3, C160.4; reviews F13, H29), assets with their synchronous renditions through Phase 8's vision endpoint, the operator content route and the attestation, resolution, archiving, the publish check and promote copies behind 9A's stubs (`themeSetPublishProblems`, `promoteThemeReferenceInTx`, `resolveThemeForSession`), the flow asset route, and the flow's theme application (C160.9: `apps/flow/src/theme/`, `prefers-color-scheme`, `style-variant`) with the three-engine `theme-csp.spec.ts`; `docs/themes/README.md`. It owns `apps/api/src/services/themes/`, `routes/server/theme-sets.ts`, `routes/flow/theme-assets.ts`, `packages/core/src/contrast/`, `packages/ui/src/styles.css` (for the tokens), `packages/db/src/repositories/{theme-sets,theme-assets}.ts`, `apps/flow/src/theme/`, `apps/flow/e2e/theme-csp.spec.ts` with its own `mock-themes.ts`, and `docs/themes/`.
  - **`api-analytics`** (A; after `analytics-logic`): the rollup SQL in the reporting zone, dirty-day refresh with the watermark, the redacted contributions and the once-a-day refresh of open days' outcome columns (C163.4; H04, P21), the rebuild after a zone change, retention, the six routes with suppression applied after filters, freshness, the read-plan tests on Testcontainers, the benchmark script. It owns `apps/api/src/services/analytics/`, `apps/api/src/jobs/analytics/`, `routes/server/analytics.ts`, `packages/db/src/repositories/analytics.ts` and `apps/api/scripts/analytics-benchmark.ts`.
  - **`api-exports`** (X): exports (job, sealed file, creator-only access, download re-checks, audit shape, masking, the sweep), saved views with the PII-filter rule, the new inquiry and transaction filters, the delivery search, bulk report creation through Phase 6's create service. It owns `apps/api/src/services/{exports,saved-views,list-filters}/`, `apps/api/src/jobs/exports/`, `apps/api/src/jobs/reports/report-bulk.ts`, `routes/server/{exports,reports-bulk,webhook-delivery-search}.ts`, `routes/dashboard/saved-views.ts` and `packages/db/src/repositories/{exports,saved-views,report-bulk-jobs,transaction-filters}.ts`.
  - **`api-access`** (U): environment-scoped permission resolution, the grant checks per environment, `grantable`, invitations resent, reactivation, the MFA default on every creation path, `seed:dev`'s explicit `false` (a borrowed edit of `seed/dev-seed.ts`), the console MFA reset, sessions and revocation, MFA enrolment revoking other sessions, the users list columns, the audit filters and retention. It also builds the organisation-level permissions, the superset and step-up rules and the sign-in environment choice (C167.2, C168.1; reviews P01, P02, F29). It owns `apps/api/src/services/access/`, the user, role, invitation, session and MFA services and dashboard routes for these changes (`services/{users,roles}.ts`, `routes/dashboard/{users,roles,invitations,sessions,mfa-factors,audit-logs,environment}.ts`), `apps/api/src/operator-auth/operator-auth.ts` for the sign-in environment, and `packages/db/src/repositories/{users,roles,audit-logs}.ts` for them.

It owns `apps/api/src/services/api-keys/`, `services/api-keys.ts`, `apps/api/src/webhooks/{attempt-headers,alerts}/`, `routes/server/{api-keys,webhooks}.ts` for these changes, `routes/dashboard/notification-preferences.ts` and `jobs/repeatable/webhook-alerts.ts` (`.gitleaks.toml` is `contracts-9b`'s, review F02).
