It owns `apps/api/src/services/cases/{layout,checklists}/`, `routes/server/case-checklists.ts`, `routes/dashboard/print-audits.ts`, and, for these changes only, the report resource builder, `routes/server/verifications.ts`, `routes/dashboard/trust-anchors.ts` and the case-action availability filter the inventory names.
- **Stage 4: `integration-9b`:** the 9B full-stack specs (C178.4); `docs/analytics/`, `docs/settings/`; the OpenAPI snapshot, `@openverify/node` and the fixtures after Stage 1; CI with job times re-measured, and the theme spec's Firefox and WebKit projects in the `e2e` job, or under a `schedule:` trigger if they cost more than 3 minutes (C160.9; review F19); the full validation; defects fixed test-first; the amendments; a draft of the Phase 9B entry for PROGRESS in its report. It owns `e2e/`, `docs/analytics/`, `docs/settings/`, `docs/api/openapi.json`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `infra/`, `turbo.json` and `docs/plans/phase-9.plan.md`.

**Shared files (Phase 9B):** the 9A list, with 9A's owned files now shared too (except the theme plumbing and `apps/flow/src/theme/`, which `api-themes` owns), plus `packages/core/src/analytics/index.ts` (after Stage 2 starts), the user-session auth plugin, `webhooks/dispatcher.ts` and the response serializer (outside their hook calls), and Phase 8's case transition service.

## Workflow 9C (the console)

Eight agents (the first draft's `dashboard-flow-builder` is split in three: review F04); 9C starts after 9B is pushed. Each Stage 1 agent's first checkpoint lands its routes' `lib/api/` clients and the navigation entries it needs through `dashboard-flow-builder`, which owns the shared dashboard files. Each agent keeps its own console route fixtures in `testing/routes/<area>.ts` (review F11).

- **Stage 1** (parallel):
  - **`dashboard-settings`** builds C170 **first** (the nonce CSP in `proxy.ts`, the report endpoint, the mode setting; its checkpoint lands before the other agents' first UI specs, so every page is built under the real header), then C167.4 (the role editor and per-environment assignments), C168 (invitations resend, reactivate, MFA reset, the users list columns, my sessions, the audit explorer), C169 (key rotation in one dialog with `OneTimeSecretDialog`, age, blocklist; webhook name and description, the previous secret's end time, failure alerts, delivery search; notification preferences; the sender name) and the 9A and 9B settings in the settings form (allowed embed and redirect origins with their validation messages, reporting zone, suppression threshold, retention months, key age, audit retention). It owns `src/proxy.ts`, `next.config.ts`, `app/csp-reports/`, `app/roles/`, `app/users/`, `app/account/`, `app/api-keys/`, `app/webhooks/`, `app/audit-logs/`, `app/settings/`, `app/notifications/`, `lib/api/{roles,users,sessions,api-keys,webhooks,notifications,settings}.ts`, `e2e/tests/ui-settings.spec.ts`, `e2e/tests/ui-csp.spec.ts` and `e2e/tests/support/ui-settings.ts`.
  - **`dashboard-flow-builder`** builds C155 (the shared editor refactor with the workflow editor's tests green, the flow adapter, outline, canvas, palette, modules, screen editor, validation in place with each fact's tier, stage and source, versions and diff, publish with acknowledgements, restore, copy, promote, used by, deployments), the `trigger-flow` label in the workflow editor's trigger picker (C150.12; review F05), and C155.9's typed catalog. **Its first checkpoint** (verification round V07) is the `GraphAdapter` refactor with the workflow editor's tests green, the empty step-panel slot the template forms mount in, and the overlay slot in `app/workflows/` and the workflow canvas that `dashboard-preview` fills; `dashboard-preview`'s overlay slice and `dashboard-template-forms`' mount start only after it (their other slices start at once, against the contracts). It owns every shared dashboard file (the navigation, layout, `package.json` and lockfile changes on request), `components/graph-editor/`, `components/workflows/` (except the overlay files `dashboard-preview` adds in `components/workflows/overlay/`), `app/workflows/` (V07), `components/flow-builder/`, `app/templates/` except `app/templates/[id]/{translations,theme,settings,preview}/`, `lib/copy/`, `lib/api/{inquiry-templates,inquiry-template-versions,deployments}.ts`, `e2e/tests/ui-flow-builder.spec.ts` and its support file.
  - **`dashboard-template-forms`** builds C153.1 and C153.3–C153.4 (`SchemaForm` and the check panels with the branches that read each check; settings forms; report template structured forms and the create-template form, K14; signal and list config forms), mounted in the builder's step panels through the slot of `dashboard-flow-builder`'s first checkpoint (V07). It owns `components/schema-form/`, `components/check-panels/`, `app/templates/[id]/settings/`, `app/report-templates/`, `e2e/tests/ui-template-forms.spec.ts` and its support file.
  - **`dashboard-preview`** builds C156.2 and C156.4–C156.6 on the console side (the preview frame and its protocol with the asset message, simulate with presets, the workflow canvas overlay, K4). Its overlay slice starts after `dashboard-flow-builder`'s first checkpoint and mounts through its overlay slot (V07). It owns `components/flow-preview/`, `components/workflows/overlay/`, `app/templates/[id]/preview/`, `lib/api/simulation-presets.ts`, `e2e/tests/ui-preview.spec.ts` and its support file.
  - **`dashboard-themes-copy`** builds C160 in the console (theme sets, revisions and restore, the editor with live contrast from `packages/core/src/contrast/`, assets with the attestation, the default set, archiving), C162 (the translations editor per locale and key, CSV import and export with row and column problems, the consent-copy rule shown), C161.5's review status shown wherever a locale is enabled, and the template's theme and translations tabs. It owns `app/theme-sets/`, `components/themes/`, `components/translations/`, `app/templates/[id]/{translations,theme}/`, `lib/api/{theme-sets,translations}.ts`, `e2e/tests/ui-themes-copy.spec.ts` and its support file.
  - **`dashboard-analytics`** builds C164.9 (Overview, Funnel, Verifications, Checks, Cases, Signals with visx, captions, tables and suppression marks), C165 in the console (saved views on every list C165.1 names, through a slot each list page gains by a borrowed one-line edit, and the new inquiry and transaction filters, for which it owns the filter components of `app/inquiries/` and `app/transactions/`; review F11), C166 (exports per type with the personal-data confirmation, the exports list, bulk report creation). It owns `app/analytics/`, `components/analytics/`, `components/saved-views/`, `app/exports/`, `lib/api/{analytics,exports,saved-views,reports-bulk}.ts`, `e2e/tests/ui-analytics.spec.ts` and its support file.
  - **`dashboard-cases-reports`** builds C172 in the console (the layout editor with keyboard and single-pointer alternatives, checklists on the case page, the status switcher, preset comments, action placements) and C173 (the inquiry document's reports, `FormDialogButton` options, the coverage words, the "Other lists" check, the verification files, the print views and their audit call, the trust-store flags). It owns `components/cases/layout/`, `components/cases/checklists/`, the case template version editor's layout and checklist tabs, `app/inquiries/[id]/` (the inquiry document's reports and print view; review F11), `app/reports/`, `app/verifications/`, `app/trust-anchors/`, the print view components, `lib/api/{case-checklists,print-audits}.ts`, `e2e/tests/ui-cases-reports.spec.ts` and its support file, and, for these changes only, the case page files Phase 8's `dashboard-reviewer` created.
- **Stage 2: `integration-9c`:** the 9C UI specs on the shards; `expectAccessible` once per console page; the real-header CSP test across every page with zero violations; the dashboard README sections; CI job times re-measured and the shards rebalanced; the full validation; defects fixed test-first; a draft of the Phase 9C entry for PROGRESS. It owns `e2e/` except the `ui-*` specs and their support files, `apps/dashboard/README.md`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `infra/` and `docs/plans/phase-9.plan.md`.

**Shared files (Phase 9C):** every shared dashboard file is `dashboard-flow-builder`'s (it adds the others' navigation entries when asked); the list pages that gain a saved-views slot are edited once each by `dashboard-analytics` for the slot only; `proxy.ts` and `next.config.ts` are `dashboard-settings`'.

## Workflow 9D (audit, review, fixes)

Nine agents; 9D starts after 9C is pushed.

- **Stage 1: `a11y-audit`** (alone): C171 end to end: the WCAG-EM sample in `docs/accessibility/audit-<date>.md`; the automated runs over every flow step state in the five locales, both pseudo-locales, light and dark, hosted and embedded, at the three widths, run once as the full cross product in shards that each fit a 10-minute call, with the timing recorded (C171.2; review F16); the own tests of C171.3 that earlier agents did not land; every defect found, fixed test-first in `apps/flow`, `packages/ui` and `apps/dashboard`; the manual checklist prepared for the person who runs it, with the results table empty; the statement (C171.6), dated and claiming only what is evidenced. It owns `docs/accessibility/`, `e2e/a11y-exceptions.json` and, for its fixes, any path in `apps/flow`, `packages/ui` and `apps/dashboard`.
- **Stage 2: review of all of Phase 9** (read-only, parallel):
  - **privacy, security and law:** the preview and embed origins and messages, the flow server's headers and its lookup, both CSPs, the internal token; server-side branching (what a condition may read, the projection leaking nothing, PII in conditions and logs); theme injection, assets and fonts; operator copy, consent texts and their review status; analytics suppression, exports and their access, saved views; environment-scoped roles and escalation; MFA defaults and resets; key blocklists and rotation; alerts and notifications; GDPR, UK GDPR and biometric law where consent texts, adverse paths or analytics touch them;
  - **correctness and honesty:** the engine's races, caps and required verifications; nullable facts and `onNull`; fallbacks and reviews; the v1 projection against today's Android SDK; migrations; metric definitions, denominators, time zones, sandbox separation and freshness; contrast figures; the preview against the live flow; translations marked as they are; coverage of the F, T, A, S and K rows;
  - **operations, licences and performance:** the rollup job's cost and the benchmark; CI job times and shards; the PGlite migration time; dependency licences and ages (the release-age record included); the flow server under load; the three-engine spec's cost;
  - **console and accessibility:** React and the RSC payload; masking in the console; the editor, layout editor and charts by keyboard and screen reader; the real-header CSP tests; the audit's method and statement.
- **Stage 3:** one verifier for every CRITICAL and HIGH finding. It records the 9D amendments.
- **Stage 4:** path-owned fixers:
  - **`fix-api`:** `apps/api`, `packages/` except `packages/flow-spec`, `packages/sdk-js` and `packages/ui`, `services/vision`, `e2e/` except the `ui-*` specs and their helpers, `docs/` except `docs/accessibility`, CI, `infra` and `.dockerignore`; it measures the e2e suite and the CI jobs again and adjusts the shards;
  - **`fix-flow`:** `apps/flow`, `packages/flow-spec`, `packages/sdk-js`, `packages/ui`;
  - **`fix-console`:** `apps/dashboard`, the `ui-*` specs with their helpers, `docs/accessibility`.

## Rules for every agent

- **TDD:** RED first, seen failing for the intended reason; the RED commit compiles and typechecks; then GREEN; then refactor on green. Evidence, with the commands run and their output, goes in `docs/tdd/phase9-<agent>.tdd.md`.
- **Shared files:** builders never edit the shared-file lists above. Route schemas, the OpenAPI snapshot, `@openverify/node`, the API fixtures and the Android fixture list change only through the Stage 1a or integration agent. A missing hook or schema change is a `contractDeviation`. Dependencies land only in a Stage 1a (9A: the axe packages; 9B: visx) or through `dashboard-flow-builder` on request.
- **Shared resources:**
  - commits are path-scoped under `flock /tmp/openverify-git.lock`, after `git diff --cached --quiet`;
  - the full-stack e2e runs under `flock /tmp/openverify-e2e.lock`, as the mocked flow suite plus shards on the reusable stack, with the load average written beside every timing;
  - long gates run in the foreground with a 600,000 ms timeout and are split so each fits one call; only the stack start may run in the background;
  - flaky tests are made deterministic, never retried; tests assert order only on keys the code orders by; time comes from the injected `Clock`; tests that order rows stamp `created_at`.
- **CI cadence** (owner, 2026-10-28 and 2026-10-29): Workflow agents never push. The orchestrator pushes `main` once per Workflow after a green full local validation, never for a single fix; there are no check branches or pull requests; only failed jobs are re-run; the next Workflow starts after the push without waiting for CI.
- **No network in tests:** no outside call from any test; the vision service and the flow server run locally; the pinned `scripts/fetch_*` downloads CI runs before tests are allowed.
- **Never code in flows:** no operator-written text (conditions, visibility rules, copy, patterns) reaches `eval`, `Function`, a template compiler or a module loader; patterns run only through the restricted grammar with the input cap (C148.3).
- **Honesty (ADR 0002, 0005, 0006, 0007, 0008):**
  - an unknown fact is `null` with its route, never `false`; a step that cannot be rendered is unsupported, never skipped; a failed verification is never counted passed, whatever path follows;
  - no flow passes a check; a path that fails a person on anything but a verification is acknowledged and recorded;
  - every analytics figure states its definition, denominator, time zone and freshness, and suppression is never bypassed;
  - no translation is called reviewed without a record; no accessibility conformance is claimed beyond the evidence;
  - every constant is provisional, with a basis; no limit is tuned on test data.
- **PII:** no field value, screen submission, initial field, consent text choice, export row, preset value, IP address or token may appear in logs (outside `pii`), request URLs, stored API logs read without `pii:view`, the step record, rollups, events, `postMessage` traffic, metadata, problems or git. Synthetic data only; no real face except the existing public-domain fixtures.
- **Origins:** every `postMessage` names its exact target origin and checks `origin` and `source`; every framing decision is a response header computed per request, failing closed.
- **Licences and versions:** rule 4 (MPL-2.0 only as unmodified tooling; AGPL, GPL and LGPL fail even as tooling; OFL-1.1 fonts are not shipped); every version at least 14 days old, enforced by `minimumReleaseAge` from 9A Stage 1a, with no exclusions; no new build script without an `allowBuilds` decision.
- **Android:** the only Android edits of Phase 9 are the fixture list of `ContractFixturesTest.kt` (Stage 1a) and one test-only file, the `FlowCoordinator` host test of C151.6 (`android/openverify-sdk/src/androidHostTest/kotlin/dev/openverify/sdk/flow/FlowCoordinatorProjectionTest.kt`, beside the existing `FakeFlowApi`, written by `contracts-9a` with the projection fixtures; reviews H01, F01; decision 73); no production Kotlin changes; every Android suite stays green; new session members must decode in today's SDK, and today's SDK must never report `Completed` before the server's position is a completion step (C178.1).
- **Content:** no Persona name, branding or copied text in product UI, docs, translations or messages.

## Validation

```bash
pnpm install --frozen-lockfile && pnpm format:check
pnpm turbo run lint typecheck --force
pnpm test                                     # every package >= 80%; flow-spec v2, contrast and analytics vectors, plural completeness and migration goldens run here
pnpm test:integration                         # flow engine races, run_workflow waits, rollup refresh and rebuild, suppression on rollups, environment-scoped permissions, exports
python3 scripts/fetch_models.py && python3 scripts/fetch_data.py
(cd services/vision && uv run ruff check && uv run ruff format --check && uv run mypy src tools && uv run pytest)
(cd android && ./gradlew testDebugUnitTest testAndroidHostTest koverVerify assembleDebug :openverify-sdk:assemble lint)   # the SDK decodes every new fixture
pnpm --filter @openverify/flow e2e           # the mocked flow suite: axe on C171.2's matrix, RTL, pseudo-locales, preview and embed messages, the theme CSP spec (Chromium locally; Firefox and WebKit in CI unless the owner installs them, C160.9)
pnpm --filter @openverify/js test            # the embed client, its size budget included
OPENVERIFY_E2E_REUSE_STACK=1 pnpm --filter @openverify/e2e e2e:stack:up     # background allowed
for i in $(seq 1 $N); do OPENVERIFY_E2E_REUSE_STACK=1 pnpm --filter @openverify/e2e exec playwright test --shard=$i/$N; done
pnpm --filter @openverify/e2e e2e:stack:down
docker build -f infra/docker/apps.Dockerfile . && docker build services/vision
for t in infra/tests/*.test.sh; do bash "$t"; done   # includes the flow server's per-entry headers
pnpm --filter @openverify/api analytics:benchmark --seed      # reported, not gated; each phase fits one call
pnpm --filter @openverify/api analytics:benchmark --measure
gitleaks git --redact .                       # with the API-key token rule
```

## Risks

| Risk | Likelihood | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| A client picks the low-friction path, or skips a verification | Med | The server decides every step and the required verifications from the path taken; current-step refusals on every flow route; no condition ever sent to a client; race tests; facts a client reports or a person enters are marked by source, and a route that lowers friction on one needs `asserted-fact:` in production (C150, C150.10, C151) |
| A condition over a missing fact silently takes a branch | Med | Publish-time typing; nullable facts need an explicit `onNull` with no default; strict evaluators; `scope_path_missing` goes to review; golden vectors per position (C150.3, C150.4) |
| Today's Android SDK breaks on v2 flows, or reports a false "Completed" | High (the first draft's projection did, reviews H01, F01) | The v1 projection lists the exited steps, the current step and the unconditional run after it, ending at a terminal step or a placeholder the SDK decodes as `UnsupportedStep`; no conditions; a `FlowCoordinator` host test drives the SDK over successive projections and asserts no `Completed` before the server's completion; the SDK decodes the new fixtures in CI; v2-only steps documented as needing Phase 10 (C151.6, C178.1) |
| Migrating v1 flows changes an outcome | Med | Lossless migration with goldens for every v1 fixture; stored rows never rewritten; the equivalence test runs both engines on every v1 fixture (C147.10, C151.3) |
| The preview or embed leaks data or is framed by a hostile page | Med | The preview makes no network call (`connect-src 'none'`), accepts messages only from the console origin and its parent; per-request `frame-ancestors` failing closed; exact-origin messages without PII; the stated limits (C156, C157, C159) |
| A theme injects style or hides text | Low | Hex-only tokens through `setProperty`, never `cssText`; the contrast gate on every save in both variants; raster logos re-encoded; no SVG; no remote fonts; the three-engine CSP spec (C160) |
| Operator copy misleads or changes a consent | Low | Plain text on declared keys; bidi overrides refused; consent copy in every enabled locale; the diff flags consent changes; the consent record names the version, keys and locale, and the digests of the exact words shown, checked by the server against the stored texts with the session's placeholder values (C162.4, C162.9) |
| Translations are wrong or presented as reviewed | Med | Every built-in translation ships `machine-drafted` with an acknowledgement for enabling one whose built-in legal texts are shown; review status shown, with per-key digests downgrading a reviewed locale when a key changes; plural completeness and pseudo-locale tests catch structural faults, not meaning (C161.5) |
| Analytics identifies a person or misleads | Med | Suppression with complementary cells after every filter; written definitions; denominators always shown; one environment per answer; freshness served; what suppression does not cover stated (C163, C164) |
| Exports leak personal data | Med | Per-type permissions admin-only by default; `pii:view` for personal columns; creator-only 24-hour files; download re-checks; audit of the filter's shape only; the never-included list (C166) |
| Environment-scoped roles open an escalation | Med | Grants checked per environment named; admin organisation-wide only; `users:*`, `roles:*` and the organisation settings (`SETTING_LEVEL`) effective only organisation-wide; acting on another user needs a superset of their permissions, and a reset or reactivation a recent second factor; production still needs `environments:production`; refusal tests per path (C167.2, C168.1) |
| `require-mfa` by default locks the dev seed or e2e out | Med | `seed:dev` sets `false` explicitly and says so; `--mfa-admin` keeps the enforced path tested; existing organisations unchanged (C168.2) |
| The enforced console CSP breaks a page | Med | Built first in 9C so every page is developed under it; real-header tests on every page; client-only rendering of style-setting libraries; the report-only mode for operators (C170) |
| Rollups cost too much or lag | Med | Dirty-day recompute measured at 1 M inquiries (2.7 s for 48 hours of inquiry buckets on one CPU); a 5-minute cadence with freshness served; the benchmark re-run in 9B (C163.4, C163.7) |
| The PGlite migration grows past the db tests' hook budget | High | Each Stage 1b measures before and after; the migrated database cache of Phase 6; one migration per Workflow; fall back to Testcontainers for the new repositories (Phase 8 F29) |
| CI jobs time out as suites grow (Phase 8 found unit-api within a minute of its limit before Workflow 6B sharded it) | High | `foundation-9a` measures and splits jobs before Stage 2; each integration agent re-measures; the axe pass in its own shard; the three-engine spec under a `schedule:` trigger in `ci.yml` if it costs more than 3 minutes (C160.9) |
| The e2e suite outgrows its shards | High | The reusable stack and shard matrix; each integration agent measures and rebalances; most accessibility coverage in the mocked suite |
| The manual accessibility audit is not done before the release | Med | The checklist and its empty results table ship with the phase; until a person runs it the statement says "not yet evaluated for conformance" and claims nothing; Phase 9 is recorded with the manual part not run, carried to the release gate; VoiceOver recorded untested without a device (C171.5, C171.6; review H12) |
| Phase 7 or Phase 8 lands differently from its plan | Med | Each Stage 1a takes the inventory and records amendments before any Phase 9 code; the scope offers only what Phase 7 as built computes in time (C150.2) |
| A Stage 1a or Stage 1b does not finish | Med | Plumbing only; checkpoints by family; Stage 2 beside Stage 1b; each Stage 3 builder waits only for its family's checkpoint and its named Stage 2 dependency |
| A young dependency enters the lockfile again | Low | `minimumReleaseAge: 20160` set in 9A Stage 1a with no exclusions; the dated record of decision 62 |

## Acceptance

- [ ] **Format (C147–C149):** v1 and v2 parse with pointers; every v1 fixture migrates to its golden v2 form; every graph, component, pattern and field rule refuses its golden invalid flow; field keys stay immutable; prefill only through the API; comparisons read initial values and say so.
- [ ] **Branching (C150):** every scope path is typed; nullable facts never reach a boolean position and every route reading one declares `onNull`; waits resolve or fall to `onNull`; fallbacks go to review unless acknowledged; adverse paths are acknowledged, carry a basis and contest route in production and write a decision record, with Phase 8's biometric cases and consent check; a missing path goes to review; an errored check never takes a "not failed" route (golden test, C150.2); signal branches read provisional snapshots, and a non-null one chooses a route end to end; every fact shows its source and tier; nothing is evaluated on a client except same-screen visibility.
- [ ] **Engine (C151, C152):** the server decides every step; only the current step accepts input; required verifications follow the path; v1 flows, branching ones included, reach the same paths and outcomes; the v1 projection carries no condition, today's Android SDK decodes every new fixture, and its host test shows no `Completed` before the server's completion step; a completion without evidence is acknowledged and visible in `flow-outcome`; actions run exactly once per entry, wait within their timeout and take `error` on failure; caps halt loops; the race tests pass.
- [ ] **Templates (C153, C154):** every member has a generated form with tiers shown; drafts save with problems and publish refuses them; ETag and `If-Match` give 412; validate, diff, restore, copy, used-by, promote and deployments work with their events; acknowledgements are stored and audited.
- [ ] **Builder and preview (C155, C156):** every builder operation works by keyboard through the outline; the preview renders from posted documents with no network access and refuses foreign messages; simulate reproduces the engine's path; the workflow overlay draws test runs.
- [ ] **Serving and embedding (C157–C159):** each entry sends its headers; embedding works on an allowed origin and is refused elsewhere, failing closed; `localhost` only in sandbox; redirects carry only `inquiry-id`, and `status` only when the version opts in (C158.5); `@openverify/js` stays within its size budget, checks origins both ways and sends no PII.
- [ ] **Themes (C160):** the gate refuses failing pairs with their ratios; the vectors hold; the built-in tokens pass through the same function; assets are typed, capped and re-encoded; edits are live, revisioned and audited; the built-in light and dark palettes pass the gate; no CSP violation in three engines (Firefox and WebKit in CI).
- [ ] **Languages and copy (C161, C162):** five locales typed and complete; RTL with `<bdi>`, mirrored icons and unmirrored pose prompts; digits normalised; plurals complete; pseudo-locales pass at 320 px; review status shown and acknowledged; copy validated, consent copy complete, translations JSON and CSV round-trip without formula injection.
- [ ] **Analytics and data (C163–C166):** rollups refresh by dirty day in the reporting zone and survive redaction; every metric matches its definition on a seeded population; suppression and complementary suppression hold in every view and download; saved views refuse PII filters; exports respect permissions, masking, ownership and expiry; bulk report creation reports per-row outcomes.
- [ ] **Settings (C167–C170):** roles act only in their environments and nobody grants what they lack; invitations resend, users reactivate, MFA defaults on for new organisations, resets and sessions work and are audited; the audit explorer filters by IP only with `pii:view`; keys rotate in one call and blocklists strip attributes; webhooks send attempt headers, alert on failures and are searchable; the console CSP is enforced with zero violations on every page.
- [ ] **Accessibility (C171):** axe with `wcag22aa` passes at every flow state of C171.2's CI matrix and every console page with only dated exceptions, and the full cross product once in 9D; the own tests for 2.4.11, 2.5.7, 3.2.6, 3.3.7, 3.3.8 and 2.2.1 (ten extensions, no timed redirect) pass; the audit report and the statement exist, the statement saying "not yet evaluated for conformance" until the manual pass is run, and the manual checklist is ready for a person. Phase 9 is recorded as "WCAG 2.2 AA audit: automated part done, manual part not run, no conformance claimed" (review H12).
- [ ] **Carried (C172, C173):** layout editor, checklists, the switcher rule, preset comments and action placements; reports, verification files, print views, the trust-store flags, the "Other lists" check and the coverage words.
- [ ] **End to end (C178.4):** the 9A, 9B and 9C specs pass on the shards.
- [ ] **Deferred (C177):** every deferral is listed in `docs/PROGRESS.md` under "Carried forward".
- [ ] **Records (C176):** ADR 0008, the PLAN, surface-index and licence corrections, the release-age record, and the docs are written.
- [ ] **Process:** every existing suite stays green at each checkpoint and stage; every validation command passes locally and on GitHub (the Firefox and WebKit theme spec in CI only unless the owner installs those browsers locally, C160.9); each agent writes its `docs/tdd/phase9-*.tdd.md`; `docs/PROGRESS.md` records Phase 9, with the manual accessibility audit as an open item (C171.6).

## Decisions for the owner (made here; confirm or change)

Each item states the default the build uses until the owner decides, so none blocks a Workflow, and its source: the behaviour note's owner questions (B1–B13), the engineering note's (E1–E14), or this plan. The owner's standing approval is to take the industry-best default and record it; where a note's default conflicts with a binding rule or with another note, the item says so and why.

**The flow and templates**
1. **Template scope** (B1). Default: environment-scoped templates with promote (C154.5), as Phase 8 decided for workflows; the public organisation-level templates would bind production inquiries to sandbox providers, which ADR 0002 forbids.
2. **Server-side branching** (B2, E3). Default: flow-spec v2 decided on the server; clients render the step named; `OpenVerify-Flow-Spec` negotiation (absent = 1); today's Android SDK served the v1 projection (the exited steps, the current step and the unconditional run after it, ending at a terminal step or at a placeholder it decodes as unsupported; no conditions; review H01, F01), with v2-only steps showing its unsupported screen until Phase 10 implements C175.1 in `commonMain`, and a host test proving it never reports `Completed` early.
3. **Strict evaluation** (E3; the task's rule). Default: an unknown path is refused at publish; a nullable fact needs an explicit `onNull` with no default; a path missing at run time never takes `else`: it ends the person's part and sends the inquiry to review (review H08); `onNull: "review"` and Phase 7's null classes; one strict visibility evaluator in `packages/flow-spec`; never a silent branch.
4. **The v2 format.** Default: steps and port-labelled edges like Phase 8's definitions (one adapter for both editors), camelCase members as in v1, modules as editor macros, stored rows never rewritten (engineering note 3.6's upcaster).
5. **The condition dialect.** Default: Phase 8's C114 JSONLogic dialect (ours), one evaluator on the server; the public serialisation is not documented.
6. **Retries and fallbacks.** Default: `maxAttempts` stays the bound; an optional `exhausted` port; a fallback path sends the inquiry to review unless the author acknowledges `onFallback: "continue"`; the failed verification stays failed.
7. **One step per verification type.** Default: kept (C11's rule); several steps of one type on exclusive branches later.
8. **Cycles.** Default: only through screens and branches; 20 entries per step and 200 moves per inquiry, then `halted`.
9. **Adverse paths** (behaviour note F10). Default: a failure reachable on non-verification facts needs an acknowledgement, and in production Phase 8's basis and contest route, with a decision record; the builder offers review first (not legal advice).
10. **Flow actions** (K1, K2). Default: a closed list; no code or HTTPS in flows; `run_workflow` through a new `trigger-flow` type, bounded at 30 s by default and 300 s at most, with a mandatory `error` edge; the template publisher must hold the workflow's grant.
11. **Drafts.** Default: drafts save with problems under `meta.allow-invalid` (the console's mode; API clients keep the 422); `If-Match` with 412; publish refuses an invalid draft.
12. **Restore.** Default: creates a draft that must be published (the public pages disagree; Phase 8 decision 19's reasoning).
13. **Deployments.** Default: a read-only `dply_` history with the public `deployment.completed`; publishing is deploying to the template's environment; no weighted splits, criteria or undeploy (behaviour note F4).
14. **Prefill** (B3). Default: through the API only; no `fields` in URLs or the embed client.
15. **Redirect payload** (B3). Default: `inquiry-id` only, plus `status` when the version opts in (`redirect-include-status`, review P32), `status` documented as non-authoritative; exact origin allowlist with the shared wildcard matcher; custom schemes per template with a denylist; a "Continue" button, never a timed redirect (review H13).
16. **Template fields.** Default: typed `field-schemas` on the version (public subset), every value still masked without `pii:view`, initial values sealed and used by the comparison checks.
17. **Preview** (E2). Default: the credential-free `postMessage` preview in both environments (ours: it creates no inquiry and holds synthetic values only); "test in sandbox" with draft-pinned inquiries later, behind a flag.
18. **The flow server** (E13). Default: a new Node process in compose's `app` profile; `vite preview` for development only; Phase 11 makes its production image.
19. **Embedding** (S2). Default: per-environment origins narrowed per template, sent as `frame-ancestors` per request by the flow server, failing closed; `localhost` in sandbox only; drafts never embed; exact-origin messages.
20. **The embed client** (B11, E1). Default: a minimal `@openverify/js` in Phase 9 (an allowlist enforces nothing without it); no template-id start, no fields; the public names without a gap row disposed of in C179 (review H23); npm publication, `sdk-react` and `apps/demo-web` in Phase 11.
21. **Components.** Default: the C148 catalogue; File upload, Home, List Container, Device Handoff Button, ID type select, country multi select, the bank component and address autocomplete later (no permissive maps provider).
22. **Capture methods by device class.** Default: client-reported guidance, labelled so; the production camera rules stay where they are.
23. **Limits** (behaviour note F28). Default: 200 steps, 400 edges, 20 routes, 40 components per screen, 1,000 fields (ours, provisional); no plan tiers.

**Themes**
24. **Theme sets per environment** (B5; this plan departs from the note's organisation-level default). Default: environment-scoped, copied by name on promote. Reason: the public organisation-level sets would let a sandbox key or a sandbox-only operator restyle production flows (a logo swapped for a phishing mark), the environment isolation that the note itself gives as the reason for environment-scoped templates.
25. **Edits apply live** (B12). Default: live after the contrast gate, every save an audited revision, restore available; the session names the revision.
26. **Tokens and the gate.** Default: hex-only palettes in a closed token set; WCAG 2.2 AA pairs refused below their thresholds, never rounded; AAA pairs warn.
27. **Fonts** (E4, B8). Default: no bundled font (OFL-1.1 is not on rule 4's list, and no exception is granted); the system stacks; operator-uploaded WOFF2 under the operator's licence attestation. An OFL-1.1 exception for unmodified font files would be needed before any shipped web font or server PDF with Arabic text.
28. **Logos** (E5). Default: PNG, JPEG and WebP, re-encoded; no SVG (no sanitiser or DOM library in the API's runtime).
29. **A separate theme permission** (E11). Default: yes, `theme-sets:write` (the public resource's name), so a reviewer cannot restyle a consent screen.

**Languages and copy**
30. **Locales.** Default: `en`, `es`, `fr`, `de`, `ar` (PLAN §6), RFC 4647 lookup on base tags, full tags for formatting, `language` as the public parameter with `lang` kept.
31. **Who translates** (B4). Default: the build agents draft `fr`, `de` and `ar` (and `es` is treated the same unless a human review is on record), which is machine translation, and every non-source locale ships `machine-drafted`; enabling one whose built-in legal texts are shown needs `locale-unreviewed:<locale>` in production; a fluent reviewer's sign-off is recorded with a name, a date and the catalog digest, and a key changed later downgrades the locale to `partially-reviewed` (not legal advice; review H14). Whether to ship machine-drafted defaults at all is decision 67.
32. **Operator copy.** Default: plain text on declared keys with `{name}` placeholders; no HTML, Markdown or ICU; consent copy in every enabled locale; bidi overrides refused.
33. **The console's language** (E6). Default: English; Phase 9's new strings and the editor's library strings in a typed English catalog; next-intl not adopted (install scripts, young unattributed dependencies).
34. **Machine translation** (behaviour note T12). Default: not built.
35. **Pseudo-locales.** Default: `en-XA` and `ar-XB` by our own transform, in tests and the preview only.

**Analytics and data**
36. **Rollups and the reporting zone** (E10). Default: daily rollups in each environment's IANA reporting zone (default UTC), refreshed every 5 minutes by dirty day, rebuilt on a zone change.
37. **Suppression** (E10). Default: counts below the threshold hidden as "fewer than <threshold>" (11 by default), rates only with denominator, numerator and complement at the threshold and otherwise as bounds computed to contain the true value, complementary suppression within an answer and along the time axis; floor 11, raisable, never lowered (reviews H18, P21).
38. **Retention** (B10). Default: step records, action rows and signal snapshots follow their inquiry's retention and redaction; rollups 13 months (13–60 configurable; the engineering note's 25 months is within reach as a setting); inquiry rollups survive redaction because they hold no end user's data and a redaction records the inquiry's contribution before it clears anything (C163.4; review H04, verification round); the per-reviewer rollup is employee personal data with its own retention and is deleted or re-keyed with its user (review P20).
39. **What is measured.** Default: server-observed facts only (the step record), never client-reported screen views.
40. **Not built.** Default: no third-party event forwarding, no SQL explorer (a read-only replica role documented), no availability metrics.
41. **Reviewer analytics.** Default: per-reviewer distributions only for readers who also hold `users:write` (not `users:read`, which every viewer holds; review P20), never exported by name, with an employee notice in the docs.
42. **Charts.** Default: visx primitives (MIT), each with a caption and a table; Recharts not adopted (its default `role="application"`), nor chart.js or ECharts.
43. **Exports.** Default: one CSV per export, 24-hour sealed files, creator-only, at most 50,000 rows (provisional), per-type permissions held by admins by default.
44. **Saved views.** Default: personal, shareable within the environment; filters on PII attributes refused.

**Settings**
45. **MFA by default** (B6). Default: `require-mfa` on for organisations created from Phase 9; existing organisations unchanged; `seed:dev` explicitly off; TOTP and WebAuthn only.
46. **MFA recovery by admins** (behaviour note S14). Default: a console reset that removes factors and revokes sessions, instead of never-expiring admin codes.
47. **Invitations.** Default: 7 days (ours; public 14), with resend and reactivation.
48. **SSO** (B7). Default: not in Phase 9; OIDC sign-in in Phase 11, SAML and SCIM after, with the library and licence checks then.
49. **Roles per environment.** Default: assignments per environment or for all current and future ones; `admin` organisation-wide only; per-template constraints and a finer `pii:view` later.
50. **API keys.** Default: one-call rotation, a 90-day age warning, attribute blocklists; template-restricted keys, inflection and payload filters later.
51. **The console's CSP.** Default: the nonce policy **enforced** by the end of Phase 9, with a report-only mode as an operator setting. This departs from the engineering note's "report-only for one release": nothing has been released, so there is no installed base to observe, and real-header tests on every page replace the observation period.
52. **Webhooks.** Default: keep the 8-attempt, 24-hour schedule (documented against the public 7 over about 51 hours); attempt headers; failure alerts; names and descriptions optional in the API; OAuth client credentials later; a per-environment sender display name only.

**Accessibility**
53. **axe** (E9). Default: `axe-core` and `@axe-core/playwright` 4.14.0 as unmodified dev dependencies under rule 4's tooling exception, listed in §5.
54. **Excluded tools.** Default: pa11y and pa11y-ci (LGPL-3.0-only fails rule 4 even as tooling); `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y` not adopted while its peer range stops at ESLint 9.
55. **The manual audit** (E7). Default: a person (the owner or a contracted auditor) runs the checklist with NVDA, TalkBack and, on an iPhone or Mac the owner arranges, VoiceOver; without a device, VoiceOver is recorded as untested in the statement; no agent claims a manual result.
56. **Assistive technologies and rule 4** (E8). Default: rule 4 does not reach products a person uses manually during the audit (NVDA is GPL), as they are never in the repository, CI or a shipped artifact.
57. **2.2.1 and the capture limit** (E14). Default: a warning 20 s ahead and a server extension route, usable at least ten times and outside the cap of 10 (review H11), in the web flow now and on Android in Phase 10.

**Carried items**
58. **Teams and availability** (B9). Default: the next release; case analytics ships without availability metrics.
59. **Print** (B8). Default: browser print views with audit entries now; the public `print` routes and a server-made PDF when a font exception exists, because a PDF that drops characters of a name misrepresents the record.
60. **The session map** (K11). Default: deferred again; still no permissive map tiles without network calls.
61. **Every carried item, placed:** K1 → C152.2; K2 → C150.2 and C152.2; K3 → C150.2 and C150.9 (provisional signals only; review H02); K4 → C156.6; K5 → C172.1–C172.5; K6 → decision 58; K7 → C164.5; K8 → C164.6; K9 → C166.3; K10 → C165.3; K11 → decision 60; K12 → C173.3 (views) and decision 59 (routes and PDF); K13 → C166.5; K14 → C153.4; K15 → C173.1; K16 → C170; K17 → C173.2; K18 → C173.4; K19 and K20 (found by this plan) → C173.1; and the engineering note's own carries: the axe check → C171.2, the 2.2.1 warning → C171.4, the flow's response headers → C157, the editor's `ariaLabelConfig` strings → C155.9.

**Records and process**
62. **The release-age deviation** (E12; the engineering fact-check's addendum). Recorded with dates: `i18next` 26.5.2 (published 2026-10-17T21:24Z) and `react-i18next` 17.1.0 (2026-10-17T19:01Z) entered `apps/flow/package.json` in `86fa58f5`, and `eslint` 10.13.0 (2026-10-16T14:24Z) entered the catalog in `28fce4eb`, both on 2026-10-20, 3–4 days after release. They pass the 14-day rule on 2026-10-31 at 21:24Z, 2026-10-31 at 19:01Z and 2026-10-30 at 14:24Z respectively, all before Phase 9's base, so no pin moves. Default: `minimumReleaseAge: 20160` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` from 9A Stage 1a, with the existing no-exclusions rule, so the convention becomes configuration; the record goes into `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`'s version-age note and `docs/PROGRESS.md`.
63. **Correct the records** (B13). Default: `integration-9a` makes C176's corrections, including the stale `phase-2.plan.md:921`.
64. **ADR 0008.** Default: written by `contracts-9a` in 9A Stage 1a.
65. **Four Workflows.** Default: 9A (10 agents), 9B (10), 9C (8), 9D (9), each pushed once after a full local validation (rebalanced in review F04: the theme builder moves to 9B, `api-flow-actions` joins 9A, and the console builder is split in three).
66. **Fact-check corrections.** Default: taken as corrected (the 88-property version theme; SVG as an image is restricted only in some browsers, which decision 28 makes moot; the 48-hour inquiry recompute of 2.7 s).

**From the review** (each with its default; `docs/plans/phase-9-review-resolution.md` gives the reasons)

67. **Machine-drafted translations as shipped defaults** (review H14; the behaviour note's owner question 4 started from "machine translation is not allowed as a shipped default"). Default: ship `es`, `fr`, `de` and `ar` as `machine-drafted`, labelled so in the console, the docs and the review table, with `locale-unreviewed:<locale>` for every built-in legal text in production, until a fluent person reviews each catalog. The alternative is to ship only `en` until reviews exist, which would leave PLAN §6's five locales unmet. This contradicts T12's reason only in part: T12 refuses machine translation of operator copy at run time, which stays unbuilt.
68. **Provisional signals in flows** (review H02). Default: a Phase 7 amendment (C150.9): flow branches read only `flow-provisional` signals, computed from the session facts stored so far while the branch waits, and recorded as snapshots; the inquiry's own signals stay as C90.5 has them. The alternative, refusing every signal in flows and deferring K3 again, was not taken, because branching on signals is what dynamic friction means.
69. **Rebalanced Workflows instead of a fifth** (review F04). Default: the theme builder moves to 9B and 9A gains `api-flow-actions`; 9C splits its builder in three; no 9A1/9A2 split, which would add a push and serialise the renderer behind the engine (CI cadence, decision 65).
70. **Organisation-level permissions** (review P01). Default: `users:*`, `roles:*` and organisation settings are effective only through an organisation-wide assignment; acting on another user needs a superset of their permissions, and a reset or reactivation a second factor within 10 minutes (review P02); deactivation needs the factor only from an actor who has one, so it stays the emergency control (verification round V05). `settings:write` stays environment-level, and a per-setting `SETTING_LEVEL` marks the organisation settings (V14).
71. **The manual accessibility audit at the release gate** (review H12). Default: Phase 9 is recorded done with the audit's automated part, the manual part not run and no conformance claimed; the manual pass is an open item for the owner (or a contracted auditor) before the release, checked by Phase 11's acceptance.
72. **The analytics settle window** (review H04). Default: 30 days (1–90); closed days are frozen, so a decision made after the window does not change its day, which the console states; redaction never shrinks a count, because it records the inquiry's contribution first (verification round: the first bound, "the shortest inquiry retention window", could not hold against on-demand redaction or a template version's own retention); outcome cells of open days refresh once a local day (P21).
73. **The Android test-only edit** (reviews H01, F01). Default: Phase 9 adds `FlowCoordinatorProjectionTest.kt`, a host test with no production Kotlin change, so the claim that today's SDK runs linear flows rests on a test of its navigation.

**Review**

Three adversarial reviews read the first draft on 2026-10-29: privacy, security and law (P01–P37), honesty and product coverage (H01–H30) and feasibility and fit with the code (F01–F29), 96 findings in all: 4 CRITICAL, 20 HIGH, 50 MEDIUM and 22 LOW. `docs/plans/phase-9-review-resolution.md` disposes of each against the code at `42895e6a` and the Phase 7 and Phase 8 plans: 92 accepted and 4 accepted in part (P21, P31, F04, F14), none rejected; 7 findings raised by two lenses (F01, F03, F15, F21, H10, H19, H20) share the fix recorded under the other lens. The main changes:
- **Today's Android SDK** (H01, F01): the first projection made it report "Completed" right after consent; the projection now lists the path's exited steps, the current step and the unconditional run after it, ending at a terminal step or an unsupported placeholder, proven by a `FlowCoordinator` host test (C151.6).
- **Signals in branches** (H02): Phase 7 computes signals only at completion, so every signal branch read `null`; branches now read provisional snapshots of the signals that can be computed mid-flow, and refuse the rest (C150.9).
- **Authority across environments** (P01, P02, F29): organisation-level permissions, superset and step-up rules for acting on another user, and a sign-in that lands where the user holds a role (C167.2, C168.1).
- **Unknowns, heuristics and adverse paths** (H03, H07, H08, H19, P25): an errored check reads `null`; a verification outcome is the verification's own status; `heuristic:` on every route reading a heuristic; a missing path goes to review, never `else`; `onNull: "review"`; Phase 8's biometric cases and consent check (C150).
- **Facts the person or the client controls** (P06, H20): every scope path carries its source, and lowering friction on one is acknowledged (C150.10).
- **Evidence and outcomes** (H09, H16, P04, H10): `flow-outcome` and `unverified-path:` for completions without evidence; one `flowOutcome` for the engine and simulate; consent digests that identify the words shown (C151.13, C156.4, C162.9).
- **The public flow's attack surface** (P03, P05, P10, F03): a linear-time pattern matcher; a per-client limit, id checks, negative caching and a validated lookup answer at the flow server (C148.3, C157).
- **Leaks to the person and to readers** (P14, P15, P16, P20, P22): an allowlisted client view, current-step copy and values only, pseudonymous step records with masked step names, employee data in analytics treated as such, and the key blocklist at every exit.
- **Analytics** (H04, H05, H06, H17, H18, P21): open and closed days so redaction never shrinks a figure, split expirations, per-attempt and per-person pass rates, a defined funnel share, true bounds and time-axis complementary suppression (C163, C164).
- **Accessibility** (H11, H12, H13, F16): a server extension route usable ten times, no timed redirect, a fixed CI matrix, and no conformance claimed before the manual pass (C171).
- **Fit with the code** (F02, F05–F13, F17–F20, F29): a gitleaks allowlist the repository already satisfies, Phase 8 trigger and typing work owned, the session's projection schema, a lossless migration, a defined start of the position, dependency-free CSP code for the flow server, owned mock and test files, the `sdk-js` skeleton in Stage 1b, the theme's token set matched to the stylesheet with a dark palette, the notification subject, the real CI jobs, and dynamic console rendering for the nonce CSP.
- **Workflows** (F04): 9A 10, 9B 10, 9C 8, 9D 9 agents (decisions 65, 69).
- **The verification round** (`verify-round.md`): 87 of 96 resolutions held; the 9 partly resolved and the 17 defects the resolutions introduced are fixed in place. The main ones: the server fills consent placeholders before hashing, so biometric matching can be consented to (V01, P04, H10); the projection leaves out exhausted verifications and its host test is committed green over fixtures that the server's projection must reproduce (V02, V03); the flow server imports one import-free module (V04); deactivation stays possible without a factor (V05); redaction never shrinks an open day, and open days' outcome cells refresh once a day (H04, P21); copy targets of flow actions live on their own rows (V08, P13); `SETTING_LEVEL` (V14); the 9C builder's first checkpoint (V07); and the grammar of patterns (V10).

## Phase 7 and 8 outcomes that amend this plan (2026-11-04; Phase 8 built as Workflows 8A to 8D)

Phase 7 closed after Workflow 7C and K7C-2 (2026-11-02), and Phase 8 was built as four Workflows, 8A (the engine and its actions), 8B (cases), 8C (console, review, fixes) and 8D (the rest of the console and the carried items, review, fixes), at `f0a32761`. These outcomes are binding on Workflows 9A to 9D; where they contradict a contract above, they win. The record of each is in `docs/plans/phase-7.plan.md` (the "Workflow 7C ... amendments" sections and "K7C-2") and `docs/plans/phase-8.plan.md` (the outcomes section, every "Workflow 8A/8B/8C/8D ... amendments" section and both "Orchestrator decisions after Workflow 8A/8B" sections), and in `docs/PROGRESS.md`.

**Migrations, storage and the database**

1. **Migrations.** Phase 7 ends at `0026_transaction_relations_page_index` and Phase 8 at `0029_phase8d` (`0027_phase8a`, `0028_phase8b`; 8C took none). So 9A's one migration is `0030_phase9a` and 9B's the next, unless a gap between Workflows adds one. The migrator applies every pending migration in one transaction, so a label added with `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE` is kept first in its file and never used in the same migration on an upgraded database, and a CHECK that names a new label compares `<column>::text` (Phase 8 Stage 1a amendment 2). To extend a Workflow's migration: `pnpm --filter @openverify/core build`, remove the file, its meta snapshot and its journal entry, `drizzle-kit generate --name <name>`, put the trailing newline back in `_journal.json`, prettier on the snapshot, and re-apply the hand edits (Stage 1a amendment 17). Upgrade tests also have `createTestAccountOnEarlierSchema` (Stage 1b amendment 37).
2. **Phase 8's vocabularies are text CHECKs, never enums.** The trigger types (`WORKFLOW_TRIGGER_TYPES`, `packages/core/src/workflows/vocabulary.ts:40`, eight today), the run creators (`WORKFLOW_RUN_CREATOR_TYPES`, `:126`), the decision kinds (`DECISION_KINDS`, `packages/core/src/workflows/decisions.ts:12`) and actors (`DECISION_ACTOR_TYPES`), `user_notifications`' kinds (`CASE_NOTIFICATION_KINDS`) and `case_history`'s kinds (`CASE_HISTORY_KINDS`) are text columns held by `oneOf` CHECKs in `packages/db/src/tables/`. So `trigger-flow`, `inquiry-flow` and `flow-failure` (C150.12, C152.2, C150.7), `webhook-failure` (C169.7) and a checklist history kind (C172.2) are recreated CHECKs, each with a migration test, and a vocabulary a dashboard `Record` is typed by gets its words in the same commit (`HISTORY_WORDS` in `components/cases/reviewer/case-words.ts`).
3. **Test hygiene.** Every Testcontainers Postgres starts through `startIntegrationPostgres` and every Testcontainers RustFS through `startIntegrationRustfs` (`apps/api/src/test-support/integration-rustfs.ts`), both on `startWithRetries` of `@openverify/db/testing`, each guarded by a test that fails an image named elsewhere (K7B-13; K8-37). No start timeout is raised and no test is retried.
4. **The watchlist benchmark's imports** are unchanged in name and signature through Phase 8: only `packages/db/src/testing.ts` and `integration-postgres.ts` changed, additively (seeds, `createTestAccountOnEarlierSchema`, the exported `startWithRetries`). Rule 16 of each script stands.

**The flow engine's Phase 8 interfaces (9A)**

5. **Decision records as built (C128, C150.7).** The resource is `decision` (`idec_`, `packages/contracts/src/decisions.ts`, table `inquiry_decisions`): kinds `approve`, `decline`, `review`, `reopen`, `adverse`; actor `user`, `api-key` or `workflow-run` only (`decisionActorSchema`, `inquiry_decisions_actor_type_valid`; "never the system", Phase 8 Stage 1a amendment 47); evidence kinds `check`, `verification`, `report`, `report-match`, `list-item`, `list-match`, `signal`, `step`; a `run` member that names a workflow run or is `null`. A flow-failure record therefore has no actor, member or evidence kind for what C150.7 records. (Default, on the owner's review list.) `contracts-9a` (S) adds the kind `flow-failure`, the actor `inquiry-flow` with the inquiry's `inq_` id (the same name as the run creator of C152.2), a nullable `flow` member beside `run` (`template-version-id`, the branch step ids and the routes taken, codes only) and the evidence kinds the flow's facts need (`extraction`, `field`, `device`, `account`, `workflow-output`, each a code with its tier, never a value); `foundation-9a` (S) recreates the three CHECKs; the console's decision words (`components/workflows/decision-words.ts`) gain them in the same commit; `recordInquiryDecisionInTx` (`apps/api/src/services/decisions/record.ts:64`) writes it.
6. **Typing and evaluation over a caller's scope (C150.12).** `typeCheckCondition(expression, scope: ScopeType)` (`packages/workflow-engine/src/expressions/typing.ts:580`, whose `reads[]` names each path read with its `nullable` and `pii` marks) and `evaluateCondition(expression, scope: EvaluationScope)` (`expressions/evaluate.ts:711`, roots supplied by the caller) already take a caller-supplied scope. `evaluateCondition` answers `{ outcome: 'null' }` without naming the operand that met the null, which is the only part `flow-spec-logic`'s borrowed edit may still add. Phase 8's null rules bind the flow's conditions too (Stage 2 amendments 16 to 18): `===` against a null operand follows `onNull`; a condition that only tests a path for null needs no `onNull`; `var` with a default is refused (`var_default_refused`). The package has no `test:integration` script.
7. **Runs and their actions (C152).** The run service is `startWorkflowRunInTx` (`apps/api/src/workflows/engine/start-run.ts:89`) and cancellation `cancelWorkflowRun` (`workflows/runs/service.ts:391`) over `cancelRun` (`workflows/runs/control.ts:90`). A parent waits for a child through its `workflow-run.*` events, never a row the child touches (Stage 3 amendment 37), which is the pattern for `run_workflow`'s wait. A run output declared non-PII shows only as a number or a boolean (amendment 40). Each catalogue action carries the triggers it allows, so `trigger-flow` joins an action only when listed (none until listed). The API process enqueues steps itself (Stage 4 amendment 101).
8. **C128.4's biometric fact** is `templateIsBiometric` from `biometricTemplates` (`apps/api/src/workflows/publish/facts.ts:42`): a template is biometric when a published version's flow holds a `selfie` step (`holdsSelfie` walks any shape, so a v2 flow reads correctly) or it screens every list while the environment has an active face or selfie-background list; a template with no published version is unknown and fails closed (Stage 3 amendment 57). C150.7 calls this function.
9. **Used by and promote (C154.4, C154.5).** Workflows that name a template are found by `findWorkflowReferences` with `WORKFLOW_REFERENCE_KINDS` (`packages/db/src/repositories/workflow-versions.ts:72`, `:409`), whose kinds include `inquiry-template`, `report-template`, `workflow` and the case kinds. Phase 8's workflow promote (Stage 3 amendment 59: the target by name, several answering `promote_unresolved`) is the pattern.
10. **Readers of a stored flow.** `storedFlow` (`apps/api/src/services/resources.ts:56`) has 13 call sites, Phase 8's `workflows/actions/verifications/run-verification.ts:93` among them. Three more readers parse with `flowSchema.safeParse` directly (`jobs/process-verification.ts:189`, `jobs/face-index-rebuild.ts:104`, `services/lists/biometric/source-selfie.ts:77`) and read a v2 flow as a parse failure. The v2-safe reader of `foundation-9a` (S) covers all sixteen.
11. **The CSV reader.** `readCsv(bytes, options)` is exported from the root of `@openverify/checks` (`packages/checks/src/csv/read.ts`; options `encoding`, `maxRows`, `maxRecordCharacters`, `headerProblems`; `decodeCsv` and `resolveCsvEncoding` beside it), keeping the importer's rules; `apps/api` no longer depends on `csv-parse` (Stage 1a amendment 19). The translations CSV (C162.7) reads with it.
12. **The flow's terminal screen (C128.7, C150.7).** `FlowRenderer` passes the session's `decision-contest-route` (`{ url, host, contact }`) to `CompletionScreen` in `apps/flow/src/steps/outcome-screens.tsx`, chosen by `terminalPosition` in `src/flow/flow-position.ts`; the Android SDK decodes `contracts/fixtures/api/flow-session-declined.json` and does not show the member. `flow-renderer` keeps this on the server's position, and C150.7's failed screen shows the contest route the same way.

C174.8's `workflow_grant_widened` joins the generator's source, never the page by hand.
