- `ACCESS_PROBLEM_STATUSES` (`problems/access/`): `role_scope_invalid` 422 (at `/assignments/<i>/environment-ids`), `no_environment_role` 403, `user_more_privileged` 403, `step_up_required` 403;
- `CASE_PROBLEM_STATUSES` gains `case_checklist_incomplete` 422, `case_fields_required` 422 and `case_action_not_available` 403, worded in `CASE_PROBLEM_WORDS` in the same commit.

  `CASE_PROBLEM_STATUSES` is at `problem.ts:551`, not the outcome section's `:546`. None of these folders is read by the Android SDK (`Fixtures.list` reads `api/`, `flows/`, `problems/` and `problems/flow/` only), so no `ContractFixturesTest.kt` entry is needed or possible (an entry for a file the SDK never lists fails its equality test). The same holds for K8-27's three problems in (C).

B8. **The 9B settings (C174.6), as built in common**, each under `settings:write`, environment-level (none is in `SETTING_LEVEL`), audited as every settings change, optional in the answer and served with its default (`PLATFORM_SETTING_DEFAULTS` and `resolvePlatformSettings` in `dashboard-api.ts`; the keys join `RETENTION_KEYS` in `services/settings.ts` and `EnvironmentSettings` in `packages/db/src/tables/tenancy.ts`; the console excludes them from `RetentionSetting` as `PlatformSetting` until 9C shows them):
- `analytics-time-zone`: an IANA name `Intl` knows, default `UTC`, at most 64 characters (`analyticsTimeZoneSchema`). Decision the plan left open: a UTC offset (`+05:30`), a lower-case `utc` and `GMT+5` are refused, because PostgreSQL reads a bare offset with the POSIX sign, the opposite of ISO 8601's, so the zone `api-analytics` passes to `date_trunc` is always a tz database name;
- `analytics-suppression-threshold`: 11 to 100, default 11 (never `null`);
- `analytics-retention-months`: 13 to 60, default 13;
- `analytics-settle-days`: 1 to 90, default 30;
- `analytics-assignee-retention-months`: default 13. Decision the plan left open: 1 to 60 (ours: an organisation may keep this employee data for less than the other rollups, never longer than their ceiling);
- `api-key-age-warning-days`: default 90. Decision the plan left open: 1 to 3,650 (ours, the retention windows' range);
- `mail-sender-name`: 1 to 100 characters, `null` by default (the install's From name). Its grammar (C169.8: control characters, `<`, `>`, `@`, `"` refused) is family (W)'s;
- `audit-log-retention-days`: 30 to 3,650, `null` by default (kept as today).

B9. **visx is not added: The Unlicense in its closure (C176, review F24; decision 42; an owner decision before 9C).** `@visx/axis`, `group`, `scale`, `shape` and `text` 4.0.0 are MIT, released 2026-06-11, and peer `react ^18.0.0 || ^19.0.0` (the workspace runs 19.3.1). But every visx package depends on `@visx/vendor` 4.0.0, which pins `d3-delaunay` 6.0.2, whose `delaunator` 5.1.0 depends on **`robust-predicates` 3.0.3 (2026-03-22), licensed only under The Unlicense**, which CLAUDE.md rule 4's list does not name (it names CC0 for public-domain dedications). The closure was derived from the lockfile after a trial install: 41 packages, every other one MIT or ISC (`@visx/vendor` "MIT and ISC"). The install was reverted through pnpm (lockfile byte-identical, frozen install passing) and nothing was committed. The owner chooses one of:
  - (a) add The Unlicense to rule 4's list (an OSI-approved public-domain dedication like CC0, without CC0's fallback licence), or grant an exception for `robust-predicates` 3.0.3 alone; `contracts-9b`'s visx step then runs as planned in a gap before 9C (the trial's 41 rows are ready to be derived again);
  - (b) keep rule 4 as it is and replace visx by `d3-scale` 4.0.2 (2021-09-24) and `d3-shape` 3.2.0 (2022-12-20), ISC, whose closure (`d3-array`, `d3-format`, `d3-interpolate`, `d3-color`, `d3-time`, `d3-time-format`, `internmap`, `d3-path`) is ISC throughout and never reaches `d3-delaunay`; 9C then draws axes, groups and text as plain React SVG (decision 42's "primitives, client-only" is unchanged, the accessibility rules of C164.9 are ours either way);
  - (c) a pnpm override that removes `d3-delaunay` from `@visx/vendor` (the charts never import it); not recommended, since the installed package would then be incomplete.

  Default proposed: (b). Until the owner decides, no chart dependency exists and Workflow 9C's `dashboard-analytics` cannot start its charts.

B10. **The analytics benchmark (C163.7, review F22), as built in common:** `apps/api/package.json` runs `analytics:benchmark` as `tsx --conditions=@openverify/source --env-file-if-exists=../../.env scripts/analytics-benchmark.ts`; the stub exports `ANALYTICS_BENCHMARK_PHASES` (`--seed`, `--measure`), `parseAnalyticsBenchmarkArgs`, `AnalyticsBenchmarkNotBuiltError` and `main`, and refuses every phase (exit 1) until `api-analytics` builds it, so no figure is ever reported from the stub.

**(A): analytics** (checkpoint `b8d6060c`)

B11. **Who owns what in `packages/core/src/analytics/`.** The plan gives `analytics-logic` the folder. `contracts-9b` froze in it, as 9A froze `v2/schemas/` inside `flow-spec-logic`'s package: `index.ts` (shared from Stage 2), `definitions.ts` and `rows.ts` (changed only by `integration-9b`), and, inside files `analytics-logic` builds, the constants `SIGNAL_BINS`, `SIGNAL_NOT_EVALUATED_BIN`, `DURATION_BUCKET_COUNT`, `DURATION_MIN_SECONDS`, `DURATION_MAX_SECONDS`, `ANALYTICS_SUPPRESSION_FLOOR` and `ANALYTICS_SUPPRESSIONS`, which it uses and never changes. It owns the function bodies of `metrics.ts`, `suppression.ts`, `durations.ts` and `signal-bins.ts`, may add files and exports beside them (re-exported through those modules, since the index is frozen), and replaces the stub tests at the end of `analytics.test.ts` with its property tests and golden vectors. The definitions live in core, not contracts, because core cannot import contracts and the pure functions key their figures by them.

B12. **`pending` is a rollup check status (C163.3 amended by C164.4).** C163.3 lists `passed`, `failed`, `not_applicable`, `errored` and `not_configured`; C164.4 shows "pending" as its own column, so `ANALYTICS_CHECK_STATUSES` ends with `pending`, and `foundation-9b`'s `analytics_check_daily.status` CHECK takes all six. `errored` is a `not_applicable` result with a reason in `CHECK_ERROR_REASONS` or ending `CHECK_UNAVAILABLE_REASON_SUFFIX`, and `not_configured` one with `provider_not_configured` (`checkErrorClassOf`, 9A).

B13. **The signal bins (C164.6), named here as the task asks**, since the registry has no bins member: `inquiry/bot_score` ten bins `0-9` to `90-100`; the two threat levels `low`, `medium`, `high`; `automation_artefact_count` and `list_match_count` `0`, `1`, `2-4`, `5+`; `session_count` `0`, `1`, `2`, `3-5`, `6+`; `handoff_count` `0`, `1`, `2`, `3+`; `tor_detected` and `proxy_detected` `true`, `false`; the never-false `datacenter_detected` and `vpn_detected` `true` only; `country_from_session` one bin per ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 code (`{ kind: 'country' }`); `country_comparison` `same`, `different`; `max_session_distance` in kilometres `0-49`, `50-499`, `500-1999`, `2000+`. A `null` value is `not-evaluated`, never a bin. Ours and provisional, from each signal's value type and range, tuned on nothing; a test holds the keys to `SIGNAL_REGISTRY` and an enum's bins to its `values`. `list_match_count` is read from `list_screenings` (a completed screening's `match_count`; any other status or none is `not-evaluated`), never from the stored signals.

B14. **The metric definitions (C164.1-C164.6) as built**, in `ANALYTICS_METRICS`, beyond the plan's words: the inquiry answer splits success three ways as counts and as rates over `finished`; `expiration-rate` is `expired-after-start / started`; verifications carry `pass-rate-per-attempt` (`passed / (passed + failed + requires-retry)`, unit attempts) and `pass-rate-per-person` (`step-passed / step-entered`, unit people-and-steps); the funnel carries its own `started` (the overall share's denominator, found missing by the test) and `step-share = entered / transitions`; a check's `failure-share` is its failed results over `failed-verifications`, the failed verifications of its type; cases carry `created`, `resolved`, `sla-met`, `sla-missed`, `resolution-time` (median and 90th percentile buckets) and `assigned`. Decisions left open: `marked-for-review` counts finished inquiries a flow or a check sent to review at least once; `decided-not-declared` is a decision record whose `solely-automated` is `null` (Phase 8's API decision default).

B15. **The answers (C164.8) as built.** Each is `{ data: { type: analytics-<report>, id: <the SHA-256 hex of the canonical query>, attributes } }`, with `time-zone`, `time-zones` (every zone the buckets were computed in; more than one marks a mixed range, C163.2), `from`, `to`, `bucket`, `threshold`, `suppressed-cells`, `settle-days` (so the console can state the window, C163.4) and `freshness { rolled-up-through, outcomes-as-of }`. Decisions the plan left open:
- **cells:** a count is an integer or `{ suppressed: 'small-count' | 'complementary' }` (a complementary cell reads "hidden", never "fewer than", because its value may be large); a rate is `{ value }` (a fraction in [0, 1]), `{ below }` or `{ above }` (whole percent), or `{ suppressed: true }`; a duration is `{ lower, upper }` in seconds, `{ suppressed: true }`, or `null`;
- **the document schema refuses any visible count from 1 to `threshold - 1`**, wherever it sits in `buckets` or `totals`, and any outcome in a live bucket (only `created` and `started`), so no code path can serve an unsuppressed small count or a live outcome;
- a bucket names its first and last local day (both included), its zone and `live`; a week starts on Monday; a funnel bucket says `step-data: false` before the environment's first step record ("no step data", never 0), and the funnel's totals carry `step-data-from`;
- the funnel is per version (`versions[]`: `started`, `steps[]`, `transitions[]`, the top 20 `paths[]`); verifications group by type, capture method (`null` when not reported) and country, and list each step's per-person outcome; a check that never ran in the window is `ran: false` with no counts ("not run"); `cases.totals.assignees` is `null` for a reader without `users:write` and its `user-id` is `null` once re-keyed to "former user"; signals list each signal's bins with its tier and stage, and `not-evaluated`;
- queries take local dates `YYYY-MM-DD` (an instant is refused), the window is at most 400 days both ends included, `bucket` defaults to `day`; `filter[tag]` is the inquiries answer's alone and `filter[case-template-id]` the cases answer's, whose query takes no inquiry filter.

**(X): exports, saved views, bulk reports, list filters, delivery search** (checkpoint `223a4ad2`)

B16. **The new list filters are contracts before they are built, so their stubs must refuse them (C165.3).** `listInquiriesQuerySchema` takes `filter[list-match]`, `filter[list-id]` (`lst_`), `filter[check]` with `filter[check-status]` (one never without the other) and `filter[next-step-name]`; `listTransactionsQuerySchema` takes up to ten `filter[fields.<key>]` (`TRANSACTION_FIELD_FILTER_PATTERN`, any other unknown parameter still 400). Until `api-exports` builds them behind `inquiryListFilters` and `transactionListFilters`, today's services would read the schema-valid query and ignore those keys, answering an unfiltered list as if filtered. So `foundation-9b` (X)'s two stubs answer 501 for any of the new keys (fail closed), never an unfiltered answer, with a test; and `filter[next-step-name]` and a string `filter[fields.<key>]` need PII visibility (`requirePiiView`) when built.

B17. **`filter[list-match]` and `filter[list-id]`: the 7B refresh's default, recorded (C165.3, K10):** `matched` is at least one open list match; `not-matched` a completed screening and no open match (only a completed screening may claim no match, ADR 0005); `unscreened` a pending, partial or errored screening with no open match; an inquiry with no screening row (no list selected) matches none of the three; `filter[list-id]` is an open match on that list (`INQUIRY_LIST_MATCH_FILTERS`).

B18. **Transaction field filters: the 7B refresh's default, recorded as a C165.3 amendment.** `filterable` joins the field config (`packages/core/src/transactions/field-schemas.ts`) and is valid on a top-level `choices`, `boolean`, `integer`, `number` or `date` field (`FIELD_TYPES_FILTERABLE`), read through the plaintext projection, and on a top-level `string` only when it is also `searchable`, read through the existing `transaction_field_keys` HMAC index (one label, `identifier-transaction-field`, rebuilt by `rebuildTransactionFieldKeys`), so no second HMAC table, label or rebuild step exists; `transactionTypeVersionProblems` refuses anything else at `/field-schemas/<i>/config/filterable` (built here with its test, as the rule beside `searchable`). Both string-equality filters (a string `filter[fields.<key>]` and the existing `filter[identifier]`) need PII visibility (Phase 8 C137.2's membership-oracle rule): a tightening of C105.2 that `api-exports` makes test-first, changing `filter[identifier]`'s existing tests (`services/transactions/transactions.ts:267-268` serves it without the check today); `filter[identifier][value]`, `filter[reference-id]` and a string `filter[fields.<key>]` are personal-data filter keys for saved views.

B19. **Exports (C166.1-C166.4, C166.6) as built.** Decisions the plan left open: the body takes `filter` (the list route's parameters, validated by its query schema) or `saved-view-id`, never both (neither exports the whole list); `verifications` needs `filter[type]` and `lists` `filter[list-id]` unless a saved view supplies them; the resource records `filter-keys` (the shape, never a value), `saved-view-id`, `include-personal-data`, `row-count`, `byte-size`, `error-code` (only on a failure), `created-at`, `cut-at` (when the rows were read), `completed-at` and `expires-at`, with the case export's invariants; `filter` is personal data in logs (`PII_EXPORT_ATTRIBUTES`, which `foundation-9b` adds to the API-log masked members with a test); `EXPORT_CSV_COLUMNS` documents each type's columns with their `pii` mark (a list item's `value`, every `reference-id`, `fields`, `query`, `extracted-fields`, `next-step-name`), and names none of C138.4's never-included members.

B20. **Saved views (C165.1, C165.2, C165.5) as built.** The nine lists' query schemas are `SAVED_VIEW_QUERY_SCHEMAS` (the case list is `listCasesQuerySchema`, the audit list `listUserAuditLogsQuerySchema`, which (U) extends). The personal-data filter keys are `SAVED_VIEW_PII_FILTER_KEYS` (`filter[reference-id]` on inquiries, accounts, reports, cases and transactions; `filter[next-step-name]`; `filter[identifier][value]`; `filter[ip]` on audit logs), and `savedViewPiiFilterKeys` adds a transaction's `filter[fields.<key>]` on a string field, every field filter when the caller does not pass the type version's string keys (fail closed). Decisions left open: a view holds at most 30 filters and 50 columns and one sort (`-` for descending); paging is never stored; the resource names its `owner-id`, and `filters-valid` with `invalid-filters` (keys only) says when its list no longer accepts a filter (C165.5); `GET /dashboard/saved-views` takes `filter[list]`.

B21. **Bulk report jobs (C166.5, C166.6) as built.** `POST /reports/bulk` is multipart: the field `report-template-id` and the CSV in the part `file`; the job resource `report-bulk-job` (`rbulk_`) carries `status`, `report-template-id`, `row-count`, `created-count`, `failed-count`, `rows[] { row, outcome, report-id, error-code }` (a created row names its report, a failed row its code, held by the schema), a whole-file `error-code` (a header or size problem) and the times. `REPORT_BULK_LIMITS` is 1,000 rows and 2 MiB (ours, provisional: the translations CSV's byte cap); the CSV part is `PII_REPORT_BULK_ATTRIBUTES` (`file`).

B22. **The delivery search (C165.4) as built.** Decisions the plan left open: a clause is `{ attribute, operator, value }`, with `updated_at` taking `gt`, `gte`, `lt`, `lte` and an ISO 8601 instant, and `status`, `webhook_id` and `event_name` taking `eq` (one value) or `in` (up to 20); `and` and `or` take 1 to 20 children and `not` one; at most two levels of operators (`WEBHOOK_DELIVERY_SEARCH_LIMITS.depth`) and 20 clauses in all; the body's optional `page { after, size }` pages the answer, which is the delivery list document.

B23. **`@openverify/node` (C174.9):** `analytics` (one method per answer, the query as the route takes it) and `exports` (`list`, `listAll`, `create`, `retrieve`, `download`). The client's `FilterNames` now reads only a query's literal keys and admits `fields.<key>` for a list with dynamic filters, so `transactions.list` keeps its typed filters.

**(U): access** (checkpoint `77a8a1d5`)

B24. **`PERMISSION_LEVEL` and `SETTING_LEVEL` as built (C167.2; reviews P01, V14).** The level names are `organization` and `environment` (the code's spelling). `users:read`, `users:write`, `roles:read` and `roles:write` are organisation-level (`ORGANIZATION_LEVEL_PERMISSIONS`); every other permission is environment-level. `SETTING_LEVEL` names `require-mfa` alone, and `settingLevel` reads any other key as the environment's, so every 9A and 9B setting is environment-level. Phase 11's organisation-level permissions extend `ORGANIZATION_LEVEL_PERMISSIONS`, which keeps its name.

B25. **The role routes (C167.3) as built.** `PUT /dashboard/users/:id/roles` takes `{ assignments: [{ role-id, environment-ids | null }] }`, 1 to 50 assignments, each role once, an environment list of 1 to 20 ids or `null` (every current and future environment), and answers `user-role-assignments` (`id` the user's). `GET /dashboard/roles/grantable` answers `grantable` (`id` the caller's) with one entry per environment of the organisation and one for `environment-id: null` (an organisation-wide assignment), each listing permissions and roles with `grantable` and, when refused, a `reason` of `GRANT_REFUSAL_REASONS` (`not-held`, `organization-level`, `admin-organization-wide`). Decision the plan left open: `PATCH /dashboard/users/:id`'s `role-ids` stays for existing clients and means every environment (`environment-ids: null`), so it can never grant less widely than it did; `api-access` applies the same per-environment checks to it.

B26. **Invitations, reactivation, the reset and the step-up (C168.1, C168.3).** Invitations live on the user row (`users.invitation_token_hash`, no invitation table), so `POST /dashboard/invitations/:id/resend` takes the invited user's `usr_` id and answers `dashboardInvitationResponseSchema`. `resend`, `reactivate` and `reset-mfa` take an empty body. **A missing route, added here:** C168.1's "the console asks for the factor again" needs a way to verify a factor on the current session, so `POST /dashboard/sessions/current/step-up` takes a TOTP code or a passkey assertion (never a recovery code, which would let a stolen code act on other users) and answers `{ mfa-verified-at, step-up-expires-at }`, the window being `STEP_UP_WINDOW_SECONDS` (600); a passkey step-up needs its options route too, `POST /dashboard/sessions/current/step-up/webauthn/options`, answering `dashboardWebauthnLoginOptionsResponseSchema`. `foundation-9b` (U) freezes both with the other routes, `api-access` builds them.

B27. **Sessions and the users list (C168.4, C168.5).** A session is `user-session` (`uss_`, the existing id) with `device` (a summary such as "Firefox on Linux", never the user agent; `null` when it names neither), `created-at`, `last-seen-at`, `expires-at`, `current` and `mfa-verified-at`; `revoke-others` answers `{ meta: { revoked } }`. The user resource gains `mfa-factors` (kinds and counts, at most one entry per kind) and `last-sign-in-at` (`null` when never), both optional and served only to holders of `users:write` (review P20).

B28. **The audit filters (C168.6).** `filter[action-prefix]` (a dotted prefix such as `inquiry.`), `filter[from]` (included) and `filter[to]` (excluded), ISO 8601 instants with `from` before `to`, and `filter[ip]` (one IPv4 or IPv6 address, never a range), matched through `audit_log_ip_index` under `AUDIT_IP_INDEX_LABEL` and needing `pii:view`. The three non-IP filters need nothing more than `audit-logs:read`.

**(W): keys, webhooks, notifications, the sender name** (checkpoint `f77b1c78`)

B29. **Rotation, the note, age and the blocklist (C169.1-C169.3) as built.** `POST /api-keys/:id/rotate` takes `meta.previous-expires-in-seconds` (0 to 2,592,000) and an optional `meta.name`, and answers the new key with its token once and the old key's id and end (`meta.previous-key-id`, `meta.previous-expires-at`); the new key copies the scopes, IPs, version, blocklist and note through `cloneApiKey`'s checks. The key gains `note` (at most 255, no control character, `null` for none) and `attribute-blocklist` (C14's webhook grammar, `attributeBlocklistSchema`) on create, update and the resource, and `age-warning` (server-computed against `api-key-age-warning-days`) on the resource.

B30. **Webhooks (C169.5, C169.6) as built.** `name` (1 to 200, trimmed, no control character), `description` (at most 1,000) and `failure-alert { enabled, threshold 1 to 10,000, window-minutes 5 to 1,440 }` are optional and nullable on create, update and the resource (the threshold's ceiling is ours); the resource also serves `previous-secret-expires-at` (when the previous secret stops signing, `null` when none does), so the page can say receivers must accept either signature until then. The three attempt headers are lower-case like the existing ones: `openverify-webhook-attempts-made`, `openverify-webhook-attempts-left`, `openverify-webhook-first-attempted-at` (an ISO 8601 instant).

B31. **The notification subject (C169.7, review F18) as built, without a migration here.** `CASE_NOTIFICATION_KINDS` keeps its three case kinds (Phase 8's case services and emails read it), and a new `USER_NOTIFICATION_KINDS` adds `webhook-failure`; `foundation-9b` (W) recreates `user_notifications_kind_valid` over the new list, adds `subject_type` and `subject_id` with one CHECK per kind (`NOTIFICATION_KIND_SUBJECT`: the case kinds name a case, `webhook-failure` a webhook) and makes `case_id` nullable. The resource serves `subject-type` and exactly one relationship, `case` or `webhook`, matching the kind (the schema holds it); a server before 9B leaves `subject-type` out and serves case kinds only. The console's typecheck minimum was taken here: `NOTIFICATION_KIND_WORDS` words `webhook-failure`, and the case notification page reads the case relationship as optional. Each row is read under its kind's rule (outcome 20): `foundation-9b` (W)'s hook in the notifications service, filled by `api-keys-webhooks`.

B32. **Notification preferences (C169.7) as built.** `notification-preferences` (`id` the user's) holds, for every kind of `USER_NOTIFICATION_KINDS`, `{ console, email }`; `PUT` sets the kinds it names (at least one) and keeps the others; the defaults are `NOTIFICATION_PREFERENCE_DEFAULT` (console on, email off).

B33. **The sender name's grammar (C169.8, review P29) as built.** `mailSenderNameSchema` (trimmed, 1 to 100, refusing control characters, `<`, `>`, `@` and `"`) is the settings route's `mail-sender-name`; RFC 2047 encoding and RFC 5322 quoting are the mailer's at send time. **A missing hook:** `MailMessage` (`apps/api/src/ports.ts`) has no sender member and `createMailer` sends the install-wide `from` (`apps/api/src/mail/index.ts:58`), so `api-keys-webhooks` needs `MailMessage.senderName?: string | null` and the mailer building `"<encoded name>" <address of OPENVERIFY_MAIL_FROM>` from it; `ports.ts` and `mail/index.ts` are shared files, so `foundation-9b` (W) adds the member and the stub `formatSender(name, from)` (owner `api-keys-webhooks`, in `apps/api/src/mail/sender-name.ts`) called from `createMailer`.

**(C): case and report completions, K8-27, the node expansion** (checkpoint `ef56b8c1`)

B34. **Vocabularies bound to a CHECK or an enum stay put; foundation-9b (C) moves each in 0031.** `case_history.kind` (`CASE_HISTORY_KINDS`), `case_actions.placement` (`CASE_ACTION_PLACEMENTS`) and `reports.origin` (the Postgres enum `report_origin`, held equal to `REPORT_ORIGINS` by `packages/db/src/schema.test.ts`) would each fail the db drift test if their lists changed without a migration. So the contracts read new lists beside them: `caseHistoryEntryKindSchema` (`CASE_HISTORY_KINDS` and `CASE_CHECKLIST_HISTORY_KINDS`), `caseActionPlacementInputSchema` (`header`, `status-switcher`, `module:<key>`) and `REPORT_RESOURCE_ORIGINS` (`REPORT_ORIGINS` and `bulk`). `foundation-9b` then, in migration 0031: puts `ALTER TYPE report_origin ADD VALUE 'bulk'` first in the file (never used in the same migration) and adds `bulk` to core's `REPORT_ORIGINS` in the same commit ((X), for the bulk job); recreates `case_history_kind_valid` over both history lists ((C)); recreates the `case_actions` placement CHECK over the fixed placements and the module pattern, and switches the action resource's and bodies' `placement` to `caseActionPlacementInputSchema` ((C)); each with a migration test. Until then the API serves `header` only and writes no checklist history, which nothing yet asks for.

B35. **The case template version members (C172.1-C172.4) as built.** `layout { columns 1 to 3, modules[] { key, column, order 0 to 99, collapsed } }` (each module once, in a column of the layout; `null` for none); `checklists[]` (at most 10, each `{ key, label, items[] }` with 1 to 50 items `{ key, label, required-for-statuses[] }`); a status's `requires-fields[]` (at most 50 field keys); `preset-comments[]` (at most 50, `{ key, label, text }`, text 1 to 2,000 characters with no `{...}` placeholder). All optional, so Phase 8's versions and answers stay valid. Decisions left open: keys are `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,39}$`; a module or permission the publisher lacks is refused at publish by `api-cases-reports`, not by the schema.

B36. **The checklist state (C172.2) as built.** `POST /cases/:id/checklist` takes `meta { checklist-key, item-key, checked, lock-version? }` and answers `case-checklist-state` (`id` the case's) with every item's `checked`, `checked-by` (a user, or a workflow run acting through Phase 8's actions; `null` unchecked) and `checked-at`, the schema holding that a checked item names both and an unchecked one neither; history kinds `checklist-item-checked` and `checklist-item-unchecked` (B34).

B37. **Case actions (C172.5).** `visible-when { statuses[], role-ids[] }` (each at most 20; an empty list restricts nothing; `null` none) joins the action resource now, optional; `placement`'s new forms wait for 0031 (B34). The availability filter is `runnableTarget` (`services/cases/actions/service.ts:465`), which `listAvailableCaseActions` calls and `runCaseAction` (`:366`) does not: `api-cases-reports` applies `visible-when` in one function both call, `run-action` answering 403 `case_action_not_available`.

B38. **Reports and verifications (C173.1, C173.2).** The report gains `latest-run-notes` (the latest run's `REPORT_RUN_NOTES` codes, optional) and its origin may be `bulk` (B34). **The verification's `files` relationship already exists** (`verificationRelationshipsSchema`, served since Phase 2) and its document may include files, so C173.2 adds only `include=files` and `redacted-at` (optional on every verification). Because `include=files` is in the route's contract before `api-cases-reports` builds it, `routes/server/verifications.ts` refuses it with 501 (a borrowed edit of three lines, with its test, `verifications-include-files.test.ts`), so it is never answered without its files.

B39. **The print audit (C173.3) as built.** `POST /dashboard/print-audits` takes `{ object-id }` (an `inq_`, `rep_` or `ver_` id) and answers `print-audit` (`id` the `aud_` entry written, `object-id`, `recorded-at`).

B40. **The trust store's flags (C173.4) are already served:** `GET /dashboard/trust-anchors` answers `settings { refresh-enabled, icao-crls-allowed }` from `OPENVERIFY_TRUST_ANCHOR_REFRESH` and `OPENVERIFY_NFC_ALLOW_ICAO_CRLS` since Phase 5 (C62.10; `packages/contracts/src/trust-anchors.ts:102-107`), so no `meta.flags` is added: 9C's `dashboard-cases-reports` shows `settings` read-only, and `api-cases-reports` has nothing to build for it.

B41. **K8-27 as built**, each code in one commit with its golden problem under `problems/cases/` and its `CASE_PROBLEM_WORDS` entry: `case_template_invalid` (422; the golden keeps `field_type_changed` in `errors[]`), `case_search_invalid` (422; it keeps `search_attribute_unknown`) and `attachment_scan_failed` (409). The plan's "listed serverOnly in ContractFixturesTest.kt" does not apply: the SDK never lists `problems/cases/` (B7). `api-cases-reports` raises them (`services/cases/templates.ts:211`, `:236`, `:713`; `compileCaseSearch`, `services/cases/search.ts:202`; `assertServable`, `services/cases/attachments/scanning.ts:64-67`), changing the existing tests that expect the unnamed answers.

B42. **The property-node expansion (owner decision 20 after Workflow 7B; a Phase 9 item the plan did not place, placed in (C) by the 7B refresh):** `POST /graph-queries/:id/nodes/:nodeId/expand` (`expandGraphNodeParamsSchema`: a `grphq_` id and a `gn_` node id; `expandGraphNodeBodySchema`: optional `meta.run-sync`), `graph:write`, plus `pii:view` through `requirePiiView` when the node's kind is in `GRAPH_PERSON_KINDS`, policy `graph-queries`, answering 201 with the new graph query (the golden `graph-query-expanded.json`), 404 for an unknown node, 410 for a redacted source query and 422 for an account node. `@openverify/node`'s `graphQueries.expandNode(id, nodeId, { runSync })`. **Owner:** no 9B bullet owns `routes/server/graph-queries.ts` or the graph services; `foundation-9b` freezes the route at 501 in that file, and `integration-9b` builds it (default), unless the orchestrator names another agent.

**(F): the contracts of the 9A items S4-12 carried** (checkpoint `0a926347`; the orchestrator's decisions before 9B, 1 and 2)

B43. **The session's links (9A Stage 3 amendment 47) as built:** `links { help-url, privacy-url }` (`flowSessionLinksSchema`; each an `https:` URL of at most 2,048 characters, or `null`), optional, served by `api-flow-carry` from the pinned version's `help-url` and `privacy-url`. Today's SDK decodes a session with it (`flow-session-links.json`).

B44. **The per-inquiry expiry windows (amendment 21).** At the base, `createInquiryMetaSchema`'s four keys are accepted by `POST /inquiries` and **read by no API code**, so each is silently ignored, and `extendExpiryOnResumeInTx` has no caller. `meta.expiration-after-create-interval-seconds` needs no column (it sets `expires_at` at creation); the other three each need a nullable `inquiries` column, named in `INQUIRY_EXPIRY_OVERRIDE_COLUMNS`: `expiration_after_start_seconds` and `expiration_after_resume_seconds` (the orchestrator's two) **and `one_time_link_expiration_seconds`, a third** (an amendment to decision 2: without it `meta.one-time-link-expiration-seconds` stays a silently ignored key). `foundation-9b` (F) adds the three in 0031; `api-flow-carry` writes them on create and applies them at the start (`position.ts`), on a resume (`resumeInquiry`, `services/inquiries.ts:708`, and the one-time-link redemption, `services/one-time-links.ts:132`, through `extendExpiryOnResumeInTx`) and when a one-time link is made (`services/one-time-links.ts:99`), the window in each case the inquiry's value, else the version's setting. The inquiry resource serves the three as `expiration-after-start-interval-seconds`, `expiration-after-resume-interval-seconds` and `one-time-link-expiration-seconds` (`null` when the version decides); today's SDK decodes it (`inquiry-expiry-windows.json`). `services/one-time-links.ts` is a path outside `api-flow-carry`'s list ("the borrowed lines of the inquiry service and of the resume and link-redemption paths" covers redemption, not link creation): `integration-9b` settles it, or the orchestrator extends the list.

B45. **`flow_run_grants` (amendment 31).** `FlowRunGrantRecord` moved, unchanged, to `@openverify/core` (`flow-records.ts`), and `apps/api/src/flow-actions/grants.ts` re-exports it, so `foundation-9b` (F) types the column `flow_run_grants jsonb not null default '[]'` as `$type<FlowRunGrantRecord[]>()` (the version row's `flowRunGrants`, the member `recordedGrantOf`, `run-workflow.ts:83-89`, already reads). **The requirement half is already built:** `flowActionAcknowledgements` is called by the publish analyses (`services/publish/flow-v2.ts:187`), so a target with egress marks already needs `egress:<step id>`; `api-templates-carry` adds only the storing of `flowActionPublishGrants(...)` at publish, with the test that publishes a `latest-published` target holding a scope.

B46. **Preview, simulate and presets re-verified against C156.1 and C156.4.** The preview body was complete (unsaved `flow`, `copy`, `field-schemas`, `theme-set-id`, `styles`; `meta.locale` with the pseudo-locales); the document gains `contrast { status: passed | failed, findings[] { pointer, foreground, background, ratio, required, level: fail | warn } }` (at most 64, ratios never rounded; `failed` exactly when a finding fails), because the preview renders a failing palette rather than refusing it (review H15: the theme editor previews a change before it is saved). `api-themes` exports from `services/themes/resolve.ts` the version's resolved theme without an inquiry with the gate's findings in this shape; `api-templates-carry` serves it. Simulate's scope gains `inquiry { status?, tags? }` (C150.2's `inquiry` root, which a route may read: tags are operator facts) and a workflow's `error-type` (C152.2's `workflows.<step>.error-type`); each simulated step gains `notes` (the step record's notes, such as `on-null-review`), so a simulated path shows what the live path would record. The preset resource, its bodies and `PII_SIMULATION_PRESET_ATTRIBUTES` need nothing.

B47. **`comparison-source` (amendment 21) as built:** the metadata key is `comparison-source` (`COMPARISON_SOURCE_METADATA_KEY`), keyed by compared field, each `initial` or `entered` (`COMPARISON_SOURCE_KINDS`); the checks' inputs gain `inquiryFieldSources` beside `inquiryFields` (`GovernmentIdReferences`, `DatabaseCheckInput`). `api-flow-carry` passes `comparisonSourceFor`'s `sources` from the two input builders (`jobs/government-id.ts:346`, `jobs/types/database.ts:130`) and writes the member in `id_inquiry_comparison` and `database_inquiry_comparison`.

B48. **No member for the handed-off v2 phone's consent (amendment 23) or for prefill by input name (amendment 49).** For the phone: when a handed-off session has no consent of its own on a flow-spec 2 version, the session serves the passed consent step's client view as `step` (its `id` differs from `position.step-id`) and keeps `position` unchanged; the consent route already accepts that session's own consent without moving the position (amendment 23), and the web flow renders `step`. For prefill: for a verification step whose `prefillFrom` is a map (a `database` or AAMVA `form` step), `values` is keyed by the step's **input names**, each the mapped field's value under the same masking (`{ set: true }` for a write-once or masked value); for a screen and a single-key `prefillFrom` it stays keyed by field key. Both are `api-flow-carry`'s in `flow-engine/session.ts`, documented in `docs/flows/`.

B49. **S4-12's placements, confirmed against the code** (inventory section 14): every item sits in the paths the orchestrator gave, except B44's link-creation path. The preview resolve is `api-templates-carry`'s, with `api-themes`' export (B46); the real-font proof stays `api-themes'` (no permissively licensed WOFF2 generator is in the lockfiles at the base: a decodable font needs a font compiler, and none is a dependency); the replay of 9A's e2e inquiries through simulate stays `integration-9b`'s; 9D's and the owner's S4-12 items are unchanged.

## Workflow 9B Stage 1b amendments (foundation-9b)

Recorded by `foundation-9b`, a chain of six calls, one checkpoint each, (A), (U), (W), (X), (C) and (F) (the orchestrator's parallel rules during Workflow 9B, 2026-11-06). Each call adds its own `### (<family>)` at the end of this section. The evidence is `docs/tdd/phase9-foundation-9b.tdd.md`, one section per call, with each checkpoint's commit and gates. Where an item contradicts a contract above, the item wins.

### (A)

Checkpoint (A) at `3834b0c7`, on Stage 1a's `98f448bd`.

A1. **Migration `0031_phase9b`** is the next free number (the base `6a290723` ends at `0030_phase9a`). (A) creates it by the amendment-17 recipe with no hand edits; the later calls extend it the same way.

A2. **The twelve rollup tables (C163.3) as built** (`packages/db/src/tables/analytics.ts`): each table's columns are exactly its `rows.ts` row type's, in snake case; counts are `integer not null default 0`, held by `<table>_counts_nonnegative`; a histogram is `integer[]` of exactly `DURATION_BUCKET_COUNT` non-negative counts (`<table>_histogram_shape`). Decisions the plan left open:
- **the key:** one unique index per table, `<table>_key`, `NULLS NOT DISTINCT` (a dimension may be `null`), on `(environment_id, day, time_zone, source, <dimensions>)`. It is the redaction's upsert target (C163.4 adds to a `redacted` row), the recompute's delete of a day's `facts` rows, the range read of every answer and the retention's delete. `time_zone` is in the key so that a `redacted` row computed in an earlier zone is never added to (C163.2: a zone change never relabels a bucket);
- **vocabularies:** `source` (`facts`, `redacted`), `platform`, `device_kind`, `capture_method` (each nullable) and `analytics_check_daily.status` (all six `ANALYTICS_CHECK_STATUSES`, B12) are CHECKs; `verification_type` and `requirement` reuse the Postgres enums `verification_type` and `check_requirement`, so a new verification type reaches the rollups with its own `ALTER TYPE`;
- **forms:** template, version and case template ids by prefix; step ids by `FLOW_RECORD_STEP_ID_PATTERN`; a path's `path_sha256` as hex and at least one step id; a check name in `verification_checks`' snake case; a country in ISO 3166-1 alpha-2; a zone 1 to 64 characters (`analyticsTimeZoneSchema`'s bound). A reason, a signal name and a bin are held only by their lengths (1-128, 1-128, 1-32; ours, provisional), because a stricter pattern that a provider's reason failed would stop the rollup;
- **no foreign key to templates or versions:** a template is archived, never deleted, and the rollups go with their environment (`environment_id` cascades).

A3. **The assignee rollup's user (C163.3, C174.5; review P20).** `analytics_case_assignee_daily.user_id` references `users(id)` **on delete cascade** (with `analytics_case_assignee_daily_user_id_idx`), and `null` means "former user". **No path deletes a user at the base**: users are deactivated (`services/users.ts`) and reactivated by C168.3, and Phase 11 deletes none either. So the bullet's "hook in the user deletion path" has no call site: `forgetAnalyticsAssigneeInTx(tx, { organizationId, userId })` (`services/analytics/assignees.ts`, `api-analytics`) is declared for a deletion path a later phase adds, which calls it first in its transaction to re-key instead of deleting, and the foreign key guarantees meanwhile that an employee's rows never outlive their user. Deactivation keeps the rows (the person may be reactivated); they go by `analytics-assignee-retention-months`.

A4. **`analytics_rollup_state` (not placed by the plan; C163.1, C163.2, C163.4, C164.8 need it):** one row per environment, `environment_id` primary key (cascading), `rolled_up_through` (the watermark, served as `freshness.rolled-up-through`), `outcomes_as_of` (the once-a-day outcome refresh, `freshness.outcomes-as-of`), `time_zone` (the zone the open days were last built in, so a change is seen), `step_data_from` (the local day of the environment's first step record, set once, because a redaction deletes step records and "no step data" must not move later), `updated_at`. All nullable until the first pass. `api-analytics` writes it.

A5. **B2's indexes:** `inquiries_environment_id_updated_at_idx`, `verifications_environment_id_updated_at_idx` and `verification_checks_environment_id_updated_at_idx` on `(environment_id, updated_at)`; `cases` keeps `cases_environment_id_updated_at_id_idx`. They are plain `CREATE INDEX` inside the migration's transaction, as every earlier index was.

A6. **The repository** `packages/db/src/repositories/analytics.ts` (`api-analytics`') is exported from the repository index with `analyticsRollupTableOf(name)` and `NewAnalyticsRollupRow<Name>`, so the owner adds its SQL there without editing a shared file.

A7. **The redaction hook site (C163.4; H04).** `recordRedactedContributionInTx(tx, { environmentId, inquiryId, at })` is called in `createRedactionService`'s `redactInquiry` transaction (`services/redaction.ts`), after `lockInquiryTransactions` and `flowActionCopyTargets` and before `redactInquiryRecords`, so it reads the inquiry, its step record, verifications, checks and signals as they were. Every inquiry redaction goes through it: on demand, through an account's redaction and through the C16 pass. It runs on a re-run too, and the owner adds nothing for an inquiry whose `redacted_at` is set; a throw rolls the whole redaction back (tested). The stub resolves.

A8. **The retention hook (C163.6, C16; not placed by the plan).** `runAnalyticsRetention({ db }, { now, limit })` (`services/analytics/retention.ts`) runs in `runRetention` after the case export retention, and `RetentionReport` extends its `AnalyticsRetentionReport { deletedAnalyticsRollupRows, deletedAnalyticsAssigneeRows }` (rollups by `analytics-retention-months`, the assignee rollup by `analytics-assignee-retention-months`). The stub reports zeros, which is true: no rollup row exists before `api-analytics` builds the refresh. Existing tests changed by the plan: `services/retention.test.ts` (`EMPTY`) and `retention-run.test.ts` (the JSON report).

A9. **The settings hook (not placed by the plan; C163.2).** "Changing it queues `analytics-rebuild`" needs a call in `services/settings.ts`, a shared file: `onAnalyticsSettingsChangedInTx(tx, environmentId, before, after)` (`services/analytics/settings-hook.ts`) runs in `updateDashboardSettings`' transaction after the graph hook, whenever a key of `RETENTION_KEYS` changes (the analytics settings are among them). The API process holds no rebuild producer: the hook works in the transaction (for example by marking the environment's state), and the `analytics-rollup` pass, which holds the producer, queues the rebuild, so a failed enqueue after the commit cannot lose a rebuild. A throw rolls the change back. The stub resolves.

A10. **Queues and jobs (C174.3) as built** (`jobs/analytics-queues.ts`, frozen): `analytics-rollup` every 300,000 ms through `analyticsRepeatableJobs(deps, { rebuilds } | undefined, runPass?)` in `jobs/repeatable/index.ts`, and the `analytics-rebuild` queue (`ANALYTICS_REBUILD_JOB_OPTIONS`: 3 attempts, exponential from 5 s, removed on completion and failure; no custom job id, because a rebuild reads the zone when it runs and a repeated one is harmless) with `createAnalyticsRebuildQueue`. Both run only with `StartWorkersOptions.analytics: WorkerAnalyticsOptions { rebuilds, processRebuild?, runRollup? }`, which the worker's main passes with its producer; existing worker tests that list jobs are unchanged. The rollup stub is not `unbuiltRepeatableJob`, because its pass takes the rebuild producer: `runAnalyticsRollup` logs "analytics rollup not built yet" and fails with `PermanentJobError('analytics_rollup_not_built')`; `processAnalyticsRebuild` fails with `analytics_rebuild_not_built`, which `analyticsRebuildProcessor` turns into `UnrecoverableError`.

A11. **The routes (C164.8) frozen** in `routes/server/analytics.ts` (`analyticsRoutes`, registered last in `routes/server/index.ts`): `GET /api/v1/analytics/{inquiries,funnel,verifications,checks,cases,signals}` under `analytics:read`, with Stage 1a's query and document schemas, problems 400, 401, 403, 429 and 501. Each handler counts the `analytics` policy for the principal first (`enforceRoutePolicy`, `principalSubject`) and then answers 501 until `api-analytics` builds it.

A12. **The `analytics` policy (C174.4):** 60 per 60 s per principal, with its `POLICY_LABELS` entry ("Analytics reads, per user or key") in the same commit. Basis (ours, provisional): a console page reads up to six answers, and a script sweeping filter combinations (C164.7's stated differencing limit) is slowed. Existing tests changed by the plan: contracts' `rate-limits.test.ts` and `plugins/rate-limit.test.ts`.

A13. **Masking, sealing, rewrap, blind index, API logs and redaction coverage: none at (A).** The rollups hold counts, codes and the ids of environments, templates, versions and steps, plus an operator's id in the assignee table; no column is sealed, so none joins `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS`, `SEALED`, `REWRAP_TABLES` or the redaction coverage lists (which are lists of encrypted tables). The analytics routes take no personal-data parameter (dates, ids, vocabularies; `filter[tag]` is an operator label, already served unmasked on inquiries), so no API-log masked member is added. A test holds that no rollup column names an end user (`phase9b-analytics-tables.test.ts`).

A14. **Existing test changed because the plan adds tables:** `packages/db/src/testing.test.ts` (`V2_TABLES` gains the thirteen).

A15. **PGlite (Risks):** before, cold 4,868 to 5,629 ms and warm about 1,130 ms at a load of 0.8 to 1.6; after, at a load of 10 to 13, cold 6,882 to 7,232 and warm 1,625 to 1,808 ms. An A/B in one process at one load (8.7 to 9.7), migrating up to 0030 against through 0031, measured 5,010 to 7,343 against 5,767 to 6,139 ms: within the noise, and the 120 s hook budget untouched.

A16. **The API unit gate at (A) needed 12 shards:** at a load of 24 to 31 (every Stage 3 builder's suites at once), a 6-shard run's third shard reached the 10-minute cap, so the suite ran as 12 blob shards with `--maxWorkers=3` (237 to 457 s each) and one merge (8,725 tests, 94.34% statements). The later calls should start at 12 while the Stage 3 builders run.

### (U)

Checkpoint (U) at `360dd3eb`, on (A)'s `3ea88b84`.

U1. **Migration `0031_phase9b` extended for access (C167.2, C168.1, C168.2, C168.6)**, by the amendment-17 recipe with no hand edits: `user_roles.environment_ids text[]` (existing rows `NULL`, every current and future environment), held by `user_roles_environment_ids_valid` (`NULL`, or 1 to 20 `env_` ids, `MAX_ROLE_ASSIGNMENT_ENVIRONMENTS`, with no `NULL` element; no foreign key, since an array cannot have one, so `api-access` checks each id is an environment of the organisation); `user_sessions.mfa_verified_at timestamptz` (`NULL` until a factor is checked); `organizations.require_mfa` default `true` (`SET DEFAULT`; existing rows keep their value); and `audit_log_ip_index (audit_log_id bigint primary key, environment_id, ip_hmac)` with `audit_log_ip_index_audit_log_fk` → `audit_logs(id)` on delete cascade, `environment_id` cascading with its environment, `audit_log_ip_index_ip_hmac_hex` and `audit_log_ip_index_lookup_idx (environment_id, ip_hmac, audit_log_id)`. Decision the plan left open: no composite foreign key holds an index row to its entry's environment, because it would need a new unique index on `audit_logs (environment_id, id)`, a write cost on the busiest append-only table; `api-access` writes both columns from the entry. No `updated_at`: the rebuild rewrites `ip_hmac`, and nothing reads when.

U2. **The audit IP label is a blind-index kind:** `IDENTIFIER_KEY_LABELS['audit-ip']` is `'openverify/audit-ip/v1'`, equal to contracts' `AUDIT_IP_INDEX_LABEL` (an API test holds them equal, since `packages/db` does not depend on contracts), so the HMAC is `identifierKey(key, { environmentId, kind: 'audit-ip', input })` under the environment's subkey.

U3. **The audit log stays append-only (C168.6; review P23):** a db test updates each kind of column of an entry that has an index row outside a rewrap (refused, "audit_logs is append-only"), rewraps its address (allowed) and changes its action inside the rewrap (refused), and deletes the entry (its index row goes by the cascade); the migration test shows an entry written before 0031 refused the same way on the upgraded database.

U4. **`permissionsForEnvironment` (review F29), as placed:** `findUserSessionContext` (`operator-auth/session-context.ts`) reads `listUserRoleAssignments` (new in `repositories/roles.ts`, `api-access`') and calls `permissionsForEnvironment(assignments, session.environmentId)` (`services/access/permissions.ts`) where `permissionsForRoles(roles)` was. The stub fails closed: only organisation-wide assignments count, which is every assignment until `api-access` builds `PUT /dashboard/users/:id/roles`, so nothing changes for stored data and a narrower assignment never grants more than it should (tested).

U5. **The other hook sites that compute permissions per environment (not placed by the plan):**
- **API-key minting:** `ensureCanGrant` (`services/api-keys.ts`, create and clone; (W)'s rotate copies through the clone's checks) compares the scopes with `mintablePermissions(principal)`. Stub: the principal's permissions, which for a user are the session environment's (the key's) through U4.
- **A promote's target:** `promoteWorkflow` (`workflows/publish/promote.ts`) now needs the rights in the target too, through `userPermissionsInEnvironment(db, user, targetEnvironmentId)`. Stub: the signed-in principal's permissions (the same in every environment while every assignment is organisation-wide). It takes the principal, not ids, because the existing promote tests act as a principal with no user row.
- **The inquiry template promote** (`promoteTemplate`, `services/templates/operations.ts:778`) has the same check, but the file is `api-templates-carry`'s while it runs, so the call is left to `integration-9b` (contractDeviation): `const targetPermissions = await userPermissionsInEnvironment(deps.db, principal, targetEnvironmentId);` after the `principal.kind !== 'user'` guard, and `required.some((permission) => !principal.permissions.has(permission) || !targetPermissions.has(permission))` at `:778`.
- **The organisation settings (V14):** `updateDashboardSettings` (`services/settings.ts`) calls `assertSettingsLevelAllowed(db, { organizationId, environmentId, actor, keys })` (`services/access/settings-level.ts`) first, with the keys the change sets, so a refusal writes nothing (tested). Stub: accepts, which is the rule while every assignment is organisation-wide; `api-access` builds it with `PUT /dashboard/users/:id/roles`.
- **Phase 8's role resolutions in SQL** (`visible-to-roles`, and who holds `cases:read` or `cases:write`): `packages/db` cannot import the API's `services/access/`, so the hook is a db SQL fragment in `api-access`' roles repository, `roleAssignmentReaches(<the user_roles alias>, <the environment>)`, added at all seven sites: `templateVisibleTo` (`repositories/cases.ts`), `holdsPermission` (which now takes the environment) and `seesTemplateOf` (`repositories/case-queues.ts`), and `services/cases/writes.ts`, `services/cases/bulk/apply.ts`, `services/cases/templates.ts` (`unreachableRecipients`) and `jobs/cases/notification-emails.ts`. Stub: `ur.environment_ids is null`; built: `is null or <environment> = any(environment_ids)`. A source test counts one call per `user_roles ur` read in each file; a behaviour test reads `readCaseAssigneeFacts`.
- **`api-access`' own files** compute permissions too and are left to it: `services/users.ts` (`updateOperator`'s and `inviteUser`'s `missingPermissions`, and the environment switch's `environments:production` at `:517`), `services/roles.ts` (`assertCanGrant`, `assertCanChangeAccess`), `routes/dashboard/sessions.ts` (`/me`'s roles), `operator-auth/operator-auth.ts` (sign-in's environment) and `countActiveUsersWithRole` (the last admin).

U6. **The C16 retention hook for `audit-log-retention-days` (not placed by the plan):** `runRetention` calls `runAuditLogRetention({ db }, { now, limit })` (`services/access/audit-retention.ts`) after the analytics step, and `RetentionReport` extends `AuditLogRetentionReport { deletedAuditLogs }`. A deleted entry takes its `audit_log_ip_index` row by the cascade (U1). Stub: zero (it deletes nothing). Existing tests changed by the plan: `services/retention.test.ts` (`EMPTY`) and `retention-run.test.ts` (the JSON report).

U7. **`blind-index:rebuild`'s entry (C168.6, C174.5):** `rebuildAuditLogIpIndex(db, key, { batchSize })` (`services/access/audit-ip-index.ts`) runs after the case field keys and before the graph rows; `BlindIndexRebuildResult.auditLogAddresses`; the line `blind-index:rebuild: done: scanned <n> audit log addresses, re-indexed <n>.`. Built, it backfills and rewrites the index from each entry's encrypted address, never updating `audit_logs`. Stub: zeros (nothing writes or reads the index before `api-access` builds `filter[ip]`). Existing test changed: `blind-index-rebuild.test.ts`'s devices line moves from `at(-6)` to `at(-7)`; (X) moves it again if it adds a step.

U8. **The routes of C167.3 and C168, frozen at 501** (schemas from Stage 1a; `api-access` builds them). Decisions the plan left open, recorded:
- `PUT /dashboard/users/:id/roles` (`users:write`) answers 200 `user-role-assignments`; problems 400, 401, 403, 404, 409 (the last admin), 422 (`role_scope_invalid`), 501.
- `POST /dashboard/users/:id/reactivate` and `.../reset-mfa` (`users:write`, empty body) answer 200 with the user (`userDocumentSchema`): the reactivated user, the reset user with no factor. The reactivation also lists 502 and 503 (it mails a new invitation).
- `GET /dashboard/roles/grantable` (`roles:read`) answers 200 `grantable`; registered before `/roles/:id`.
- `POST /dashboard/invitations/:id/resend` (`users:write`, empty body) answers **201** `dashboardInvitationResponseSchema`, as an invitation does (a new token), with 429. It lives in a new plugin `invitationResendRoutes` (`routes/dashboard/invitations.ts`), registered in `admin.ts`'s authenticated block, because the acceptance in the same file takes no session.
- `GET /dashboard/sessions` (200 `userSessionListDocumentSchema`, no query: the caller's live sessions are few, so the list is not paged), `POST /dashboard/sessions/revoke-others` (`{}` → 200 `{ meta: { revoked } }`), `POST /dashboard/sessions/:id/revoke` (`{}` → **204**, as `DELETE /sessions/current`), `POST /dashboard/sessions/current/step-up` (200 `stepUpResponseSchema`, 429) and `.../step-up/webauthn/options` (`{}` → 200 `dashboardWebauthnLoginOptionsResponseSchema`), in `sessionRoutes`: a session that satisfied MFA, no permission.
Each is listed in the OpenAPI document (ten operations; sha256 `7fb7509c...a44c`).

U9. **The audit explorer's filters fail closed until built (C168.6; as B16 for the list filters):** `listUserAuditLogsQuerySchema` has accepted `filter[action-prefix]`, `filter[from]`, `filter[to]` and `filter[ip]` since Stage 1a, and the service ignored them: the RED run showed `filter[ip]=192.0.2.1` answering every entry. `GET /dashboard/audit-logs` now answers 501 for any of them (`UNBUILT_AUDIT_FILTERS`) until `api-access` builds them, and lists 501 among its problems.

U10. **The `invitations-resend` policy (C174.4; review P35):** 5 per 3,600 s, counted per invitation (the invited user's `usr_`, so a caller cannot reset it by acting as another operator), before anything else; label "Invitation resends, per invitation" in `POLICY_LABELS`. The dashboard admin routes had no rate limiter: `DashboardAdminOptions.api` now picks `rateLimiter` from `ApiDependencies` (the container already passes `deps`) into `AdminRouteContext.rateLimiter`; without one (Phase 1 tests) the policy is not counted, as C12 fails open. Existing tests changed by the plan: contracts' `rate-limits.test.ts` and `plugins/rate-limit.test.ts`.

U11. **The require-mfa default and who relied on the old one (C168.2; Risks):** `createOrganization` takes `requireMfa?` (left out: the column's `true`); `seed:dev` (`seed/dev-seed.ts`, a borrowed edit the task assigns to this checkpoint), `createTestTenant` (`TestTenantOptions.requireMfa`, default `false`), the dashboard test harness and `operators.integration.test.ts` pass `false`. The graph and face-scan benchmarks keep the default (they sign nobody in). The e2e stack runs `seed:dev`, so it is unchanged. `api-access` owns the creation paths from here and adds the seed:dev output line that says so. **Process note:** the db GREEN (`1ba02092`) reached the shared tree about 12 minutes before the harness fix (`63c2df42`); a dashboard-harness test run in that window answered 401 `mfa-required`.

U12. **`mfa_verified_at` and the principal:** the column is on the session row, which `UserSessionContext.session` already carries; no member was added to `UserPrincipal`. `api-access` sets it on every factor check (the MFA sign-in, the step-up, enrolment) and reads it for the step-up rule.

U13. **What `api-access` replaces when it builds:** in `phase9b-access-plumbing.test.ts`, "keeps an organisation-wide assignment as before, and grants nothing through a narrower one until built", "count a role through an organisation-wide assignment, and not through a narrower one until built" and "the stubs change nothing until api-access builds them"; in `phase9b-access-routes.test.ts`, the 501 tests and the audit filters' 501; in `packages/db/src/phase9b-access-tables.test.ts`, "reaches an environment through the stub only organisation-wide".

U14. **User deletion:** as A3, no path deletes a user; nothing to hook at (U).

U15. **Sealing, rewrap, masking, API logs, redaction coverage: none at (U).** `audit_log_ip_index` holds an HMAC, not ciphertext, so it joins no `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS`, `SEALED` or `REWRAP_TABLES` entry; `blind-index:rebuild` covers it (U7) and it goes with its entry (U1, U6). The new routes are dashboard routes, which the API logs never record (they have no server-API principal), and none carries personal data in its body (the step-up's code is a credential that is never stored or echoed).

U16. **PGlite (Risks):** before, cold 5,035 to 5,126 ms and warm 1,193 to 1,440 ms at a load of 0.5 to 0.8; after, cold 5,085 to 5,094 ms and warm 1,190 to 1,252 ms at a load of 1.9 to 2.1. Within the noise; the 120 s hook budget is untouched.

U17. **The API unit gate at (U)** ran as 12 blob shards with `--maxWorkers=3`, three at a time in heavy slots (217 to 292 s each, load 4.4 to 16.8), merged at 642 files, 8,795 tests, 93.89% statements. The forced API lint first failed on four errors in `api-analytics`' uncommitted files and passed when re-run after they committed.

### (W)

Checkpoint (W) at `9f0d3317`, on (U)'s `f36560ca`.

W1. **Migration `0031_phase9b` extended for keys, webhooks and notifications (C169.1, C169.3, C169.5-C169.7)**, by the amendment-17 recipe with no hand edits; the regenerated file keeps every (A) and (U) statement (compared statement by statement against a copy) and adds 34.

W2. **The key columns (C169.1, C169.3):** `api_keys.note text` (`NULL` for none; `api_keys_note_length`, at most 255; the control-character rule stays the contract's, `apiKeyNoteSchema`) and `api_keys.attribute_blocklist text[] not null default '{}'` (`api_keys_attribute_blocklist_size`, at most 100, as the webhook's; every existing key `{}`). The key repository takes both: `NewApiKeyInput.note`, `NewApiKeyInput.attributeBlocklist`, `ApiKeyChanges.note` (`null` removes it) and `ApiKeyChanges.attributeBlocklist` (replaces it).

W3. **The webhook columns (C169.5, C169.6).** `webhooks.name` (`webhooks_name_length`, 1 to 200) and `webhooks.description` (`webhooks_description_length`, at most 1,000), both nullable. Decision the plan left open: the failure alert is **three typed columns**, not one JSON column, so the database holds its bounds and the sweep reads a boolean: `failure_alert_enabled boolean`, `failure_alert_threshold integer` (`webhooks_failure_alert_threshold_range`, 1 to 10,000, B30's ceiling) and `failure_alert_window_minutes integer` (`webhooks_failure_alert_window_range`, 5 to 1,440), all set or none (`webhooks_failure_alert_whole`), with the partial index `webhooks_failure_alert_enabled_idx (environment_id, id) where failure_alert_enabled` for the sweep. **Not placed by the plan:** "at most once per window" (C169.6) needs the last alert's time, so `webhooks.failure_alert_notified_at timestamptz` holds it; the sweep sets it keeping `updated_at` as it was (an alert is not a change of the webhook). The webhook repository selects all four and takes `name`, `description` and `failureAlert: WebhookFailureAlertSetting { enabled, threshold, windowMinutes } | null` on create and change (`null` clears the three); `webhookFailureAlertOf(webhook)` reads the setting back, `null` for none.

W4. **The polymorphic notification subject (C169.7, review F18; B31).** `user_notifications.case_id` is nullable, and `webhook_id text` sits beside it with its own composite foreign key `user_notifications_webhook_fk (environment_id, webhook_id)` → `webhooks (environment_id, id)` on delete cascade, and `user_notifications_webhook_idx (environment_id, webhook_id) where webhook_id is not null`. Decision the plan left open: `subject_type` and `subject_id` are **stored generated columns** (`case when webhook_id is null then 'case' else 'webhook' end`, `coalesce(case_id, webhook_id)`, both NOT NULL), so they can never disagree with the foreign keys that hold the subject, and Phase 8's rows read `case` and their case id with no backfill. `user_notifications_kind_valid` is recreated over `USER_NOTIFICATION_KINDS` (a text CHECK, dropped and added in the same migration; no enum), and one CHECK per kind, `user_notifications_<kind>_subject` (`assigned`, `sla_breached`, `mentioned`, `webhook_failure`), generated from `NOTIFICATION_KIND_SUBJECT`, holds a case kind to its case and `webhook-failure` to its webhook. A row naming no subject is refused by `subject_id`'s NOT NULL before the per-kind CHECK. Phase 8's `createUserNotifications` stays the case kinds'; `createWebhookFailureNotifications(tx, environmentId, rows[{ id, userId, webhookId }])` writes `webhook-failure` rows. The migration test keeps an earlier case notification, reads it as subject `case`, takes a webhook failure about an earlier webhook and refuses a case kind naming a webhook. Existing test changed by the plan: `phase8-case-tables.test.ts` (the column list).

W5. **`notification_preferences` (C169.7; B32)**: `(user_id, kind)` primary key (`notification_preferences_pkey`), `console`, `email`, `updated_at`, `kind` held by `notification_preferences_kind_valid` over `USER_NOTIFICATION_KINDS`, deleted with the user. Decision the plan left open: preferences are **per user, the same in every environment of its organisation**, since the resource's id is the user's; a kind with no row has `NOTIFICATION_PREFERENCE_DEFAULT`. No trigger: the repository sets `updated_at` on every upsert. `listNotificationPreferences(db, userId)` (in `USER_NOTIFICATION_KINDS` order) and `setNotificationPreferences(tx, userId, changes, at)` (sets the kinds named, keeps the others).

W6. **`webhook_alert_emails`, the alert's email outbox (not placed by the plan; outcome 21, "C169.6's failure-alert email follows the same rule")**, as Phase 8's `case_notification_emails`: `id bigint generated always as identity`, `environment_id`, `user_id` (users, cascade), `webhook_id` (`webhook_alert_emails_webhook_fk` → `webhooks (environment_id, id)`, cascade), `attempts` 0 to 3 with `claimed_at` (`webhook_alert_emails_attempts_range`), `created_at`; ids only, the address read at send. The claim and delete are `api-keys-webhooks`' to add in `repositories/webhook-alerts.ts`.

W7. **Who owns the new database code.** The plan's bullet gives `api-keys-webhooks` no repository, so (W) hands it `packages/db/src/repositories/notification-preferences.ts` and `packages/db/src/repositories/webhook-alerts.ts` (both exported from the repository index). The members (W) added to `repositories/api-keys.ts` and `repositories/webhooks.ts` are plain pass-throughs; `api-keys-webhooks` still needs `apps/api/src/services/webhooks.ts` (shared since Phase 1) for the webhook members, which its bullet does not list: `integration-9b` records it as a borrowed edit for these changes, or the orchestrator extends the list.

W8. **The key attribute blocklist hook (C169.3; B3).** A second `onSend` hook in `plugins/api-version.ts` calls `applyKeyAttributeBlocklist(payload, { principal, blocklist, contentType })` (`services/api-keys/blocklist.ts`) for an API-key principal whose blocklist is not empty, on every server-API answer, problems included, and sends what it answers; idempotency and the API logs, registered after it, replay and store that (a test reads the stored log). Not placed by the plan: the blocklist reaches the hook on the principal, read from the key row at authentication (`ApiKeyRecord.attributeBlocklist`, filled by `auth/api-key-lookup.ts` only for a key that has one; `Principal['api-key'].attributeBlocklist`, set by `plugins/principal-auth.ts` when not empty), so no request reads the key twice and a `PATCH` takes effect on the next request (the version lookup's cache is not used for it). The stub answers the payload unchanged, which is right while the routes refuse a blocklist (W9). Exits that are not an HTTP answer (an export's file, a bulk job's results) are the owner's to strip in those features (C169.3's list).

W9. **The new key and webhook members fail closed until built (as B16 for the list filters).** Stage 1a's bodies accept `note`, `attribute-blocklist`, `name`, `description` and `failure-alert`, and today's services ignored them, so a key created with a blocklist would have been served what it blocks and an alert set would never fire. `POST /api-keys` and `PATCH /api-keys/:id` now answer 501 for `note` or `attribute-blocklist` (`UNBUILT_API_KEY_MEMBERS`), and `POST /webhooks` and `PATCH /webhooks/:id` for `name`, `description` or `failure-alert` (`UNBUILT_WEBHOOK_MEMBERS`), each listing 501; `api-keys-webhooks` removes the guards and replaces their tests when it builds them.

W10. **The rotate route (C169.1; B29), frozen:** `POST /api-keys/:id/rotate` (`api-keys:write`; `rotateApiKeyBodySchema` → 201 `rotatedApiKeyDocumentSchema` with `Location` and `Cache-Control: no-store`; 400, 401, 403, 404, 501) on the stub `rotateApiKey(deps, ctx, principal, id, meta, issue)` (`services/api-keys/rotate.ts`). Existing test changed by the plan: `routes/server/api-keys.test.ts` (the route list).

W11. **The delivery attempt headers hook (C169.5; B30):** `deliverWebhook` asks `deliveryAttemptHeaders({ attempt, maxAttempts, firstAttemptedAt })` (`webhooks/attempt-headers/headers.ts`; the plan's `webhooks/attempt-headers.ts` placed in the owner's directory) for each attempt and sends its headers after the custom ones and before the dispatcher's own, which win. `maxAttempts` is the schedule's length capped by the row's `max_attempts`; `firstAttemptedAt` is the recorded first attempt, or now for the first. The stub adds none.

W12. **`webhook-failure-alerts` (C169.6, C174.3):** `WEBHOOK_FAILURE_ALERTS_QUEUE` and `WEBHOOK_FAILURE_ALERTS_INTERVAL_MS` (60,000) in the frozen `jobs/webhook-alert-queues.ts`; `webhookAlertRepeatableJobs(deps, { mailer, dashboardPublicUrl } | undefined, runPass)` in `jobs/repeatable/index.ts`, scheduled only with `StartWorkersOptions.webhookAlerts: WorkerWebhookAlertOptions { mailer?, dashboardPublicUrl?, runAlerts? }`, so the existing worker tests are unchanged. The worker's main passes it, with one `workerMailer` that the case emails now share. The pass is `runWebhookFailureAlerts(deps: WebhookFailureAlertDeps { db, clock, logger, mailer?, dashboardPublicUrl? }): Promise<WebhookFailureAlertResult { alerted, notifications, emailsQueued, emailsSent }>` (`jobs/repeatable/webhook-alerts.ts`); the stub logs "webhook failure alerts not built yet" and fails with `PermanentJobError('webhook_failure_alerts_not_built')`, so the compose and e2e worker log it every minute until it is built (as (A)'s rollup).

W13. **Each notification read under its kind's rule (outcome 20; B31).** `services/cases/notifications.ts` reads a case kind with `cases:read` and its case's visibility, as Phase 8 did, and hands the page's `webhook-failure` rows to `readableWebhookNotifications(db, user, rows)` (`webhooks/alerts/notifications.ts`), keeping, in their order, the rows each rule keeps; the stub keeps none (fail closed). The routes (`routes/dashboard/notifications.ts`) no longer require `cases:read` in their config: the service refuses a session holding neither `cases:read` nor `webhooks:read` (403, as before for a session without `cases:read`). Behaviour changed by the plan: a user holding `webhooks:read` and not `cases:read` now gets 200 with no case rows. The resource serves `subject-type` and the subject's relationship from the generated columns.

W14. **The notification preference routes (C169.7; B32), frozen; not placed by the plan** (their registration is in `routes/dashboard/index.ts`, a shared file): `GET` (200 `notificationPreferencesDocumentSchema`; 401, 501) and `PUT /dashboard/notification-preferences` (`updateNotificationPreferencesBodySchema` → 200; 400, 401, 501) in `routes/dashboard/notification-preferences.ts` (`notificationPreferenceRoutes`, `api-keys-webhooks`'), a session and no permission, on the stubs `getNotificationPreferences(db, user)` and `updateNotificationPreferences(db, user, body)` (`webhooks/alerts/preferences.ts`).

W15. **The sender name (C169.8; B33).** `MailMessage.senderName?: string | null` and `createMailer` sending `from: formatSender(message.senderName ?? null, from)` (`mail/sender-name.ts`, B33's names and path; the orchestrator's prompt named `mailSenderDisplayName` in `webhooks/alerts/sender-name.ts`, and the Stage 1a names win). The stub keeps the install-wide From. Not wired at (W): the sends that must pass the environment's `mail-sender-name` are `workflows/actions/messaging/messenger.ts`, `jobs/cases/notification-emails.ts`, `services/otp/send.ts`, `services/handoff/flow-handoffs.ts`, the alert emails and, for an organisation-level message, `services/users.ts`' invitations (no environment: the owner's decision); each is another owner's path.

W16. **Sealing, rewrap, blind index, API-log masking, redaction coverage and retention: none added at (W).** The new columns hold an operator's note, attribute names, a webhook's name and description, alert bounds, booleans and ids: no end user's data, nothing sealed, so nothing joins `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS`, `SEALED`, `REWRAP_TABLES`, `blind-index:rebuild` or the redaction coverage. `note` was already an API-log masked member and `token` (the rotation's `meta.token`) a redacted key. C16 retention: `deleteExpiredUserNotifications` is kind-agnostic, so `webhook-failure` rows go after 90 days; preferences go with their user; outbox rows go when the sweep sends, skips or exhausts them.

W17. **PGlite (Risks):** before, at `55bf5879`, cold 5,534 to 5,959 ms and warm 1,205 to 1,437 ms at a load of 2.2 to 3.3; after, at `3e4f7988`, cold 5,490 to 6,161 ms and warm 1,347 to 1,524 ms at a load of 2.8 to 3.9. Within the noise; the 120 s hook budget is untouched.

W18. **The gate at (W)** (`docs/tdd/phase9-foundation-9b.tdd.md`, "Gates of checkpoint (W)"): the API unit suite ran as 12 blob shards with `--maxWorkers=3` in the reserved slot, one after another (138 to 165 s each, load 1.9 to 11.8). Three gates failed only on `api-access`' own paths, each re-run after waiting: the forced API lint (two errors in `routes/dashboard/role-assignments.test.ts` and `services/users.ts`; passed on the re-run), the db integration suite (`case-pull.integration.test.ts`'s two R008 read budgets, which read 100 and 300 rows since `roleAssignmentReaches` reads `environment_ids` in `seesTemplateOf`'s subquery, `36d9a833`) and the API unit merge (`services/users.test.ts` "keeps the SMTP failure kind on the 502", refused 403 by the new grant check because its inviter holds no stored role). The last two still failed after 30 to 45 minutes and are reported as failed by those tests and their owner, never as passed; `integration-9b` (or `api-access`) fixes them test-first: the R008 budgets need the predicate in a form the plan can hoist (or a plan amendment), and the invite test an inviter that holds its roles.

### (X)

Checkpoint (X) at `db00d996`, on (W)'s `b0b5a2a3`.

X1. **Migration `0031_phase9b` extended for exports, saved views, bulk reports and the transaction filters (C165, C166, C174.5)**, by the amendment-17 recipe with no hand edits; the regenerated file keeps every (A), (U) and (W) statement (compared statement by statement against a copy) and adds 26. Its first statement is `ALTER TYPE "public"."report_origin" ADD VALUE 'bulk'`, as drizzle-kit generates it, and `'bulk'` appears nowhere else in it (Phase 8 Stage 1a amendment 2; held by `migration-0031.test.ts`). No table of (X) has an `updated_at` trigger: the owner's repositories set it, as (W)'s `notification_preferences`, so 0031 stays free of hand edits.

X2. **`exports` (C166.1, C166.6; B19) as built**, Phase 8's `case_exports` (C138.1) as the pattern: the creator (`user` or `api-key`; a workflow run never exports), `object_type`, `format`, `include_personal_data`, `status`, the request sealed for the row (`SEALED.exportRequest`), `filter_keys` (the shape the audit and the resource record, at most 100), `saved_view_id` (an id only, no foreign key: a view may later be deleted and the export still names it), `row_count` (0 to `EXPORT_LIMITS.rows`), `byte_size`, `error_code`, the file's `storage_key` and `wrapped_dek`, `cut_at`, `completed_at`, `expires_at`. Decisions the plan left open: the request is kept exactly while the export is `pending` or `running` (stricter than `case_exports`, which only requires it until then: the filter's values must not outlive the cut, review P19); C166.6's resource invariants are CHECKs too (`exports_finished_with_times`, `exports_file_while_completed`, `exports_size_while_completed`: only a file still kept has a size, `exports_error_when_failed`), so no row the resource could not serve can be written. Indexes for the creator's list, the sweep's expiry and the job's unfinished rows.

X3. **`saved_views` (C165.1, C165.2) as built**: owner (`users`, on delete cascade), `list` (`SAVED_VIEW_LISTS`), `name` (1 to 100), `filters` jsonb (an object), `columns` (at most 50), `sort` (at most 65), `shared`. The filters are plaintext because the API refuses every personal-data filter key before storing a view (C165.2, `saved_view_pii_filter`); a view never holds a person's data. Indexes for the owner's views by list, the environment's shared views by list, and the owner (the cascade).

X4. **`report_bulk_jobs` and `report_bulk_job_rows` (C166.5, C166.6) as built.** The job: creator (`user` or `api-key`), the report template (a composite foreign key to `report_templates (environment_id, id)`), `status`, `row_count` (0 to 1,000), a whole-file `error_code` (only on `failed`), the CSV's `storage_key` and `wrapped_dek` (both or neither), `completed_at` (exactly when `completed` or `failed`). **Not placed by the plan:** `report_bulk_job_rows (environment_id, job_id, row_number, outcome, report_id, error_code)`, one row per CSV row (primary key on the three ids), created naming its report or failed with its code (CHECKs), deleted with its job, so the owner writes each row's outcome in the transaction that creates its report and a retried or killed job never creates a row's report twice or skips one (C166.6). `REPORT_BULK_MAX_ROWS` (1,000, `packages/db`) equals contracts' `REPORT_BULK_LIMITS.rows`, held by an API test since `packages/db` does not depend on contracts. The column is `row_number`, served as `row`.

X5. **`report_origin` gains `bulk` (B34).** Core's `REPORT_ORIGINS` ends with `bulk` in the same commit (`packages/db/src/schema.test.ts` holds the enum to it), and contracts' `REPORT_RESOURCE_ORIGINS` is now `REPORT_ORIGINS` itself (the same five, kept by name; `[...REPORT_ORIGINS, 'bulk']` would list `bulk` twice). Existing tests changed by the plan: `packages/db/src/phase6-tables.test.ts` and `phase8-action-tables.test.ts` (the enum's labels read `bulk` last); they failed from `54f50536` until `db00d996`, about an hour.

X6. **The transaction filter projection (C165.3; B18) as built**: `transaction_filter_values (environment_id, transaction_id, field_key, value)`, one plaintext row per value in its canonical text (a choice, `true`/`false`, a number, a date), primary key on all four, a composite foreign key to its transaction (cascade), `transaction_filter_values_lookup_idx (environment_id, field_key, value, transaction_id)`, the field-key form and a value of 1 to 1,024 characters (the filter value's own bound). A filterable `string` field is read through `transaction_field_keys`, so there is no second HMAC table, label or rebuild step (B18). **Borrowed edit (redaction, not placed by the plan):** `redactTransactionRecords` (`packages/db/src/repositories/transaction-redaction.ts`) deletes the transaction's rows but for its kept fields, as it does the field keys (a filterable date can be personal data). **Hook not placed by the plan:** `writeTransactionFilterValuesInTx(tx, { environmentId, transactionId, fieldSchemas, fields, changedFieldKeys })` (`services/list-filters/transaction-values.ts`, `api-exports`) is called by the transaction create (`changedFieldKeys: null`) and update (the changed keys) in `services/transactions/transactions.ts`, beside `replaceTransactionFieldKeys`, with the fields as stored (typed `{ type, value }`); the stub writes nothing, and a transaction written before `api-exports` builds it has no rows (a backfill is the owner's), while its filters answer 501 meanwhile.

X7. **Sealing and rewrap (C174.5):** `ENCRYPTED_COLUMNS` gains `exports.request` (row), `exports.wrapped_dek` (file) and `report_bulk_jobs.wrapped_dek` (file); `SEALED.exportRequest`; `REWRAP_TABLES` gains `exports` and `report_bulk_jobs`; a `kek:rewrap` test re-wraps all three (`phase9b-exports-tables.test.ts`). Existing tests changed by the plan: `packages/db/src/schema-v2.test.ts` (the encrypted-column list), `testing.test.ts` (`V2_TABLES` gains five), `repositories/key-rotation.test.ts` (it seeds a pending and a completed export and a bulk job, so every encrypted column is rewrapped).

X8. **Redaction coverage (C166.1, C166.5; review P24):** `EXPORT_PII_TABLES` (`exports`: a principal's own 24-hour copy, its request sealed until cut and its file purged by `export-sweep` and the C16 pass; no subject's redaction reaches it, so an export cut before a redaction keeps that person's data until it expires, as C166.1 says) and `THIRD_PARTY_REPORT_BULK_PII_TABLES` (`report_bulk_jobs`: third-party personal data, beside 7B's `THIRD_PARTY_LIST_PII_TABLES`), both in `services/redaction.test-support.ts` and the coverage test.
